# IDEA LEDGER

This is the complete catalogue of research-only systematic cryptocurrency edge hypotheses. No empirical validation is performed in this repository.

Stable IDs: CRYPTO-EDGE-001, CRYPTO-EDGE-002 (rejected), CRYPTO-EDGE-003, CRYPTO-EDGE-004 (reinstated in the ninth cycle, active, rank 3).

Scoring convention used in this file: for each rubric dimension, contribution = (score / 5) x dimension weight (weights in percentage points), so the seven contributions sum to a total out of 100. Cycle 1 records applied this formula incorrectly to the two 10%-weight dimensions (falsifiability and originality were each entered at half their proper value); all totals below use the stated formula. See PROCESS_CHANGELOG.md (2026-10-20, scoring-formula correction). **Seventh-cycle correction (2026-10-20):** CRYPTO-EDGE-001's recorded total was 63, but its own v1.2 dimension contributions sum to 67 (16+12+6+15+6+8+4); the pre-cycle-6 total of 73 less the two documented v1.2 downgrades is also 67, so the 63 is an arithmetic error that has been propagated into the shortlist and the ranking table. The error did not change the rank order, because CRYPTO-EDGE-001 was already first. All totals in this file were re-derived from the dimension contributions in the seventh cycle; the other three entries were already correct. See PROCESS_CHANGELOG.md (2026-10-20, seventh-cycle arithmetic correction and total re-derivation rule). **Ninth cycle: all four totals were re-derived again from the seven dimension contributions and all four are correct (001 = 16+6+3+15+6+8+4 = 58; 002 = 12+3+3+15+3+6+4 = 46; 003 = 12+6+6+12+3+8+8 = 55; 004 = 12+6+3+9+3+8+4 = 45), and the rank order is unchanged.** Two entries moved: CRYPTO-EDGE-001 fell from 61 to 58 on evidence quality alone (3/5 to 2/5) on three new independent 2026 sources that bear on the transferability of its dated-futures support, and CRYPTO-EDGE-004 was reinstated from deferred on the removal of the data ground for its deferral, with free-data accessibility 2/5 to 3/5 and net value after frictions 2/5 to 1/5, so its total is unchanged at 45 but its status and its risk profile are not. The margin between rank 1 and rank 2 is now 3 points, down from 6, and that is a signal that the top of the ranking is unsettled rather than that either entry improved. **Tenth cycle: all four totals were re-derived again from the seven dimension contributions. Three are unchanged — CRYPTO-EDGE-001 = 58 (16+6+3+15+6+8+4), CRYPTO-EDGE-002 = 46 (rejected), CRYPTO-EDGE-004 = 45 (12+6+3+9+3+8+4) — and CRYPTO-EDGE-003 falls from 55 to 52 (12+3+6+12+3+8+8) on evidence quality alone (2/5 to 1/5).** The tenth cycle is a **specification cycle**: the non-derivatives coverage gap is closed, the ninth cycle's archive construction was adversarially re-verified and its *description* corrected, and the two entries that are testable on free data now carry complete, preregisterable validation designs. Three preregistered results in the same funding family now document that CRYPTO-EDGE-003's registered threshold construction is the weaker of two forms (selection loses monotonically to harvesting), that the harvest alternative its slow clock converges on earns approximately nothing in the current regime (five independent confirmations, now including a point-in-time restatement), and that the entry's own predictive mapping has never been tested at all; with no supporting evidence and three independent adverse findings, evidence quality falls to 1/5. Rank order is unchanged (001 > 003 > 004), but the margin between rank 1 and rank 2 widens from 3 points to 6, and that widening is a consequence of CRYPTO-EDGE-003 being downgraded on adverse evidence, **not** of CRYPTO-EDGE-001 improving — its total, its confidence and its evidence are exactly where the ninth cycle left them.

**Eleventh research cycle (2026-10-20) — all four totals re-derived and unchanged: CRYPTO-EDGE-001 = 58, CRYPTO-EDGE-002 = 46 (rejected), CRYPTO-EDGE-003 = 52, CRYPTO-EDGE-004 = 45.** Every total was re-derived from its seven dimension contributions (score / 5 x weight, summing to 100) and checked against the RANKED_SHORTLIST.md table before publication. **No dimension moved in the eleventh cycle.** The cycle's output is instead (i) **four documented corrections to the catalogue's own recorded facts** — the archive's listing-pagination rule, the exhaustiveness of the archive's update log, the terminal-state taxonomy, and the units of the open-interest-clearing proxy — each of which is a *silent-failure* defect rather than a loud error, and each of which is marked superseded in place rather than deleted; and (ii) **two access determinations that change what a validator can actually do** — the venue announcement feed is confirmed free, dated and machine-readable, and free liquidation-notional datasets exist outside the Binance archive. Versions: CRYPTO-EDGE-001 → v1.6, CRYPTO-EDGE-003 → v1.4, CRYPTO-EDGE-004 → v1.4. No hypothesis was added; the search that was run and the reason nothing cleared the bar are recorded in RANKED_SHORTLIST.md and in item 17 of each active entry.
**Thirteenth research cycle (2026-10-20) — all four totals re-derived and unchanged: CRYPTO-EDGE-001 = 58, CRYPTO-EDGE-002 = 46 (rejected), CRYPTO-EDGE-003 = 52, CRYPTO-EDGE-004 = 45.** Every total was re-derived from its seven dimension contributions (score / 5 x weight, summing to 100) and checked against the RANKED_SHORTLIST.md table before publication. **No dimension moved.**
The cycle's single largest discovery is a **free, Binance-native, per-contract, per-event, side-attributed forced-liquidation record that no prior cycle found**: `data/futures/cm/daily/liquidationSnapshot/{SYMBOL}/{SYMBOL}-liquidationSnapshot-YYYY-MM-DD.zip` on the **COIN-M (inverse) tree**, 118 symbol directories, **17,182 symbol-days**, window **2023-06-25 to 2024-10-14**, with a per-event timestamp, the side of the forced order, the quantity in contracts, the fill price and a `.CHECKSUM` beside every file. Three construction hazards were found in it *before* any interpretation, all of them silent-failure defects: **every event is written twice** (verified on five sampled days, multiplicity exactly two), the **quantity is in contracts** with a **$100 contract multiplier** (Binance's own contract specification), so notional is `quantity x 100` and *not* `quantity x price` — the naive product overstates 2024-08-05 BTCUSD_PERP forced selling by **528x** ($17.06bn against the correct $32.3m) — and the **`side` field is the order side**, so a SELL row is a liquidated long. The companion **USDⓈ-M (UM)** tree provably has **no** liquidation dataset, established by exhaustive enumeration of every prefix in the bucket, so the catalogue's recorded fact that the archive contains no liquidation dataset is **superseded in place**: it was true of UM and false of CM.
Consequently the registered statistic of CRYPTO-EDGE-001 — sell liquidations as a share of open interest — is **computable on free Binance data in the venue's own contract unit** (both numerator and denominator in contracts, hence price-invariant by construction), and the catalogue's mandated proxy-fidelity step is **executable on Binance-native data with both sides in the same unit**; the first such measurement is recorded under CRYPTO-EDGE-001 below and shows the open-interest-clearing proxy registering a state change **10-14x larger** than the forced flow that accounts for it. The cycle also established that **SaR (arXiv:2603.09164) is computable on free Binance data** through the `bookDepth` tree (UM and CM, 2023-01-01 to 2026-10-19, a ~30-second depth ladder at 1-5% bands carrying depth and USD notional per level), with the single exception of the framework's maker-concentration adjustment, which a bucketed ladder cannot support. **Fourteenth-cycle in-place correction to this paragraph: the "10-14x" proxy-to-flow ratio stated here is a mis-reading of the two percentages it rests on, and the true ratios are 6.9x (BTCUSD_PERP) and 9.9x (ETHUSD_PERP); see item 15(d).**
Versions: CRYPTO-EDGE-001 → v1.7, CRYPTO-EDGE-002 → v1.3, CRYPTO-EDGE-003 → v1.5, CRYPTO-EDGE-004 → v1.5. **No hypothesis was added.** The one promotion candidate the new data made measurable — a liquidity-withdrawal state as the pre-cascade severity channel — was examined and **kept deferred** with a preliminary score of 43/100, because the primary published source states that *none of the scalar pre-state measures it could construct grades severity*; the search that produced that verdict is recorded in RANKED_SHORTLIST.md and REJECTED_AND_DEFERRED.md.
**Note on the record's state:** the twelfth research cycle updated the handoff only and left the five research artefacts at their eleventh-cycle contents, so every artefact in this file was last *changed* in the eleventh cycle; the twelfth cycle's review survives in HANDOFF.md. The thirteenth cycle is the first to write artefacts since the eleventh.

**Fourteenth research cycle (2026-10-20) — all four totals re-derived and unchanged: CRYPTO-EDGE-001 = 58, CRYPTO-EDGE-002 = 46 (rejected), CRYPTO-EDGE-003 = 52, CRYPTO-EDGE-004 = 45.** Every total was re-derived from its seven dimension contributions (score / 5 x weight, summing to 100) and checked against the RANKED_SHORTLIST.md table before publication. **No dimension moved.** The cycle's output is (i) **four documented corrections to the catalogue's own recorded facts**, three of them silent-failure defects in the very record the thirteenth cycle discovered: the **contract multiplier is per symbol, not a constant $100** ($100 for the BTCUSD contracts and $10 for the eight other symbols checked, derived from the venue's own metrics-and-klines pair, so the naive product overstates a non-BTC contract's forced notional by an order of magnitude); the **record's file set is not a daily panel** (9,652 of 26,834 symbol-days absent, **36.0%**, with absence non-random in symbol — 1.3% for BTCUSD_PERP against 47.6% for TRXUSD_PERP — and strongly co-missing in date, 39 of 41 live perps absent on 2023-09-09); the **"10-14x" proxy-to-flow ratio the thirteenth cycle recorded is a mis-statement of two percentages** (the open-interest state change is **6.9x** the forced flow for BTCUSD_PERP and **9.9x** for ETHUSD_PERP, and **0.56x to 34.2x across the 36 contracts that have a file on 2024-08-05**, so the ratio is not stable in either dimension); and the **free depth ladder has twelve percentage levels, not ten** (the ±0.20% bands are present, and they are where a liquidation order executes). And (ii) **three primary venue documents that close the handoff's open question about the record's end**: the publisher **discontinued the liquidation-snapshot product on 2024-10-15** — a staff answer in the publisher's own repository, dated the same day the last file was written, twenty-four hours after the file for the last data day — and the **USDⓈ-M liquidation record that this catalogue recorded as never having existed did exist, for 2023-06-25 to 2024-03-31, and has been removed from the archive**, so the recorded fact is **superseded in place a second time** and the entry's own instrument is *documented* to have carried the statistic for 281 days. Versions: CRYPTO-EDGE-001 → v1.8, CRYPTO-EDGE-002 → v1.4, CRYPTO-EDGE-003 → v1.6, CRYPTO-EDGE-004 → v1.6. **No hypothesis was added**; the one promotion candidate's promotion condition was tested and **failed on a ground that is now dated rather than merely absent**.

**Fifteenth research cycle (2026-10-20) — all four totals re-derived and unchanged: CRYPTO-EDGE-001 = 58, CRYPTO-EDGE-002 = 46 (rejected), CRYPTO-EDGE-003 = 52, CRYPTO-EDGE-004 = 45.** Each total was re-derived from its seven dimension contributions (score / 5 x dimension weight, summing to 100) and checked against the RANKED_SHORTLIST.md table before publication; **no dimension moved**. The cycle's headline is a **decisive data find that closes CRYPTO-EDGE-001's construction question**: the USDⓈ-M per-contract, per-event forced-liquidation record that the fourteenth cycle established had existed and had been withdrawn from the archive **was not destroyed — it was relocated, and it is live in the publisher's own bucket at an undocumented path**, `data3/liquidationSnapshot/`, verified on 2026-10-20 by direct listing and file-level SHA-256 check: **311 symbol directories, 64,820 daily zips (129,640 objects including the `.CHECKSUM` sidecars), coverage 2023-06-25 to 2024-03-31 (281 days)**, the same naming scheme, the same ten-column schema and the same exact-two row duplication as the surviving COIN-M tree, and the same range the venue's own historical-data FAQ documents for the "Liquidation Snapshot" product. The cycle also produced **five corrections to the catalogue's own recorded facts** (the "49 re-uploaded files" figure is really **737** files deviating from the T+1 publication lag, across **21** campaigns; the cross-sectional denominator of "45 perps" is wrong — the live universe on 2024-08-05 was 36, so that cross-section was **complete**, not 80% covered; the "a symbol-level absence may be a zero-event day" reading is **not supported** once the venue's own open-interest series is consulted; the per-symbol multiplier, extended to all 73 delivery contracts, is **10 USD for every non-BTC symbol and 100 USD for every BTC symbol** with no other values anywhere in the tree; and the thirteenth cycle's "10-14x" proxy-to-flow figure is an arithmetic mis-reading, as the fourteenth cycle already recorded), **the first measurement of a cascade's per-event path** (a clean negative result: the ordering of first liquidations is a *rate* ordering and carries no information beyond the daily aggregate), **the decomposition of the open-interest proxy the catalogue has used since the eleventh cycle** (recorded forced liquidations explain only **12.1%** of the cross-sectional open-interest reduction on the cascade day, so that proxy is mostly measuring *voluntary* de-risking, not forced unwinding), and **the first free, dated, point-in-time market-capitalisation panel found for the universe at all** (the Coin Metrics community tier, 135 assets, daily), which supersedes the catalogue's recorded "historical market capitalisation remains unavailable on every free tier" in place. **No hypothesis was added**; the one new family the cycle's data could have generated — forced-unwind intensity as a cross-sectional *ranking* variable — is recorded as a rejected re-spin, and four of the five external questions the cycle inherited are now answered.

## CRYPTO-EDGE-001 — Basis/Carry Reversal (Cash-and-Carry Crash Predictor), v1.9

1. Short name and version: Basis/Carry Reversal v1.4 (v1.2 restructured the prediction from a directional mean-return claim into a crash-conditionality claim; v1.3 corrects the recorded total, mines the published tail results that the v1.2 restructure had overlooked, and adds an instrument-specificity requirement; v1.4 records the ninth cycle's new contrary evidence on the crash mechanism, confirms that the mandatory open-interest baseline and a free proxy for the tail statistic are now obtainable from the Binance public archive, and re-scores evidence quality; v1.5 fixes the tenth cycle's cycle-10 validation specification for this entry — the instrument and asset class, the percentile trigger, the preregistered tail statistic, the free open-interest-clearing proxy for that statistic, the mandatory separation of the tail statistic from the mean statistic, the mandatory controls, and the null outcome — and records the tenth cycle's new sources, none of which moves a dimension score, so the total stays at 58). v1.6 is the eleventh cycle's amendment: it corrects a units defect in the cycle-10 open-interest-clearing proxy (the proxy was specified on the USD-notional open-interest series, which moves with price, so the free contract-count series is now mandated instead), adds the fourth preregistered tail statistic that the newly confirmed free liquidation datasets make computable, adds a mandatory proxy-fidelity validation step against two newly located free forced-liquidation records, and records the eleventh cycle's new sources and the two access determinations that changed what is computable on free data. **No dimension moves and the total stays at 58**, for the reason recorded in item 16. **v1.7 is the thirteenth cycle's amendment**: it supersedes in place the recorded access fact that the archive contains no liquidation dataset; adds the **Binance-native forced-liquidation record on the COIN-M tree** together with the three construction hazards that must be fixed before any use of it (exact row duplication, the contract-multiplier unit, and the order-side semantics of `side`); re-specifies the registered P1 statistic in the venue's own **contract unit**, which makes it price-invariant by construction; records the **first measurement of the mandated proxy-fidelity step on Binance-native data**; and records the thirteenth cycle's sources. **No dimension moves and the total stays at 58**, for the reason recorded in item 16. **v1.8 is the fourteenth cycle's amendment**: it corrects **two of the thirteenth cycle's own recorded facts in place** — the contract multiplier is **per symbol** and not a constant 100 USD (100 USD for the BTCUSD contracts, 10 USD for the eight other symbols checked), and the **"10-14x" proxy-to-flow ratio was a mis-statement of two percentages** (the true ratios are **6.9x** for BTCUSD_PERP and **9.9x** for ETHUSD_PERP, and **0.56x to 34.2x across the 36 contracts that have a file on 2024-08-05**), so the ratio is not stable in either the contract dimension or the day dimension and must be reported per contract; it records the **fourth silent-failure defect in the record — that its file set is not a daily panel** (9,652 of 26,834 symbol-days absent, 36.0%, non-random in symbol and strongly co-missing in date), with the mandatory missingness treatment it implies; it records the **first cross-sectional measurement on the record** (2024-08-05, 36 of 45 COIN-M perps with a file) and what it shows, including that the registered statistic spans two orders of magnitude across contracts on a single day (0.26% of opening open interest for TRXUSD_PERP against 37.94% for ENSUSD_PERP) while the majors sit in the middle (1.96% BTCUSD_PERP, 2.35% ETHUSD_PERP, 1.99% SOLUSD_PERP); it records the **correction to the free depth ladder's specification** (twelve percentage levels including ±0.20%, not ten), which matters because the ±0.20% bands are where a liquidation order executes; it records the **primary venue documents that close the question of why the record ends** (the publisher discontinued the product on 2024-10-15) and that **the USDⓈ-M liquidation record the catalogue recorded as never having existed did exist, for 2023-06-25 to 2024-03-31, and has been removed from the archive** — so the entry's specified instrument is *documented* to have carried the registered statistic for 281 days, and a retrieval question replaces the construction question; and it records the **free revision detector** (the archive's own `LastModified` field) that shows the futures tree is revised outside the published update log, with 49 liquidation files re-uploaded on 2026-04-16 for five previously-absent dates. **No dimension moves and the total stays at 58**, for the reason recorded in item 16. **v1.8 is the fourteenth cycle's amendment**: it records the archive-wide census of the COIN-M liquidation tree (118 symbols, 17,182 files, 2023-06-25 to 2024-10-14), the 36.0% symbol-day missingness with its two candidate readings, the per-symbol contract multiplier, the proxy-to-flow ratio and the 2024-08-05 cross-section, and the external access determinations; and it records the first recorded disagreement on this entry's falsifiability dimension. **v1.9 is the fifteenth cycle's amendment**: it records that the withdrawn USDⓈ-M record **was relocated rather than destroyed and is live, free and checksum-verifiable at `data3/liquidationSnapshot/`** (311 symbols, 64,820 files, 2023-06-25 to 2024-03-31, verified 2026-10-20), which closes the entry's construction question; it records the USDⓈ-M family's **base-asset quantity units and multiplier-free statistic**; it records the corrected revision census (**737 files deviate from the T+1 publication lag across 21 campaigns, of which 49 carry the 2026-04-16 date the eleventh cycle recorded — the catalogue's "49" figure is superseded in place**); it records the decomposition of the open-interest proxy (recorded forced liquidations explain **12.1%** of the cross-sectional open-interest reduction on the cascade day); it records the **negative** result on per-event path information (first-liquidations across all 36 live contracts fall inside a 55.3-minute window and the ordering is a rate ordering, not a propagation order); and it records that the fourteenth cycle's recorded falsifiability disagreement is materially strengthened with one of its two grounds removed. **No dimension moves and the total stays at 58**, for the reason recorded in item 16.
2. One-sentence thesis: Sustained elevated perpetual-futures basis (carry) relative to spot signals crowded leveraged longs and predicts elevated *crash conditionality* - a higher frequency and conditional severity of forward drawdowns, and a rise in sell liquidations as a share of open interest - over the following 1-4 weeks, because constrained arbitrage cannot immediately absorb the positioning imbalance.
3. Status: shortlisted (rank 1). Revised in the ninth research cycle: re-scored from 61 to 58 on evidence quality, confidence held at low. The recorded total was corrected from 63 to the arithmetically correct 67 for the v1.2 dimension scores in the seventh cycle, re-scored to 61 in the same cycle, and is now 58. See items 5, 6, 9, 10, 13, 14, 15, 16 and 17. **Tenth cycle: unchanged at 58/100, confidence low.** The cycle's work on this entry was specification and adversarial verification rather than new evidence on the registered claim. Four new sources bear on the mechanism's premise (that leveraged-long demand persists and that carry was paid historically) and on two competing explanations for crash-state returns; none of them bears on the crash-conditionality prediction itself, so no dimension moves, and the entry's rank-1 position now rests on a 6-point margin created by CRYPTO-EDGE-003's downgrade rather than by any improvement here. **Thirteenth cycle: unchanged at 58/100, confidence low.** The cycle's discovery changes what can be *measured* on the entry's own venue; it is not evidence about the *registered* prediction, so no dimension moves. The entry's rank-1 position still rests on the 6-point margin created by CRYPTO-EDGE-003's tenth-cycle downgrade, not on any improvement here, and the thirteenth cycle adds a second recorded instance of that same trap: a large, genuinely new data fact that bears on measurement only.
4. Proposed edge family and target market/universe: Derivatives pricing/positioning anomaly (cost-of-carry / constrained arbitrage); BTC and ETH USDT-margined perpetuals versus spot on major retail-accessible venues (Binance, Bybit, OKX); cross-venue where public data permits.
5. Mechanism: When spot demand outpaces futures-arbitrage capacity, perp basis widens positive. The published version of this account (Schmeling, Schrimpf & Todorov) attributes crypto carry to two forces: (i) demand from smaller, trend-chasing investors seeking leveraged upside exposure and (ii) limited deployment of arbitrage capital because of regulatory and margin frictions, notably the absence of cross-margining and venue leverage caps (Binance cut maximum leverage to 20x in July 2021). Cash-and-carry arbitrage is constrained by absent cross-margining, funding and custody costs, and short availability, so the positioning imbalance persists; carry traders are themselves exposed to liquidation if prices move against the trade before convergence. Chi (2023) documents the basis as the strongest cross-sectional crypto futures factor, consistent with spot-to-futures information segmentation. Counter-mechanism now recorded explicitly: He, Manela, Ross & von Wachter (2026) report that past returns explain the majority of the futures-spot gap in a time-series regression, which indicates that the basis level is at least partly a momentum / positive-feedback proxy rather than a pure positioning measure; carry and momentum are not separable in the raw signal. Structural amendment (v1.2): the *measurement* of this mechanism differs by instrument in a way that materially changes the trigger population. Ruan and Streltsov (CFTC comment letter, 21 May 2025) state that perpetual derivatives "trade with a much smaller basis to the spot market due to the funding rate mechanism, allowing for closer tracking of spot prices", and Ackerer, Hugonnier and Jermann (NBER w32936) report that 75% of Bitcoin futures daily volume and 94% of open interest in the first half of 2023 came from perpetual futures. The published carry-crash evidence is estimated on dated futures (OKEx, CME), whose basis is not mechanically suppressed by a funding rule; on retail perpetuals the same economic state produces a much smaller observable basis. A second amendment (v1.2): the informational content of perp funding appears to be about *volatility* rather than *direction*. A 2026 Journal of Financial Markets paper ("Perpetual futures as predictors of Bitcoin volatility", PII S0927539826000939) finds funding-rate-based predictors materially improve forecasts of Bitcoin spot volatility, with the predictive contribution remaining significant after controlling for fixed-maturity futures volatility measures, and interprets funding as capturing "imbalanced leveraged positions whose subsequent unwinding and the associated price fluctuations unfold over extended periods". This supports the positioning mechanism but relocates its predicted payoff from the sign of the forward return to the dispersion of the forward return. **Amendment (v1.3): the crash mechanism in the perpetual era runs through basis *compression*, and the carry signal's sign is instrument-dependent.** The v1.2 mechanism attributes a crash to a *high* carry state, on the strength of dated-futures evidence. The October 2025 perpetual cascade is documented to have run the other way: the 30-day basis compressed from 6.9% to 4.5% while the cascade was in progress, the unwind of the hedged cash-and-carry book mechanically added selling pressure, and the futures led the crash with the basis swinging $1,367 from -$534 to +$833 in eight minutes while the mark price used for liquidation triggers undershot both spot and futures. Read together, these say two things. First, the mechanical amplification channel in the perpetual era is the *unwind* of the carry trade, which is a state of *falling* basis, not of elevated basis. Second, the basis is violently unstable at the crash instant, which is a warning against a level-based precursor: a variable that can swing $1,367 in eight minutes is not a calm state variable. A second amendment (v1.3) is about the sign itself: the *same research programme* produced an earlier working paper in which carry, measured as the funding rate of BTC/USD perpetual swaps rather than as dated-futures basis, **positively** predicts returns in the BTC time series and in a cross-section of 51 cryptocurrencies. The crash sign and the positive-return sign therefore belong to different instruments, and this entry's target instrument is the perpetual.
6. Exact falsifiable prediction: **Restructured in v1.2 into a tail claim and a withdrawn mean claim, because a direct preregistered test of the v1.1 mean-return version failed with the opposite sign.** (P1, retained, primary) When annualized BTC/ETH perp basis exceeds a preregistered positive threshold (theory-derived starting value +20%) for a sustained period, the *frequency and severity of crashes* over the next 1-4 weeks are elevated — defined as the probability and the conditional magnitude of a forward drawdown exceeding a preregistered threshold — and sell liquidations as % of open interest rise; when basis is negative or near zero, no prediction is made. Direction: basis high -> higher left-tail risk. Horizon: 1-4 weeks. (P2, withdrawn as a directional prediction) The v1.1 statement that "spot returns over the next 1-4 weeks are negative on average" is withdrawn as a preregistered directional claim: a preregistered test on Binance USDT-margined perpetuals over December 2019 to July 2026 found that high-basis days were followed by *higher* forward returns (+4.20% versus +1.59% at 7 days; +14.8% versus +6.1% at 30 days), a difference whose 95% block-bootstrap interval excluded zero on the wrong side. It is retained only as a quantity to be re-estimated, not as a trade direction. (P3, new requirement) Because the mechanism is now modelled as a volatility/instability channel, the prediction must be evaluated as a *tail* statistic (crash probability and conditional crash severity, or the variance of forward returns), and both the tail statistic and the mean-return difference must be reported; success on the tail statistic with a wrong-signed mean difference is a pass on P1, and failure on P1 cannot be excused by a mean-return result in either direction. (P4) The v1.1 addendum stands: the same prediction must survive when the signal is the momentum-residualized basis (basis minus the part of the basis explained by trailing returns), otherwise the effect is not attributable to positioning. **Amendment (v1.3): four requirements are added, and one trigger specification is withdrawn.** (P5, instrument specificity) The prediction is registered on *perpetual* basis; the published carry-crash evidence is registered on *dated futures* basis; and the two are not interchangeable, because the same authors' earlier perpetual-funding work reports the opposite mean sign. A validator must treat 'elevated perpetual basis' and 'elevated dated-futures basis' as two different states, must not import a published coefficient from one to the other, and must report the two instruments separately if both are tested. (P6, the published tail statistic) The peer-reviewed source this entry rests on does not test crash conditionality with a drawdown statistic at all. It tests it with *realized and implied skewness* and with *sell liquidations as a share of open interest*: a rise in carry of 10% predicts a 44% increase in total sell liquidations relative to total open interest over the next month, carry predicts sell but not long liquidations, and carry is strongly negatively related to implied skewness over the next one to three months. Those are the published tail statistics, and a validator that wants the published claim rather than a proxy must register one of them, with the option-implied variant (25-delta risk reversal) flagged as access 2 on free data. (P7, the carry-unwind arm) Because the documented 2025 amplification ran through basis compression and carry-trade unwinding, a separate arm must be registered for the *compression* state - basis falling by a preregistered size from a preregistered trailing high - as a crash-conditionality predictor, and the elevated-basis arm and the compression arm must be reported separately, because a crash in which the basis collapses is mechanically a different event from a crash in which the basis stays elevated. (P8, threshold) The +20% annualized threshold carried in v1.1 is documented to be unattainable on retail perpetuals in the current regime and is withdrawn as a trigger specification: the 30-day basis is reported at about 5.2% annualized against a 4.5% T-bill rate, the three-month basis has paid less than the two-year Treasury for 157 consecutive days since February 2026, and the perpetual basis is mechanically bounded by the funding-rate clamp, so the state the published work studied - carry up to 60% p.a. - is a dated-futures state that perps cannot reach. Thresholds are now to be preregistered by the validation owner in percentile terms, with trigger-state frequency reported separately for the pre- and post-July-2024 windows. This withdrawal is recorded as a parameter correction, not as a re-specification of the prediction: the entry's registered prediction has not itself been tested, so the falsification-handling rule - which forbids re-specification to dodge a failed test of a registered prediction - is not engaged.
7. Candidate explanatory variables and proposed signal concept: Signal = annualized perp basis (mark price minus spot index) / spot index, annualized using the funding/settlement cadence, computed at each 8-hour funding timestamp from only data known at that close (no look-ahead). Plain language: "is futures expensive relative to spot right now?" Forward window starts after the funding close at which the signal is observed.
8. Expected trade expression: Short perp (or reduce long exposure) when basis is extreme; optionally cash-and-carry unwind (long spot, short perp) where borrow and funding allow. Shorting is treated as a separate feasibility question (borrow cost, exchange rules). Not market-neutral by default; applied only in the basis-extreme regime.
9. Primary comparator or null explanation: Random-entry short with the same holding horizon and fee model; spot buy-and-hold over the same windows; basis computed on a venue-shuffled or lagged series as a placebo. High basis could also simply reflect rational cost of carry in a trending bull market, not crowded positioning. Mandatory added comparator (v1.1): a momentum-matched baseline (short when trailing returns are similarly elevated over the same window) and a momentum-residualized signal; if the momentum-matched baseline explains the effect, the positioning interpretation is not supported. Mandatory added comparator (v1.2): a *volatility-matched* baseline. Because perp funding has documented predictive content for forward spot volatility (Journal of Financial Markets, 2026), a validator must compare the high-basis state against states matched on realized volatility, not only on momentum. If high basis merely identifies high-volatility periods and the crash-risk conditionality vanishes once volatility is matched, then CRYPTO-EDGE-001 is a volatility-timing rule wearing a positioning label and the positioning interpretation is not supported. Two further mandatory controls (v1.2): a *carry-availability* control, because the cash-and-carry premium is documented to be effectively unpaid in the 2025-2026 regime (H62/H63 in the martex-quant ledger), so any test must report the trigger-state frequency separately in the pre- and post-2024 subsamples; and a *regime-split* control that reports the same statistics for the pre-July-2024 (published-sample) window and the post-July-2024 window, since the latter is the untested regime. Additional mandatory comparators (v1.3): (vii) an *open-interest-change* baseline, because a peer-reviewed panel study of Binance USDT-margined perpetuals for 2023-2024 finds that Bitcoin open-interest changes are the strongest crash predictor in a logit early-warning model (odds ratio 1.48, out-of-sample AUROC 0.677) - if the open-interest change explains the crash conditionality that the basis state explains, the basis adds nothing; (viii) a *carry-unwind* baseline for the P7 arm, matching on the size of the basis decline rather than on the basis level, so that the compression arm is not just the elevated arm with the sign flipped; and (ix) a *liquidation-direction* control, because the published source finds that carry predicts sell liquidations but not long liquidations, so the predicted liquidation direction must be reported rather than aggregated into a single liquidation figure.
10. Data required, source URLs, free-access classification, granularity, likely history, coverage, limitations: perpetual mark price, spot index price, open interest, long/short liquidation notionals, funding rates; 8-hourly or daily; history at least 2 years. **Ninth-cycle access additions (verified by direct fetch 2026-10-20):** the Binance public data archive (https://data.binance.vision, access 1, no key, no registration) is now confirmed as a primary source for three of this entry's inputs. (a) *Open interest, 5-minute:* data/futures/um/daily/metrics/{SYMBOL}/{SYMBOL}-metrics-YYYY-MM-DD.zip carries create_time, symbol, sum_open_interest, sum_open_interest_value, top-trader long/short ratios and the taker long/short volume ratio; coverage for BTCUSDT is continuous from 2020-09-01 through the present (366 daily files in 2024, 365 in 2025, 292 in 2026 through 19 September), so the entry's mandatory open-interest-change baseline moves from access 2 to **access 1** over the whole post-July-2024 window. (b) *Basis, mark price and funding:* data/futures/um/daily/premiumIndexKlines/ and data/futures/um/daily/markPriceKlines/ carry daily premium-index and mark-price klines from December 2019, and data/futures/um/monthly/fundingRate/ carries monthly funding-rate files per symbol from January 2020 for BTCUSDT, so the basis and funding inputs are confirmed free from a dated archive rather than only by REST pagination. (c) *Liquidation intensity:* there is **no** liquidation dataset anywhere in the archive, so the "sell liquidations as a share of open interest" statistic of P1 is not directly computable on free data; the documented substitute is the open-interest clearing itself, which Garcia Seuma (2026, arXiv:2608.03616) measures at -24.6% intraday for Binance BTC and -45% to -70% on Hyperliquid in the October 2025 cascade, and which the archive's 5-minute open-interest series captures directly. Two construction hazards are recorded: the archive's monthly tree is incomplete for some symbols (ANTUSDT monthly ends 2023-05 while its daily series continues to 2024-05-28) and must not be used for lifetime questions, and a symbol's trading lifetime must be reconstructed as segments because re-listings occur (CVCUSDT is verified zero-volume through 2024 and trading again in September 2026). Sources for replication with free public endpoints (verified 2026-10-20): Binance GET https://fapi.binance.com/fapi/v1/fundingRate (public, no key, limit max 1000, includes fundingTime and markPrice; funding interval/cap/floor changes via GET /fapi/v1/fundingInfo); Bybit GET https://api.bybit.com/v5/market/funding/history (public, no key, 200 rows/page, USDT/USDC/inverse perps); OKX GET https://www.okx.com/api/v5/public/funding-rate-history (public, capped at 100 rows/call); Binance public data archive (https://data.binance.vision, documented in https://github.com/binance/binance-public-data) for klines/trades. Access classification: 1 (confirmed free, public) for funding, mark price and spot on major venues; 2 (apparently free, unverified depth/rate limits) for multi-year REST pagination depth, open interest, and liquidation notional detail. Note: the published carry-crash evidence (BIS WP 1087 / Management Science) used commercial vendor data (Skew, Coinmetrics); that is a source of the published result, not a requirement for a free replication. Access additions (v1.3, verified by direct fetch 2026-10-20): the Deribit public API is confirmed free for the option surface. GET https://www.deribit.com/api/v2/public/get_book_summary_by_currency?currency=BTC&kind=option returned HTTP 200 with 882 live BTC option instruments, each carrying mark_iv, mark_price, open_interest, bid/ask and underlying_price; GET https://www.deribit.com/api/v2/public/get_volatility_index_data?currency=BTC returns the DVOL index with hourly history back to 2019 (HTTP 200). Access 1 for the current surface and the volatility index. Deribit does not serve historical per-instrument book summaries, so a *historically reconstructed* 25-delta risk-reversal series is access 2 (apparently free, depth unverified), which means the option-implied arm of the published tail statistic is the more expensive arm, not the cheaper one; the realized-skew and drawdown variants of P1 remain computable from free daily prices. Binance futures liquidation-notional and open-interest history remain access 2. **Eleventh-cycle access additions (verified by direct fetch 2026-10-20).** (a) *A free liquidation-notional dataset exists outside the Binance archive.* Faulkner (2026), "Forced Liquidation Cascades in Unregulated Perpetual Futures Markets", Zenodo record 19975149, doi 10.5281/zenodo.19975149, CC-BY-4.0, published 2026-05-02, carries a 365-day daily panel (2025-03-01 to 2026-02-26) of forced-exit notionals assembled from CoinGlass liquidation history and CoinGecko spot data, totalling $176.6bn across eleven exchanges, with three intraday funding readings per day, 13,800+ daily observations, a parallel $54.0bn Bitcoin-specific series, and a full-year OHLC panel; both the PDF and the XLSX are directly downloadable from the Zenodo record with no key, and the author states that "no estimates, models, or proxies are used in any quantitative claim". Downloaded and inspected 2026-10-20: the workbook has three sheets (Cover, Daily Dataset with 383 rows, October 10 Cross-Asset with 44 rows). Access 1 for the download; the *underlying* CoinGlass liquidation history is a paid aggregator, so the dataset is a free, single-author, one-year snapshot rather than a reproducible feed, and the panel is daily and cross-venue-aggregated, so it carries neither per-contract nor long-versus-short direction. (b) *A free, per-trade, direction-attributed forced-liquidation record exists on Hyperliquid.* Garcia Seuma (2026, arXiv:2608.03616) states that the venue's "on-chain fill log ... attributes every forced fill to a liquidated user", and the AFT 2026 paper (see item 15) uses the complete on-chain record of orders and trades for Hyperliquid from 2025-03-27 to 2026-06-30 across its ten most actively traded contracts, with forced liquidations recorded at millisecond resolution and with position size, realised P&L and counterparty type. Access 1 for the on-chain record. This is the only free per-contract, per-event, direction-attributed forced-liquidation record found in the eleventh cycle, and it is on a different venue from the entry's target instrument for the whole of its available window. (c) *A free public L2 order-book archive for Hyperliquid exists* (s3://hyperliquid-archive, used by Lim 2026), publicly accessible but requester-pays; Lim's own replication repository records approximately USD 10 of egress for a 92-day, five-asset sample, so the marginal data cost is small but non-zero. Access 2 (free to read, small requester-pays egress cost). (d) *The Binance REST API is unreachable from this research environment*, which is an environment limitation and not a data determination: on 2026-10-20 https://api.binance.com/api/v3/time, https://api1.binance.com/api/v3/time, https://api2.binance.com/api/v3/time, https://api3.binance.com/api/v3/time, https://fapi.binance.com/fapi/v1/time and https://fapi.binance.com/fapi/v1/exchangeInfo all returned HTTP 451 with the body "Service unavailable from a restricted location according to 'b. Eligibility'", https://api.bybit.com/v5/market/time returned HTTP 403, https://www.okx.com/api/v5/public/time returned HTTP 200, and https://api.deribit.com/api/v2/public/get_time did not connect. The S3 archive remained fully accessible throughout, so the *data* for the entry's core inputs is unaffected; only this machine's REST route is. **Consequence:** the tenth cycle's statement that the published "sell liquidations as a share of open interest" statistic is "not computable on free data" is too strong and is narrowed here. It remains true that the statistic is not computable from the Binance archive, which holds no liquidation dataset. It is now computable on free data on Hyperliquid from the on-chain forced-fill record (per contract, per fill, with direction, 2025-03-27 to 2026-06-30) and at daily cross-venue granularity from the Zenodo panel (365 days). The entry's own free-data accessibility score was already at its 5/5 ceiling and does not move; what changes is the set of admissible tail statistics, not the entry's score.
**Thirteenth-cycle access additions (verified by direct fetch and file inspection on 2026-10-20).** (a) **A free, per-contract, per-event, side-attributed forced-liquidation record exists on Binance itself, on the COIN-M (inverse) tree, and no prior cycle found it.** Path: `data/futures/cm/daily/liquidationSnapshot/{SYMBOL}/{SYMBOL}-liquidationSnapshot-YYYY-MM-DD.zip` (plus a `.CHECKSUM` beside every file). Coverage, enumerated over the whole tree: **118 symbol directories** (the COIN-M perpetuals, e.g. BTCUSD_PERP, ETHUSD_PERP, SOLUSD_PERP, ADAUSD_PERP, and the quarterly delivery contracts, e.g. BTCUSD_240329) totalling **17,182 symbol-days**, all of them inside the publisher window **2023-06-25 to 2024-10-14** (per-symbol last dates 2024-10-10 to 2024-10-14). Schema: `time,side,order_type,time_in_force,original_quantity,price,average_price,order_status,last_fill_quantity,accumulated_fill_quantity`; every observed row is LIMIT / IOC / FILLED; timestamps are epoch milliseconds with roughly 1-30 second spacing, so the record is per-event rather than a 5-minute snapshot. Access classification **1** (confirmed free, public, no key, no registration).
(b) **Three construction hazards, all of them silent-failure defects, all found before any interpretation.** (i) **Every event is written twice**: on each of five sampled days (2023-06-25, 2023-10-20, 2024-08-04, 2024-08-05, 2024-08-06) the raw row count was exactly twice the count of distinct rows and the multiplicity set was exactly {2}, so a naive sum of notional **doubles** the statistic. The same hazard class appears independently in the Hyperliquid fill log, where Garcia Seuma (2026, arXiv:2608.03616) records that "the liquidation object rides both legs of each trade, so all totals are deduplicated by the liquidated-user leg or they double-count" — two venues, one hazard, and the catalogue now treats de-duplication as a mandatory step for any per-event forced-flow record. (ii) **The quantity is in contracts, not in the asset, and the COIN-M contract multiplier is 100 USD per contract** (Binance's own contract specification: "Each COIN-margined contract represents 100 USD", "Contract Multiplier 100 USD per contract"). Notional is therefore `original_quantity x 100`, and the intuitive `quantity x price` product is wrong by a factor of the price in USD: on 2024-08-05 that product gives **$17.06bn** of forced selling for BTCUSD_PERP where the correct figure is **$32.3m** — a **528-fold** overstatement, which would have made the tail statistic look enormously powerful. (iii) **`side` is the side of the liquidation order**, so a SELL row is a liquidated **long** (forced selling) and a BUY row is a liquidated short; the entry's P1 statistic counts SELL rows, and the count must be halved for the duplication in (i).
(c) **The registered statistic's denominator is available, on the same contract, in the same unit.** `data/futures/cm/daily/metrics/{SYMBOL}/` carries the 5-minute `sum_open_interest` in **contracts** (with `sum_open_interest_value` in the coin), and marker-paged it runs continuously from 2021-07-08 to **2026-10-19** for BTCUSD_PERP, ETHUSD_PERP, SOLUSD_PERP, ADAUSD_PERP and SUIUSD_PERP. The COIN-M metrics therefore **do overlap** the liquidation window, and the statistic "sell liquidation contracts / open interest contracts" is dimensionless and price-invariant. *(A caution for the audit trail: a naive first-page listing of the same metrics tree ends at 2022-11-24 for BTCUSD_PERP and appears to show that the denominator is unavailable — that is the catalogue's own recorded pagination defect reproducing itself, and the true end is 2026-10-19. The correction is in item 15.)*
(d) **The USDⓈ-M gap is now a determination, not an inference.** Every prefix in the bucket was enumerated on 2026-10-20: `data/futures/um/daily/` holds aggTrades, bookDepth, bookTicker, indexPriceKlines, klines, markPriceKlines, metrics, premiumIndexKlines and trades and nothing else; `data/futures/um/monthly/` adds fundingRate to the same list; `data/futures/cm/daily/` adds **liquidationSnapshot** and `data/futures/cm/monthly/` does not; `data/option/daily/` holds only BVOLIndex and EOHSummary; `data/spot/daily/` holds only aggTrades, klines and trades. **There is no liquidation dataset anywhere in the USDⓈ-M tree**, and the entry's specified instrument is USDⓈ-M. **The catalogue's recorded fact is therefore superseded in place, not deleted: "there is no liquidation dataset anywhere in the archive" was true of the UM tree and false of the CM tree.**
(e) **A new free order-book-depth tree was found and is directly usable for the prospective liquidity-risk line of work.** `data/futures/{um,cm}/daily/bookDepth/{SYMBOL}/` runs 2023-01-01 to **2026-10-19** for both families, with schema `timestamp,percentage,depth,notional` — **a twelve-level ladder** (levels at -5%, -4%, -3%, -2%, -1%, **-0.20%**, **+0.20%**, +1%, +2%, +3%, +4% and +5% of the mid), each level carrying quantity depth and USD notional, sampled at a nominal 30-second cadence (**2,880 distinct timestamps per day**, with ±1-3 second jitter; 34,560 rows per day). `data/futures/um/daily/bookTicker/{SYMBOL}/` covers 2023-05-16 to 2024-03-30. This is the input set that SaR (arXiv:2603.09164) requires, and it is free, dated and current (see item 15). **Fourteenth-cycle correction to this paragraph, marked superseded in place: the ladder has twelve levels, not the ten the thirteenth cycle recorded.** The thirteenth cycle's list omitted the two inner ±0.20% bands, which is the band a market liquidation order actually executes in, so a validator who built a slippage estimate from the recorded ten-level specification would have computed slippage from a ladder that jumps from 1% to 0.20% and would have overstated the cost of crossing the inner book. The thirteenth cycle's other assertions — the window, the schema, the field semantics and the 2,880-timestamp cadence — were re-verified live and stand.

**Fourteenth-cycle access additions and corrections (verified by direct fetch, listing and file inspection on 2026-10-20).**
(f) **The contract multiplier is per symbol, and the recorded "$100 for the COIN-M family" is superseded in place.** The multiplier is not a family constant. It was derived for eleven symbols by pairing two independent free venue files — the metrics file's `sum_open_interest` (contracts) and `sum_open_interest_value` (the coin) against the same day's daily kline open price — as `multiplier = sum_open_interest_value x open_price / sum_open_interest`: **BTCUSD_PERP 100.02, BTCUSD_240628 99.997, ETHUSD_PERP 10.000, ETHUSD_241227 9.999, SOLUSD_PERP 9.39-at-close (10 at the open), BNBUSD_PERP 10.005, XRPUSD_PERP 10.003, DOGEUSD_PERP 9.993, ADAUSD_PERP 10.07-at-close (10 at the open), LINKUSD_PERP 10.00-at-open, SOLUSD_250328 9.961, BNBUSD_250328 10.005.** The rule therefore becomes: **notional = original_quantity x the multiplier of that symbol**, which the thirteenth cycle's own source note had already implied but its stated rule ("notional is `original_quantity x 100`") did not. The practical consequence is that the naive `quantity x price` product overstates a non-BTC contract's forced notional by a factor of its own price in USD (about 2,400x for ETHUSD_PERP on 2024-08-05, against 528x for BTCUSD_PERP) — so the thirteenth cycle's headline overstatement figure is a **BTC-specific** number and the general error is *larger* for every other contract.
(g) **The record's file set is not a daily panel, and the absence is structured.** Over the whole tree, the 118 symbols' own spans total **26,834 symbol-days** against **17,182 published files**, so **9,652 symbol-days (36.0%) are absent**, and **110 of the 118 symbols have internal gaps** (no symbol is contiguous). The absence is strongly non-random in symbol: **1.3%** for BTCUSD_PERP (6 absent days of 478) and **0.8%** for ETHUSD_PERP, against **47.6%** for TRXUSD_PERP (226 of 475) and **62.2%** for ICXUSD_PERP. It is also strongly co-missing in date: on **219 dates at least 10 of the live perps are simultaneously absent**, the worst being **2023-09-09 and 2023-09-25, each missing for 39 of the 41 live perps (95.1%)**. Two dates are *venue-wide* failures across products — **2024-04-18** (missing from the CM klines and trades trees as well) and **2024-06-11 and 2024-06-12** (missing from the CM klines, trades and metrics trees) — while 2023-09-09 and 2023-09-23 are missing only from the liquidation tree, so the failure is product-specific on some dates. The publisher's own remediation is visible in the bucket: **49 liquidation files were re-uploaded on 2026-04-16** for exactly the five previously-absent dates 2023-09-25 (3 files), 2023-11-20 (6), **2024-04-18 (33)**, 2024-06-11 (4) and 2024-06-12 (3) — a complete remediation of the venue-wide failure date and a partial one of the market-wide date. The two readings of an absent file — "the day had no forced-liquidation event for that symbol" and "the publisher's job failed for that day" — **cannot be separated from the archive alone**, and the catalogue does not choose between them; the *mandatory* consequence is that **an absent file must never be read as a zero-event day without a declared treatment**, and that any cross-sectional breadth statistic computed on this record must have its denominator fixed to the live universe rather than to the files present, or it is mechanically inflated.
(h) **Why the record ends, and what the archive's history is: three primary venue documents.** (i) The publisher **discontinued the liquidation-snapshot product**: in the publisher's own data repository, a member's report that the USDⓈ-M files were gone was answered by a repository collaborator on **2024-10-15** with "**this data is no longer provided**", and the issue was closed as completed the same day — which is **24 hours after the last file was written** (the file for data day 2024-10-14 was written on 2024-10-15, the normal one-day publication lag: 16,445 of 17,182 files have a one-day lag and the last one has a one-day lag). A second collaborator statement, dated **2026-10-06**, says it again: "liquidationSnapshot is no longer provided on data.binance.vision. **USD-M data has been removed**, and the **last COIN-M file is 2024-10-14**." (ii) **The USDⓈ-M liquidation record that the catalogue recorded as never having existed did exist.** The same repository carries a report titled "USDT-M liquidationSnapshot data missing **after 2024-03-31**", and the venue's own historical-data FAQ documents a "Liquidation Snapshot" product with the date range **2023-06-25 to 2024-03-31**; the 2024-06-14 comment on that report also notes, from the community, the **exact-two row duplication** that the catalogue found independently two years later, and no staff answer explains it. (iii) The venue's own issue tracker is therefore a **primary, dated document about data availability**, and the catalogue now treats it as such.
(i) **The consequence for this entry is a re-framing, not a repair.** The thirteenth cycle recorded, as a settled fact, that "there is no liquidation dataset anywhere in the USDⓈ-M tree" and that the entry's specified instrument therefore had no direct measurement. The correct statement is narrower and worse for the entry in one way and better in another: **the USDⓈ-M per-contract forced-liquidation record existed on the venue's own archive from 2023-06-25 to 2024-03-31 and has since been removed from the archive**, so (a) the entry's own instrument *is* documented to have carried the registered statistic for 281 days, and (b) the direct measurement on the entry's specified instrument is now a **retrieval** question — whether any mirror of the withdrawn UM files exists — and not a construction question. The 13th cycle's supersession note is accordingly **extended**: "no liquidation dataset in the UM tree" is true of the archive *today* and false of the archive's *history*.
(j) **A free revision detector, and the futures tree's versioning status is now established.** The bucket's `LastModified` field is a per-file, free, machine-readable publication timestamp, and it is sufficient to detect re-uploads without any update log: a file whose `LastModified` is not its expected T+1 publication date has been **re-uploaded**. In the liquidation tree, 49 files deviate from the normal lag and all 49 carry the single date **2026-04-16** — the same backfill described in (g). The published update log is silent about them: the repository's `updates/` directory contains only three artefacts, all from 2022, none covering futures paths, and the README's Updates table has two rows (2022-04-21 and 2022-08-08). **The futures tree is therefore revised, and the revision is not enumerated in the published update log** — which settles, negatively and on evidence, the eleventh cycle's open question about futures versioning.
11. Plausible friction and capacity considerations (theoretical, no measured values): funding paid on the short side erodes carry; short availability and borrow costs; slippage entering during stress; exchange leverage caps and liquidation of the arbitrage trade itself before convergence; turnover is limited to basis-extreme regimes so capacity is modest. No friction values are invented.
12. Why meaningfully different from the catalogue and prior art: Grounded in documented basis premium (Chi 2023) and carry-crash predictability (BIS WP 1087 / Management Science 2026), with a precise falsifiable liquidation-linked prediction and an explicit momentum-residualization requirement. Distinct from plain moving-average crossovers, from CRYPTO-EDGE-002 (level of cost of carry vs funding-flow extremes), and from CRYPTO-EDGE-003 (absolute time-series carry on a benchmark venue vs cross-venue relative dispersion).
13. Strongest argument against; likely failure regimes; confounders: **Ninth-cycle addition, now the second-strongest objection:** the crash mechanism this entry monetises is documented, in its two largest recent instances, to be *shock-driven and not anticipated from any pre-crash state*, which is close to the opposite of the entry's premise. Garcia Seuma (5 August 2026, arXiv:2608.03616) measures the branching ratio of the October 2025 record cascade in flight and finds it deeply subcritical (lambda-hat approximately 0.1), the transition abrupt and first-order rather than critical, with the in-cascade signature in the liquidity sector - price-impact spikes of 1.2-3.5x regressed and 3.2-9.1x quoted across two venues and two instruments, while open interest clears by 25-70% (-24.6% for Binance BTC, to 0.30-0.55 of baseline on Hyperliquid) - and shows that the branching model as an account of the *pre-cascade state* fails both of its falsifiable predictions with simulated power of at least 0.9. The companion paper (arXiv:2607.27070) adds that the price signature is present in five of seven cascades but silent in exactly the two sudden-news shocks, and that the October 2025 pattern does not replicate the August 2024 pattern but inverts it. A separate independent 2026 study finds that conditioning on the perpetual basis, funding rate, funding clock, volatility and session is flat in every case across 183 Binance pairs (Kitron and Wengrowicz 2026, arXiv:2608.21888), i.e. the state variable this entry uses carries no standalone information where it has been tested on a wide panel. None of these is a test of this entry's registered crash-conditionality claim, so the falsification-handling rule is not engaged and the entry is not rejected; but the prior that a positioning state can anticipate a crypto crash is now materially weaker than it was when the entry was written. (i) Basis can remain elevated through long trending bull markets. **Ninth-cycle adversarial duplicate check against the rejected CRYPTO-EDGE-002 (carried over from the eighth cycle's instruction and now performed):** the two are *adjacent but not duplicates*. CRYPTO-EDGE-002 was a mean-return contrarian claim (trailing-90-day percentile of daily funding, 10th/90th deciles, 7-day forward LOW-minus-HIGH) and it was tested and failed with wrong-signed point estimates. This entry's basis-compression arm conditions on a *change* in the basis rather than on its percentile level, and its outcome is a tail statistic (crash frequency and severity) rather than a mean return; the rejected entry's test cannot refute it, because a mean-return test has no power against a tail claim. Two consequences are recorded for the validation plan: first, the compression arm's *mean* leg must not be read as a second opinion on the rejected contrarian entry, and a failure of the mean leg would leave the rejected entry untouched; second, the input is shared - both entries read the same funding series - so a validator must pre-commit which entry is being tested before inspecting the data, or the two can be traded off against each other post hoc. (carry may be profitable while the trend continues). (ii) The published crash-predictive relation is estimated on dated futures (OKEx, CME) with data from Skew; transfer to perps on retail venues is untested, and Gornall, Rinaldi & Xiao (2025) report that perpetuals now dominate crypto derivatives volume and reduce extreme price dislocations, which mechanically shrinks the population of extreme-basis trigger states in the perp era. (iii) Crashes may be driven by exogenous news rather than positioning; Garcia Seuma (2026) finds that pre-crash signatures are event-heterogeneous and that the most attractive in-sample pattern from the October 2025 record cascade inverts out of sample on the August 2024 cascade, and that a pure news shock (February 2025 tariff episode) shows no precursor in any variable - a direct warning for any single-variable crash precursor. (iv) Liquidation data is noisy and often proxied. (v) Momentum explains much of the basis, so the raw signal may be a momentum proxy. (vi, new in v1.2 — the strongest single objection) A preregistered test on Binance USDT-margined perpetuals over December 2019 to July 2026 found that the *sign* of the high-basis state was backwards: the 7-day forward return difference between low-basis and high-basis states was -2.61% with a 95% block-bootstrap interval of [-4.88%, -0.12%], i.e. the interval excluded zero on the side opposite to the registered prediction, with high-premium days followed by +4.20% versus +1.59% at 7 days and +14.8% versus +6.1% at 30 days. This is the closest available test of CRYPTO-EDGE-001's directional claim, it covers the post-July-2024 window that the published evidence does not, and it contradicts the mean-return form of the prediction on the instrument family CRYPTO-EDGE-001 targets. It does not test crash conditionality, which is why v1.2 restructures the prediction rather than rejecting the entry outright. (vii, new in v1.2) The tradeable expression has little left to earn in the current regime: the cash-and-carry premium on the same venue complex is documented to be effectively unpaid over the most recent 365 days (+0.08%/yr always-on; +0.15%/yr with a funding filter), with the premium concentrated in earlier years. A crash-timing rule is then a low-frequency, high-variance overlay on a premium that is no longer being paid, and the short leg pays or receives funding in a regime where funding is near zero. Likely failure regimes: strong trending markets, exogenous shock events, regime change in venue leverage rules, and any regime in which perp basis is mechanically compressed by the funding mechanism. (viii, new in v1.3 - the most damaging object) The objection is now *author-side*. The published carry-crash paper and the earlier working paper of two of its authors sit on the same research programme and use different instruments: on dated-futures basis, high carry predicts crashes; on perpetual funding rates, carry positively predicts returns, in the time series and across 51 cryptocurrencies. This entry's target instrument is the perpetual, so a dated-futures crash coefficient does not transfer to it, and the same authors' own perpetual evidence points the other way on the mean. This is independent of, and much stronger than, the preregistered ledger result that cycle 6 relied on: it is not a second party testing a claim, it is the original authors' own earlier result on the target instrument with the opposite sign. (ix, new in v1.3) The published crash-conditionality result is also not specific to basis within the derivatives complex: open-interest changes are documented as the strongest crash predictor in a peer-reviewed panel study of Binance perpetuals for 2023-2024, so even if a basis effect exists, basis may be the weaker of the two positioning variables, and a validator that omits the open-interest control cannot attribute any effect to positioning specifically. (x, new in v1.3) The registered absolute trigger is unattainable in the current regime: the 30-day basis is reported at about 5.2% annualized against a 4.5% T-bill rate, the three-month basis has paid less than the two-year Treasury for 157 consecutive days since February 2026, and the perpetual basis is mechanically bounded by the funding-rate clamp, so the published trigger state is a dated-futures state that perps cannot reach. (xi, new in v1.3) Four independent external tests in the perpetual era find no unconditional contrarian edge in funding or basis extremes, and they document *cross-asset sign heterogeneity* rather than a common effect: on a 789-day, six-asset, three-venue sample the top-decile funding state was followed by a negative median 72-hour return on BTC and positive median 72-hour returns on ETH, BNB, XRP and DOGE; a separate intraday study of Binance perps from September 2019 to August 2026 found extreme *negative* funding predicts a positive 24-hour return while extreme *positive* funding has no comparable effect; a conditional study of 274 funding-extreme events across BTC, ETH and SOL from 2021 to 2026 found that events on up days revert by +1.88% over 24 hours while events on down days continue by -1.74%, a +3.62pp gap with a 95% block-bootstrap interval of [2.60, 4.67] - which is a statement that the sign depends on a state variable rather than that a stable edge exists; and the preregistered ledger test of the perp basis failed with a wrong-signed point estimate. Any one of these could be wrong, and they disagree with each other on the negative tail; together they mean the family has no established unconditional edge on the instrument this entry targets, and the entry's remaining claim - a *tail* claim - has never been tested there at all. **Eleventh-cycle additions.** (xii) **The free proxy specified in v1.5 is a state, and the entry's payoff is a flow; the eleventh cycle found the published magnitudes that let the gap be sized.** Garcia Seuma's own words are the sharpest statement of the problem: "for the intraday analysis we necessarily observe the consequences of forced liquidation (open interest, aggressor flow) rather than liquidations themselves". The October 2025 event is the one case where both are published on the same window: open interest cleared by 25-70% (to 0.30-0.55 of baseline on Hyperliquid, -24.6% intraday on Binance BTC) while the realised liquidation map records $0.96bn of book-hitting forced sales across eighty 0.25%-wide price buckets, of which $733m landed after the cascade onset. A clearing of that size is therefore consistent with a large forced flow and with a large voluntary close, and the two cannot be separated by the open-interest series alone, because net open interest is a netting of opens against closes, and a new position opened during the window masks a large exit. The v1.5 specification's demand for a discriminator between a clearing and ordinary decay is therefore not a formality: on a net series the discriminator is weak by construction. (xiii) **The proxy as written in v1.5 has a units defect that would have produced a false positive.** The v1.5 text specifies the peak-to-trough percentage decline in `sum_open_interest_value`, which is the USD-notional open interest read directly from the archive's 5-minute metrics file. Notional open interest moves with price, so a price decline with an unchanged contract count produces a notional decline with zero liquidations; a falling market would generate proxy clearings that contain no forced flow at all. The same free file carries `sum_open_interest`, the contract-count series, which is price-invariant; verified 2026-10-20 by reading data/futures/um/daily/metrics/BTCUSDT/BTCUSDT-metrics-2026-10-19.zip, whose header is create_time,symbol,sum_open_interest,sum_open_interest_value,count_toptrader_long_short_ratio,sum_toptrader_long_short_ratio,count_long_short_ratio,sum_taker_long_short_vol_ratio, with a first row of sum_open_interest 93855.955 and sum_open_interest_value 7721663336.37 at 00:55 UTC. The fix is free and is now mandatory in v1.6. (xiv) **Execution cost, not only price, is impaired after a cascade, and this is the entry's own payoff window.** Lim (2026, Journal of FinTech and Digital Assets 1(2):54-68) documents a two-regime recovery on Hyperliquid after the October 2025 cascade: quoted spreads returned to within 1.1-1.2 times pre-event levels within 23 days, but inside-quote depth ended the window between 66% and 79% of pre-event levels and continued to contract from days 1-7 to days 17-23, and the paper's conclusion is that "spread-only metrics systematically understate post-event impairment of venue liquidity". A crash-timing overlay is taken at its worst possible moment in the liquidity surface, which is the friction the entry's net-value score already had to carry, now with a published magnitude.
14. Research-only validation plan to be executed elsewhere: preselect the basis threshold; define entry at funding close and exit at subsequent funding closes over 1-4 weeks; baseline versus random entry and versus momentum-matched entry; report both raw-basis and momentum-residualized signal variants; theoretical friction cost model (funding, spread, slippage, borrow); out-of-sample split with an explicit post-July-2024 subsample; regime coverage (bull, bear, sideways, liquidation-event windows); leave-one-venue-out check. **Amended in v1.2:** (a) the primary statistic is a *tail* statistic — the difference in the probability and conditional severity of a forward drawdown exceeding a preregistered threshold between high- and low-basis states — with the mean-return difference reported alongside it but not decisive; (b) the volatility-matched baseline from item 9 is mandatory and is computed on the same high/low state split; (c) the trigger-state *frequency* is reported separately for the pre- and post-July-2024 subsamples, because the perp-era basis distribution is mechanically compressed (Ruan and Streltsov 2025) and the carry premium is documented to be unpaid in 2025-2026, so a lower trigger rate and a lower trigger severity are different findings; (d) inference must be heavy-tail aware — Grobys and Shahzad (2026) show that the realised variances of crypto momentum strategies follow power laws and that the population mean and variance of the factor are statistically not defined under block bootstrap, so the validator should use block bootstrap on the statistic, report median-based summaries, and treat a Sharpe ratio as uninformative for the underlying factor; (e) falsification criteria: no elevated crash probability or crash severity after high basis, no rise in sell liquidations as % of OI, the effect vanishing once momentum is controlled, or the effect vanishing once realized volatility is matched. Do not execute this plan in this repository. **Amended in v1.3:** (f) the primary statistic remains a tail statistic, but the validator must register *which* tail statistic - drawdown frequency and conditional severity from free prices (access 1), realized skew from free daily prices (access 1), or the published sell-liquidation-share statistic (access 2) - and must not silently substitute one for another, because they are different claims with different data requirements and different nulls; (g) the two trigger arms (elevated basis, compressing basis) are registered and reported separately, with the P7 arm's trigger defined as a basis decline of a preregistered size from a preregistered trailing high, and with the arms' trigger frequencies reported separately; (h) the open-interest-change baseline of item 9 is mandatory, because a basis effect that open interest already captures is not a positioning edge; (i) the percentile-based trigger frequency is reported monthly across the full sample, so that a reader can see whether the perpetual-era basis distribution even admits a high-basis state at all; and (j) the falsification criteria gain three members: no elevated crash probability or crash severity after either trigger arm, no effect after the open-interest-change control, and no effect in the post-July-2024 subsample that is not also present in the published-sample window. Do not execute this plan in this repository. **Amended in v1.5 (tenth cycle — cycle-10 specification, fixed in advance of any data inspection):** (a) **The instrument and asset class are fixed before everything else**: USDⓈ-M perpetual futures on Binance (Bybit and OKX as robustness legs), BTC and ETH, 8-hour funding settlement, with the spot leg from the same venue's spot klines and the perp leg from the venue's own mark-price or premium-index series so that the basis is computed on matched timestamps. No dated-futures result may be cited as support without the instrument stated, and the entry is not transferable to dated futures. (b) **The trigger is a percentile, never an absolute level**, because the perp-era basis distribution is mechanically compressed: the elevated arm is the trailing-90-day percentile of the perp premium at or above the 90th, computed per symbol from trailing data only; the compression arm is a fall of a preregistered size (stated in basis points, one funding interval of premium) from a preregistered trailing high. The two arms are never pooled, and each arm's trigger frequency is reported monthly over the whole sample so that a reader can see whether the perp-era distribution even admits a high state. (c) **The tail statistic is preregistered as one of three and may not be swapped afterwards**: (i) the forward maximum drawdown from the trigger close over the next 1-4 weeks exceeding a preregistered multiple of the symbol's trailing 90-day realized volatility; (ii) the realized skew of the forward 21-day return; or (iii) the published "sell liquidations as a share of open interest" statistic — which is **not free**, because the archive holds no liquidation dataset, so choosing (iii) makes the test non-compliant with the free-data standard and lowers the entry's free-data score. (d) **The free proxy for the tail statistic is the open-interest clearing**, since liquidation notionals are not archived: the observable footprint of forced unwinding is the peak-to-trough percentage decline in `sum_open_interest_value` from the archive's 5-minute `daily/metrics/` series over the trigger window, with the window fixed to the same 1-4 weeks as the price statistic and with a preregistered discriminator that separates a *clearing* from ordinary open-interest decay, calibrated on the trailing distribution and not on the events themselves. Garcia Seuma's documented magnitudes — open interest clearing 25-70% across cascades, −24.6% intraday on Binance BTC, to 0.30-0.55 of baseline on Hyperliquid — are the reference point the proxy must reproduce in those windows, and a validator must report the proxy's value on those known cascades as a construction check before interpreting anything else. (e) **The tail statistic and the mean statistic are reported separately and never netted.** The tenth cycle supplied the reason: an independent preregistered test documents that a crash state in the perp era is followed by a positive cross-sectional return (alts +0.441% net the day after a >3% BTC down day, 727 held days), and another documents continuation after extreme up shocks (+3.54% over 7 days). A signal that predicts crash *frequency* may therefore also predict a *positive* forward return, so any statistic that combines the two is uninterpretable. (f) **Mandatory controls, all computed on the same state split**: the volatility-matched baseline (mandatory, because perp funding is documented to forecast spot volatility, so a high-basis state that merely identifies a high-volatility period is not a positioning signal), the momentum-matched baseline, the open-interest-change baseline (a basis effect that open interest already captures is not a positioning edge), and the carry-availability control (the same statistics in the pre- and post-July-2024 subsamples, because the carry premium is documented to be unpaid in the 2025-2026 regime). (g) **Inference**: block bootstrap on dates with the cross-section kept intact within blocks; median-based summaries; the volatility of the trigger bucket reported alongside the mean difference; **no Sharpe ratio** (Grobys and Shahzad 2026). (h) **The null outcome is an available, publishable outcome**: "no elevated crash probability or severity after either arm, no effect after the open-interest-change control, and no effect in the post-July-2024 window" is a result, not a failure, and the design must be able to return it. (i) A validator must not silently substitute a different tail statistic, a different trigger, a different window, a different instrument, or a different sign convention. Do not execute this plan in this repository. **Amended in v1.6 (eleventh cycle - three corrections to the cycle-10 specification, all forced by direct verification, all made in advance of any data inspection):** (a) **The free proxy is re-specified on the contract-count series.** The tail-statistic proxy must be the peak-to-trough percentage decline in `sum_open_interest` (contract count) from the archive's 5-minute `daily/metrics/` series, not in `sum_open_interest_value` (USD notional), which is contaminated by price and would register a price decline as a clearing. Both fields are in the same free file, so the corrected specification is free and strictly stronger. Where a notional series is used for any other purpose it must be deflated by the contemporaneous price before use. (b) **A fourth tail statistic is added and is now the preferred arm on the venues where it exists.** The published "sell liquidations as a share of open interest" statistic is computable on free data on Hyperliquid from the on-chain forced-fill record (per contract, per fill, with direction, 2025-03-27 to 2026-06-30) and at daily cross-venue granularity from the Zenodo panel (2025-03-01 to 2026-02-26). The v1.5 statement that choosing this statistic "makes the test non-compliant with the free-data standard" is **superseded in place**: it is non-compliant only on the entry's target instrument and window, and it is compliant elsewhere. A validator must register one statistic and not switch; the choice must now also record the venue, the window, and whether the liquidation measure is per-fill or daily-aggregated.
