(c) **A proxy-fidelity validation step is mandatory and must be reported before any interpretation.** The proxy's fidelity to realised forced flow is no longer an open question to be argued: it is testable, and a validator must (i) measure, on the same instrument and window, the proxy's value against the realised forced-exit notional on the free on-chain record for Hyperliquid over 2025-03-27 to 2026-06-30 and on the Zenodo daily panel where both series exist, (ii) report the proxy's value on the known cascades against Garcia Seuma's published magnitudes (25-70% clearing; -24.6% Binance BTC; 0.30-0.55 of baseline on Hyperliquid; $0.96bn book-hitting forced sales with $733m after onset), and (iii) report the proxy's false-positive rate on ordinary open-interest decay days, because the failure mode of interest is a price-driven decline being read as a liquidation event. A validator who cannot run the fidelity step must say so and must not interpret the proxy's values as evidence of forced liquidations. The v1.5 items (a) through (i) are otherwise unchanged. Do not execute this plan in this repository.
**Amended in v1.7 (thirteenth cycle — the tail statistic is now directly measurable, and the proxy-fidelity step is now executable, both on free Binance-native data; made in advance of any interpretation).** (i) **The primary tail statistic may now be computed directly rather than proxied**, on the COIN-M family: `sell-side liquidation contracts / open-interest contracts`, both read from the venue's own free daily files, over 2023-06-25 to 2024-10-14, with the three construction hazards fixed in advance — de-duplicate on the full row, multiply contracts by the venue's 100 USD multiplier rather than by the price, and read `side` as the order side so that SELL is a liquidated long. (ii) **The mandatory proxy-fidelity step now has a same-venue, same-unit benchmark**, and the first measurement of it is recorded in item 15; because the two quantities are of different kinds — the open-interest reduction is a stock change and the forced flow is a flow — a validator must report their **ratio** rather than treating them as interchangeable, and must state whether the ratio is stable across ordinary days and cascade days. (iii) **Because the direct measurement exists only on the inverse (COIN-M) family, a test carried out there is a replication, not an exact replication, of a test on the entry's specified USDⓈ-M instrument**; the entry's regime-coverage statement must say so explicitly, and the instrument substitution is a *documented* substitution rather than an implicit one. (iv) The entry's registered prediction, the mandatory controls, the trigger definition, the horizons and the null outcome are all unchanged, and no empirical validation was performed in this repository.

**Amended in v1.8 (fourteenth cycle — four additions that the record's own re-verification forces, made in advance of any interpretation).** (i) **The missingness treatment is now mandatory and must be declared, not assumed.** A validator using this record must state, for every symbol-day it reads, whether an absent file is treated as a zero-event day, as a missing observation, or as excluded; must compute its denominators over the **live universe** (the symbols whose own span covers the date) rather than over the files present; and must report the number of absent symbol-days in the sample it uses, because the absence is 36.0% of the panel and is non-random in both symbol and date. (ii) **The proxy-fidelity ratio must be reported per contract and per day, not once.** The fourteenth cycle's cross-sectional measurement shows the open-interest-reduction-to-forced-flow ratio running from **0.56** (ENSUSD_PERP, where the forced flow *exceeds* the net open-interest reduction) to **34.2** (DOTUSD_PERP) on a single day, and from 4.3 to 8.2 for one contract (BTCUSD_PERP) across three consecutive days; a single headline ratio is therefore uninformative, and a validator must report the ratio's distribution and state the mechanism for each contract's departure from one (a new position opened during the cascade masks an exit — the same contamination the eleventh cycle identified in the proxy's own units, now visible across the cross-section). (iii) **The cross-sectional aggregation rule must be preregistered with its weighting.** On 2024-08-05 the registered statistic spans **0.26% to 37.94%** of opening open interest across the 36 contracts with a file, so an equal-weighted cross-contract mean and a notional-weighted mean differ by an order of magnitude and both are defensible; a validator must fix the weighting in advance, and must report the statistic per contract as well as in aggregate. (iv) **The notional conversion must use the symbol's own multiplier** — 100 USD for the BTCUSD contracts and 10 USD for the others checked — and a validator must not carry forward the thirteenth cycle's "x 100" rule, which is wrong for at least eight of the eleven symbols checked. (v) The registered prediction, the mandatory controls, the trigger definition, the horizons and the null outcome remain **unchanged**, and no empirical validation was performed in this repository.

**Amended in v1.9 (fifteenth cycle — the direct-measurement arm is no longer a replication on an inverse family: it is a direct measurement on the entry's own specified instrument, and the record's structure is now fully characterised).** (i) **The USDⓈ-M record is live and free.** Verified 2026-10-20 by direct listing of `https://s3-ap-northeast-1.amazonaws.com/data.binance.vision?prefix=data3/liquidationSnapshot/` (access 1, keyless, no registration): **311 symbol directories, 64,820 daily zips and 64,820 `.CHECKSUM` sidecars (129,640 objects)**, coverage **2023-06-25 to 2024-03-31 (281 days)**, with `BTCUSDT`, `ETHUSDT`, `SOLUSDT`, `DOGEUSDT`, `LINKUSDT` and `ADAUSDT` present for all 281 days. The documented path `data/futures/um/daily/liquidationSnapshot/` now lists **zero** objects and its objects return HTTP 404, while the empty prefix itself is still enumerable, so the withdrawal from the documented path is confirmed and is not a deletion. Three files were verified end to end: `BTCUSDT-liquidationSnapshot-2023-08-01.zip` (13,928 bytes; SHA-256 `45bd7d03dbf002c7382652938252b777cedca66292d11aea48d0dfdc372301be`, equal to the published sidecar; 1,386 data rows = exactly 2 x 693 unique rows), `ETHUSDT-…-2024-03-31.zip` (12,346 bytes; 1,158 rows = 2 x 579) and `BTCUSDT-…-2024-03-31.zip` (15,296 bytes; 1,544 rows = 2 x 772). **Every object carries `LastModified` 2025-04-16**, so this is a single bulk relocation and not a series of revisions, and every sampled file's SHA-256 equals its published sidecar, so the bytes are verifiable and citable. (ii) **The entry's registered statistic is now directly measurable on the entry's own instrument, and the construction question the eleventh to fourteenth cycles treated as open is closed**: "sell liquidations as a percentage of opening open interest" can be computed for the USDⓈ-M family over 281 days at per-event resolution, and for the COIN-M family over a further 199 days to 2024-10-14. (iii) **The USDⓈ-M family needs no multiplier and carries no multiplier hazard.** The USDⓈ-M `original_quantity` is in the **base asset**, not in contracts: `BTCUSDT-liquidationSnapshot-2023-08-01` contains a SELL of `0.036` BTC at `29,105.23`, and the venue's own USDⓈ-M `metrics` file for the same day gives `sum_open_interest` 108,876.86 BTC against `sum_open_interest_value` 3,181,469,038 USDT, an implied price of 29,220.80 consistent with the day's kline. Notional is therefore `quantity x price` in USDT directly and the open-interest denominator is `sum_open_interest_value`, so the statistic is **multiplier-free and contract-unit-free on this family**; the COIN-M multiplier rule (10 USD per contract for every non-BTC symbol and 100 USD for every BTC symbol) applies to the inverse family only. (iv) **The USDⓈ-M panel is nearly complete and the COIN-M panel is not, and the two families must not be pooled without a declared treatment.** The USDⓈ-M panel spans 66,348 symbol-days against 64,820 files, i.e. **1,528 absent symbol-days (2.30%)**, with 220 symbols carrying at least one internal gap and the worst single symbol `USDCUSDT` missing 127 of its 279 days; the COIN-M panel is 9,652 of 26,834 symbol-days absent (**35.97%**). (v) **The open-interest proxy has now been decomposed, and the decomposition is adverse.** On 2024-08-05 the recorded forced liquidations across the 36 live contracts (3,612,966 SELL and 288,128 BUY contracts) explain only **12.1%** of the 32,265,624-contract cross-sectional open-interest reduction; the median contract's forced liquidations are 11.11% of its own net open-interest reduction (min 3.09%, max 177.83%). The residual is either *voluntary* closing or liquidations the file does not capture, and both readings are adverse, because from the eleventh cycle onward the proxy has stood in for "the forced unwinding" and it is now measured to be **mostly not forced unwinding**. The same figure is 24.8% on 2024-08-04 and 4.9% on 2024-08-06. (vi) **The per-event path carries no information beyond the daily aggregate, and the catalogue must not read cascade sequencing off this record.** On 2024-08-05 the first forced liquidation in all 36 contracts falls inside a **55.3-minute window** (00:02:30 to 00:57:49 UTC) and 50% of the day's forced SELL contracts are recorded in the first 57 minutes, against 910.3 minutes (2024-08-04) and 1,242.3 minutes (2024-08-06) for the same measure on the adjacent days; and the rank order of first liquidation is *negatively* correlated with contract size on all three days (Spearman -0.50, -0.58, -0.59 against opening open interest; -0.42, -0.73, -0.59 against the day's forced SELL contracts). That is the signature of an exponential-arrival process in which the first event is a function of the event rate, so the "path" is a **liquidity ordering**, not a propagation order. (vii) **The registered prediction, the mandatory controls, the trigger definition, the horizons and the null outcome remain unchanged**, the instrument-substitution caveat is now narrower (the direct arm can be run on the specified instrument for 281 days and on the inverse family for a further 199 days), and no empirical validation was performed in this repository.
15. Evidence notes with sources and dates: **Tenth-cycle sources (2026-10-20).** Christin, Nicolas, Routledge, Bryan R., Soska, Kyle and Zetlin-Jones, Ariel, "The Crypto Carry Trade", CMU working paper (August 2023; free PDF at https://gerbil.life/papers/CarryTrade.v1.0.pdf; conference abstract at https://westernfinance-portal.org/viewpaper?n=853216; a public summary of the paper by its author appears as a comment letter in the CFTC docket S7-2026-21, https://www.sec.gov/comments/S7-2026-21/s7202621-1011079-3235289.pdf), access 1 for the free PDF and the SEC-hosted letter, access 2 for the conference abstract page, which renders as an unparseable PDF to automated fetch - *establishes* that a strategy shorting perpetual futures and hedging with spot earned high realised returns across a broad set of contracts on Binance for 18 cryptocurrencies, on both Tether- and coin-denominated contracts; that funding is typically positive, so longs pay shorts most of the time; and that the demand to hold long perpetual exposure has been strong and persistent, which is the premise half of this entry's mechanism; the authorised public summary also states that liquidation dynamics amplify price movements and that this "supports concern about this mechanism" while explicitly stating that it "does not establish that perpetual futures are the sole or primary cause of sharp cryptocurrency price movements"; *does not establish* any crash-conditionality claim, any post-July-2024 result, or any cost-adjusted edge - the reported Sharpe figures are pre-cost and are not relied on here - and the working paper is unreviewed. martex-quant ledger H22 "Crash-Day Alt Bounce (strategy-grade)" (verdict 2026-07-12; free GitHub raw, access 1 for the document; unreviewed preregistered tier): with zero tunable parameters and the BTC down-day threshold fixed in an earlier registration, an equal-weight basket of all listed alts held for exactly one day after each BTC crash day (a BTC move worse than −3%) earns a mean net held-day return of +0.441% (95% block-bootstrap CI [+0.168%, +0.742%]) over 727 held days within 3,248 days and 11,084 fills, annualised net +32.1%, with a source-reported Sharpe of 0.89 and MDD −47.8%, flat on roughly 78% of days, and being paper-traded rather than deployed; the source's performance figures are reported here for identification of the result and are not relied on as evidence of an edge; *establishes* that a crash state in the perp era carries exploitable cross-sectional reversal structure, so a signal that predicts crash frequency may also predict a positive forward return; *does not establish* anything about perp basis or funding. martex-quant ledger H13 "Single-Day Shock Persistence" (verdict 2026-07-12; access 1 for the document): on a pooled 8-symbol daily panel over 23,209 symbol-days, extreme up shocks (today's return at least two trailing-30-day volatilities) are followed by +3.54% extra over the next 7 days (CI [+1.25%, +5.99%], n = 924), a clear continuation signal, while the moderate up, moderate down and extreme down buckets are noise, the document noting that this is the fourth independent test in that ledger where continuation beats reversal in crypto; *establishes* that a crypto price-shock asymmetry is estimable with exactly the machinery this catalogue requires (block bootstrap with the cross-section intact within date blocks) and that the direction after extreme up shocks is continuation; *does not establish* any funding-extreme result, and it bears on the deferred conditional funding-extreme family rather than on this entry. CFTC comment letter S7-2026-21 (21 August 2026, https://www.sec.gov/comments/S7-2026-21/s7202621-1011079-3235289.pdf, access 1): the author of "The Crypto Carry Trade" summarising that paper for the regulator, stating that liquidation dynamics matter because they can amplify price movements and that perpetual contracts are economically similar to leveraged retail commodity contracts; *establishes* that the carry-trade and forced-liquidation transmission channel is on the public regulatory record; *does not establish* any predictive claim, and a comment letter is not a peer-reviewed paper. Binance public data archive, re-verified 2026-10-20 (access 1, fetched directly) **Eleventh-cycle sources (2026-10-20).** Faulkner, Paul, "Forced Liquidation Cascades in Unregulated Perpetual Futures Markets: Evidence from $176.6 Billion in Forced Position Exits", Zenodo record 19975149, doi 10.5281/zenodo.19975149 (2026-05-02, CC-BY-4.0, access 1, free PDF and XLSX, inspected 2026-10-20) - *establishes* that a free, dated, 365-day, eleven-venue panel of forced-exit notionals exists with daily funding readings and a full-year OHLC panel, assembled from CoinGlass liquidation history and CoinGecko spot data, and states that no proxy is used in any quantitative claim; *does not establish* any crash-conditionality claim, any per-contract or directional decomposition, or any reproducibility of the underlying aggregator history, and it is a single-author non-peer-reviewed working paper. "Who Loses and Why? Market Microstructure of Perpetual Futures", ACM International Conference on Advances in Financial Technologies 2026, extended abstract doi 10.4230/LIPIcs.AFT.2026.36 (2026-09-24; full version SSRN 7270041; access 1 for the extended abstract) - *establishes* that Hyperliquid's complete on-chain record of orders and trades from 2025-03-27 to 2026-06-30 for its ten most active contracts observes forced liquidations at millisecond resolution with trade-time position size and realised P&L, and that front-end (retail) traders account for 96.0% of three-group liquidation volume (97.6% excluding the October 10, 2025 episode) while forced liquidations impose a $344 million loss on front-end traders against which auto-deleveraging returns $118 million; *does not establish* any predictive claim about perp basis or crash conditionality, and the extended abstract is a summary. Lim, Boon Chuan, "Two-Regime Liquidity Recovery After a Perpetual Futures Liquidation Cascade: Evidence from Hyperliquid and the October 10, 2025 Event", Journal of FinTech and Digital Assets 1(2):54-68, doi 10.34900/jfda.v1i2.1395 (2026-09-01, open access, MIT replication code on GitHub) - *establishes* the two-regime post-cascade liquidity recovery (spreads back to within 1.1-1.2x pre-event within 23 days; inside-quote depth ending at 66-79% of pre-event and still contracting), and that spread-only metrics understate post-event liquidity impairment; *does not establish* any return effect. Sepper, Otar, "Slippage-at-Risk (SaR): A Forward-Looking Liquidity Risk Framework for Perpetual Futures Exchanges", arXiv:2603.09164 (2026-03-10, access 1) - *establishes* a forward-looking liquidity-risk framework on Hyperliquid order-book data with a stated mechanism (liquidity withdrawal, then SaR increases, then cascade) and a claimed leading-indicator property; *does not establish* any return effect, and it is a single-author preprint. Ali, Zeeshan, "Anatomy of the Oct 10-11, 2025 Crypto Liquidation Cascade: Macroeconomic Triggers, Market Microstructure, and Systemic Risk Lessons", SSRN 5611392 (2025-10-15, access 2 - SSRN pages return 403 to automated fetch, abstract read via search index) - *establishes* that the October 10-11, 2025 cascade erased $19 billion of open interest within 36 hours, was triggered by an exogenous tariff announcement, and is documented from hourly data on ten major cryptocurrencies from Binance cross-validated with Bybit and CoinGlass, with high volatility persistence and cross-asset contagion; *does not establish* any precursor signal. Shah, Vihan, "Regime-Dependent Contagion in DeFi Liquidations: Disentangling Self-Excitation from Common Price Shocks", SSRN 7087338 (2026-07-08, access 2) - *establishes* that on 18,408 ETH-collateralised Aave V3 liquidations (2023-01 to 2026-06) the self-excitation parameter is regime-dependent, with stress defined ex ante by trailing 24-hour ETH drawdowns above 7%; *does not establish* anything about perpetuals, and it is a single-author preprint. Binance public data archive, daily/metrics/ header verified 2026-10-20 (access 1, fetched directly) - *establishes* that the metrics file carries both sum_open_interest (contract count) and sum_open_interest_value (USD notional), so the v1.5 proxy could be re-specified on the price-invariant series at no cost; *does not establish* anything about returns. Binance REST endpoints, checked 2026-10-20 (data access 1, but HTTP 451 from this environment on api, api1, api2, api3 and fapi) - an environment limitation, not a data determination. **No dimension moves on the strength of these sources**: they bear on the measurement of the tail statistic and on who bears the forced-unwinding cost, not on the registered crash-conditionality prediction, so the falsification-handling rule remains unengaged and the entry is neither promoted nor demoted.  - see item 10 for the corrections to the ninth cycle's description of the construction, which bind this entry's own mandatory open-interest baseline as well as the universe construction: the kline tree has an interval subdirectory, a delisted contract's file series ends at the delisting date while an inactive-but-not-delisted contract's series continues with zero-volume files, and the archive publishes an exhaustive dated update log (the single kline event, 2022-08-08, replaced 73,234 files across 1,659 spot symbols, with original and replacement checksums). **No dimension moves on the strength of these sources**: they bear on the mechanism's premise and on competing explanations, not on the registered crash-conditionality prediction, which remains untested, so the falsification-handling rule remains unengaged and the entry is neither promoted nor demoted. **Ninth-cycle sources (2026-10-20).** Garcia Seuma, "Measuring the engine of a liquidation cascade: subcritical branching inside a first-order transition", arXiv:2608.03616 (5 August 2026, access 1, arXiv open) - the October 2025 record cascade ran deeply subcritical and abrupt, with the in-cascade signature in the liquidity sector (price-impact spikes 1.2-3.5x regressed and 3.2-9.1x quoted; open interest clearing 25-70%, -24.6% for Binance BTC and -45% to -70% on Hyperliquid), and with the branching model failing as an account of the pre-cascade state on both of its falsifiable predictions at simulated power of at least 0.9; *establishes* that a crypto liquidation cascade's observable footprint is an open-interest clearing plus an impact spike and that no pre-cascade state variable was shown to anticipate the largest recorded event; *does not establish* any crash-conditionality claim about perp basis or funding; single-author arXiv preprint, not peer-reviewed, and no test was run here. Kitron and Wengrowicz, "Short-horizon mean reversion in cryptocurrency markets: a matched cross-market measurement", arXiv:2608.21888 (22 August 2026, access 1, with public replication code and frozen results) - 90% of 183 Binance pairs carry significant 15-minute directional reversal under one matched, strictly out-of-sample walk-forward protocol, against 2.7% of 187 US stocks and ETFs; the reversal concentrates after moves driven by aggressive taker flow and grows with flow intensity while the order-book depth a move consumes conditions nothing; the gross edge peaks near 1.3 bp per trade against a 5 bp round-trip cost; and Appendix F reports that conditioning on the perpetual basis, funding rate, funding clock, volatility and session is flat in every case; *establishes* that funding and basis conditioning adds nothing where it has been tested on a wide crypto panel, and that a robustly detected reversal signal can still be cost-killed at retail scale; *does not establish* any crash-conditionality claim. Binance public data archive, https://data.binance.vision (access 1, fetched directly 2026-10-20) - 998 distinct USD-M perpetual symbols in the daily kline tree including delisted contracts, dated daily files per symbol per calendar day, terminal files for ceased contracts carrying volume=0 and trade count=0 with a frozen close, 5-minute open interest under data/futures/um/daily/metrics/ from 2020-09-01 to the present, monthly funding-rate files from 2020-01, daily premium-index and mark-price klines from 2019-12, daily order-book depth from 2023-01-01, and a parallel spot kline tree from 2017-08; *establishes* the free-data inputs and the dated delisting record recorded in item 10; *does not establish* anything about returns, and no performance statistic was computed from these files in this repository. Original source list continues: BIS Working Paper 1087, "Crypto carry", free PDF https://www.bis.org/publ/work1087.pdf (April 2023, revised October 2025), checked 2026-10-20 - high carry predicts spot crashes and rising sell liquidations; published version: Schmeling, Schrimpf & Todorov (2026), "Crypto Carry", Management Science, doi 10.1287/mnsc.2024.05069, published online 6 May 2026 - abstract accessible free (https://pubsonline.informs.org/doi/abs/10.1287/mnsc.2024.05069), full text access classification 2; the authors' own summary column (CEPR VoxEU, 22 Dec 2025) states the sample runs March 2019 to July 2024 for OKEx with data from Skew and Coinmetrics, and the published data description states daily data March 2019 to July 2024 (checked 2026-10-20). This supersedes the earlier characterisation in cycles 1-3 that the sample ended January 2022: that was true of the March 2023 working-paper version, and the "post-2022 validity gap" is now materially narrower (the published sample includes 2022-2024) while a new gap opens after July 2024. He, Manela, Ross & von Wachter, "Fundamentals of Perpetual Futures", arXiv:2212.06888 (v7, 17 Sep 2026), free - no-arbitrage benchmark for perps; deviations from it are larger than in FX, co-move across currencies and diminish over time; past returns explain more than half of the futures-spot gap in a time-series regression, indicating positive feedback/momentum behaviour (v5/v6 text). Gornall, Rinaldi & Xiao (2025), "Perpetual Futures and Basis Risk: Evidence from Cryptocurrency", SSRN 5036933 (rev. 19 May 2025) and AEA 2026 program - perps dominate volume, enhance liquidity, reduce extreme price dislocations, arbitrage drawdowns on perps mean-revert quickly. Garcia Seuma (2026), arXiv:2607.27070, free - seven BTC perpetual liquidation cascades 2022-2025; out-of-sample inversion; exogenous shocks show no precursor. Cao, Zhai & Luo (2024), "Anatomy of cryptocurrency perpetual futures returns", Journal of Futures Markets - 134 return predictors, basis and price-volume factors explain all 48 significant strategies (abstract free, full text access unverified). Chi (2023), doi 10.1002/fut.22425 - basis strongest cross-sectional factor (abstract only). Labels: published findings and mechanistic reasoning are separated from unverified assumptions (the +20% threshold, the sustained-period rule, and the mapping of published dated-futures results onto retail perps are assumptions, not evidence). **Added in v1.2 (all checked 2026-10-20):** martex-quant public research ledger, "Hypothesis 10 — Spot-vs-Perp Basis as a State Variable" (docs/hypotheses/10-spot-perp-basis.md, verdict 2026-07-11), free GitHub raw, access 1 for the document; preregistered before the test ran with fixed thresholds (trailing-90-day percentile of daily perp-vs-spot basis, LOW <= 10th, HIGH >= 90th), 17,899 symbol-days of Binance USDT-margined perpetual and spot data, verdict FAIL: 7-day LOW-minus-HIGH = -2.61%, 95% block-bootstrap CI [-4.88%, -0.12%], 2/8 symbols positive, high-premium days followed by +4.20% vs +1.59% at 7 days and +14.8% vs +6.1% at 30 days; establishes a direct, dated, preregistered contradiction of the mean-return form of this entry's prediction on retail perpetuals, covering December 2019 to July 2026, and does not test crash conditionality; not peer-reviewed, so treated as a new and relatively strong evidence tier (preregistered external kill test) rather than as peer-reviewed evidence. martex-quant "Hypothesis 62 — Delta-neutral Carry" and "Hypothesis 63 — Funding-conditional Carry" and the H72 point-in-time restatement, free GitHub raw, access 1 for the documents; establishes that the cash-and-carry premium on Binance USDT perps was historically real (reported Sharpe 2.29 always-on and 6.00 with a funding filter, the latter with a point-in-time-universe restatement of 4.83 and CAGR +2.35%) but is effectively unpaid in the current regime (+0.08%/yr always-on and +0.15%/yr filtered over the most recent 365 days) and that the point-in-time universe correction removes roughly half the headline CAGR; establishes the regime collapse of the carry premium, and does not establish any crash-prediction result. Ruan and Streltsov, "Perpetual Futures Contracts and Cryptocurrency Market Quality" (CFTC comment letter, 21 May 2025), free PDF, access 1; establishes that perpetual derivatives trade with a much smaller basis to spot because of the funding mechanism, and that the perpetual-to-spot volume ratio has held near 3-4x over five years; establishes a structural reason the perp-era basis distribution differs from the dated-futures sample, and establishes no return-predictive claim. "Perpetual futures as predictors of Bitcoin volatility", Journal of Financial Markets (2026), PII S0927539826000939, abstract freely readable via search index, publisher page returned HTTP 403 to automated fetch on 2026-10-20, access 2; establishes that funding-rate-based predictors materially improve forecasts of Bitcoin spot volatility and remain significant after controlling for fixed-maturity futures volatility, and interprets funding as capturing imbalanced leveraged positions; establishes volatility predictability, not return predictability. Ackerer, Hugonnier and Jermann, "Perpetual Futures Pricing", NBER Working Paper 32936 (3 September 2024), free PDF, access 1; establishes no-arbitrage pricing of perpetual futures and that 75% of Bitcoin futures daily volume and 94% of open interest in H1 2023 were perpetual; establishes the instrument share, and no return claim. Grobys and Shahzad, "Cryptocurrency Momentum: Is It an Illusion?", International Journal of Finance and Economics 31(2):2180-2193 (April 2026), abstract freely readable via publisher and RePEc indexes, access 2; establishes that crypto momentum strategies' realised variances follow power laws such that the population mean and variance are statistically not defined under block bootstrap, so performance metrics using variance as an input are uninformative; this is the source of the heavy-tail inference requirement in item 14 and is a design constraint, not evidence for or against CRYPTO-EDGE-001. Label update: the published evidence is peer-reviewed but dated-futures and pre-July-2024; the perp-era contradiction is preregistered and dated but not peer-reviewed; the carry-premium collapse is preregistered and dated but not peer-reviewed. **Added in v1.3 (all checked 2026-10-20):** Franz, Friedrich-Carl and Schmeling, Maik, 'Crypto Carry' (27 January 2021), SSRN 3774118, abstract freely readable via the SSRN abstract page and the search index, access 2; establishes that carry measured as the funding rate of BTC/USD perpetual swaps resembles components of a fear-and-greed index and of past returns, and that carry in perpetual swaps *positively* predicts returns in the time series of BTC/USD and in a cross-section of 51 cryptocurrencies; establishes a documented, author-side, instrument-specific sign split in the carry signal (dated-futures basis predicts crashes, perpetual funding positively predicts returns); establishes no crash or tail claim of any kind. The same 2021 working-paper programme is described in a public replication note (ipozdeev/crypto-fx-strategies, free, access 1 for the notebook) as documenting funding-rate return predictability across 51 coins listed on Huobi with a reported long-short Sharpe of 1.21; establishes only that the funding-based result circulated early and independently of the dated-futures work; the reported Sharpe is pre-cost and is not relied on here. The published carry-crash paper's own crash-risk section (BIS Working Paper 1087 section 3.4 and tables 7-8, free PDF, access 1) is mined for the first time in this cycle: it establishes that carry is strongly negatively related to implied skewness over the next one to three months, that the relationship with realized skew is negative and significant for OKEx, that a rise in carry by 10% predicts a 44% increase in total sell liquidations relative to total open interest over the next month, that carry predicts sell but not long liquidations, and that option measures alone explain 42-44% of the variation in carry; establishes direct published support for the *tail* form of the prediction on dated futures; establishes no perpetual-era result and no post-July-2024 sample. Tran, Duc Trong, Nguyen, Kim Cuc Thi, Le, Anh Nam and Pham, Ha Thu Thi (2026), 'Systemic risk from financial leverage in digital asset markets', Studies in Economics and Finance, doi 10.1108/SEF-04-2026-0367, abstract freely readable via the publisher page, access 2; provides daily and hourly Binance USDT-margined perpetual data for 10 major cryptocurrencies over 2023-2024, a panel logit early-warning model in which Bitcoin open-interest changes are the strongest crash predictor (odds ratio 1.48, out-of-sample AUROC 0.677), a generalized forecast-error variance decomposition in which Ethereum is the dominant risk transmitter (total connectedness 79.8%), and a cascade event study in which liquidations propagate across 6 of 10 assets within two hours; establishes that a derivatives-positioning variable carries genuine crash-predictive information in crypto perpetuals and that open interest is the strongest documented candidate; establishes no basis effect and no coverage of the post-July-2024 window. Lim, Boon Chuan (2026), 'Anatomy of a Crypto Cascade: Minute-Level Evidence from the October 2025 Crash', SSRN 6579278, abstract freely readable via the SSRN abstract page and the search index, access 2; provides that Bitcoin fell 12.6% in ten minutes on Binance on 10 October 2025, that futures led the crash with the basis swinging $1,367 from -$534 to +$833 in eight minutes, that volume surged 22x baseline seven minutes before the price trough, that SOL showed a 13.4pp futures-spot divergence and that the mark price used for liquidation triggers undershot both spot and futures, creating a reflexive loop absent in traditional equity markets; establishes the 2025 cascade mechanism and that the basis is violently unstable at the crash instant, which cuts against a level-based precursor; establishes no forward-return prediction. Amberdata (11 March 2026), 'The Carry Trade That Broke: How Basis Compression Triggered the Cascade', https://blog.amberdata.io/the-carry-trade-that-broke-how-basis-compression-triggered-the-cascade, freely readable, access 2, vendor-published and marketing-adjacent; provides that the 30-day basis compressed from 6.9% to 4.5% during the October cascade, that closing the carry trade mechanically adds selling pressure, and that the current 30-day basis is about 5.4% annualized; establishes the carry-unwind amplification channel; treated as a mechanism source rather than as evidence. CoinDesk (3 August 2026), 'The bitcoin futures yield collapse: Once over 20%, now behind Treasury notes', https://www.coindesk.com/markets/2026/08/03/the-bitcoin-futures-yield-collapse-once-over-20-now-less-than-treasury-notes, freely readable, access 2, news reporting citing Glassnode and Coinglass; provides that the three-month Bitcoin basis has paid less than the two-year Treasury for 157 consecutive days since February 2026 and that the cash-and-carry trade now returns about 3% against a 3.8% two-year Treasury yield; establishes the current-regime compression of the published trigger state; establishes no predictive claim. Gate Research (11 September 2026), 'Can Deleveraging Predict a Rebound? A Time-Series Analysis of BTC Price, OI, Funding, and Liquidation Volume', https://www.gate.com/announcements/article/101698, freely readable, access 2, exchange-published and marketing-adjacent; provides that average 7-day and 14-day returns after sharp open-interest declines were -0.68% and -0.91%, and -1.25% and -2.50% after liquidation spikes, that average forward returns after funding turned negative were positive at every horizon from 1 to 14 days but not significant once momentum, volatility and volume were controlled, and that a 'pressure release plus price confirmation' sequence is what improves rebound odds; establishes that extreme positioning states do not by themselves mark bottoms; treated as practitioner material. Pindza, Edson and Mashele, Hopolang Phillip (2026), 'Delayed Marks, Funding Memory, and Forecasting Liquidation-Tail Risk in Crypto Perpetual Futures', MDPI Forecasting 8(5):76, doi 10.3390/forecast8050076, abstract freely readable via the publisher page, access 2; provides a first-passage density-forecasting framework that forecasts the probability of an isolated-margin boundary breach conditional on a delayed or smoothed mark and on funding as a persistent collateral drain, together with a rolling comparison of model-consistent forecasts against historical simulation on public price and funding paths; establishes that the funding-conditioned tail-risk question is formally identifiable from public data and that latency-to-margin ratio is the governing quantity; establishes no return or crash-timing claim. Label update (v1.3): the published evidence for the *tail* form of the prediction is peer-reviewed but dated-futures and pre-July-2024; the instrument-specific sign instability is peer-review-track but unpublished and authored by two of the same authors; the perpetual-era external tests are unreviewed (SSRN working papers, one low-tier journal, one exchange study, one practitioner blog) and disagree with each other on the negative tail; the current-regime compression of the trigger state is documented in vendor and press reporting.
**Thirteenth-cycle sources (2026-10-20).**
(a) **Binance public data archive, COIN-M `liquidationSnapshot`** (https://data.binance.vision, access 1, no key) — the dataset described in item 10(b); *establishes* that a free, per-contract, per-event, direction-attributed forced-liquidation record exists on Binance for the inverse family over 2023-06-25 to 2024-10-14, and *does not establish* anything about the entry's registered prediction, about the USDⓈ-M family, or about any period after 2024-10-14.
(b) **Binance, COIN-Margined Futures contract specifications** (https://www.binance.com/en/blog/futures/three-benefits-of-coinmargined-perpetual-futures-that-you-need-to-know-421499824684900864 and the COIN-M contract-specification FAQ; access 1, primary venue document, admissible as evidence about venue mechanics) — *establishes* "Each COIN-margined contract represents 100 USD" and "Contract Multiplier 100 USD per contract" for the COIN-M perpetual, and that the contract size for COIN-M quarterly delivery contracts is a different figure again; *does not establish* the multiplier for any symbol not individually checked, which is why the rule is "take the multiplier from the venue's specification for that contract family" rather than "multiply by 100".
(c) **Binance CMS delisting-announcement catalog** (https://www.binance.com/bapi/composite/v1/public/cms/article/catalog/list/query?catalogId=161&pageNo=N&pageSize=20; access 1, HTTP 200, no key) — re-verified live: **442 articles** across 23 pages, including perpetual-contract notices dated as late as **2026-03-25** ("Binance Futures Will Delist COIN-M APTUSD and OPUSD Perpetual Contracts (2026-03-25)"). *Establishes* that individual COIN-M perpetuals continued to be delisted through 2026 and therefore that the COIN-M liquidation dataset's terminal date is a **publisher-side** event rather than an instrument-side one; *does not establish* why the publisher stopped the dataset.
(d) **A single-day construction description, not a performance statistic** (2024-08-05, the yen-carry unwind cascade; read to verify the *construction* of the registered statistic and the behaviour of the proxy, exactly as the eleventh cycle read single-day klines to verify construction facts): BTCUSD_PERP recorded 952 unique events, of which **710 were SELL (liquidated longs) totalling 322,865 contracts** and 242 were BUY (liquidated shorts) totalling 50,609 contracts; the contract-count open interest fell from 16,437,041 to 14,195,452 (**-13.6%**, minimum 14,193,072), so the forced sell flow is **1.96% of the opening open interest** and **14.4% of the open-interest reduction**. ETHUSD_PERP recorded **2,147,567 SELL contracts = 2.35% of the opening open interest** against a **-23.3%** open-interest reduction (**10.1%** of the reduction). On the two adjacent days the same numbers are BTCUSD_PERP 0.482% (2024-08-04, open interest -4.0%) and 0.247% (2024-08-06, -1.1%), and ETHUSD_PERP 0.411% (-1.6%) and 0.136% (-4.1%). *Establishes* that the direct statistic separates the cascade day from adjacent days by roughly 4-5x, and that **the open-interest-clearing proxy registers a state change larger than the forced flow that accounts for it** — so the proxy is a direction-and-severity indicator, not a measure of the flow, and the ratio must be reported. **Fourteenth-cycle correction, marked superseded in place: the thirteenth cycle stated the ratio as "10-14x larger", which is a mis-reading of the two percentages it had just computed.** The flow is 14.4% (BTCUSD_PERP) and 10.1% (ETHUSD_PERP) of the open-interest reduction, so the reduction is **6.9x** and **9.9x** the flow, not 10-14x; the two figures are the percentages, and their reciprocals are 6.9 and 9.9. The *direction* of the conclusion is unchanged — the proxy overstates the flow, and the ratio must be reported — but the magnitude of the overstatement is smaller than the record said, and a validator preregistering a ratio to beat would have anchored on a wrong number. *Does not establish* any predictive relationship between the basis state and any of these quantities, and no return, Sharpe or drawdown was computed for any strategy.
(e) **Sepper (2026), "Slippage-at-Risk (SaR): A Forward-Looking Liquidity Risk Framework for Perpetual Futures Exchanges", arXiv:2603.09164 (submitted 10 March 2026, 32 pages)** — free preprint, access 1. *Establishes* that a forward-looking liquidation-execution-risk metric can be built from current order-book microstructure, with three metrics (a cross-sectional slippage quantile SaR(alpha), the expected tail slippage ESaR(alpha) and the aggregate dollar-denominated tail slippage TSaR(alpha)), a maker-concentration adjustment, a mapping to capital requirements drawing on Chitra et al. (2025) on auto-deleveraging and insurance-fund design, and an empirical application to **Hyperliquid** order-book data including the 10 October 2025 cascade. *Does not establish* that the metric works on Binance, on USDⓈ-M, or on any window other than the paper's own; its venue-native data is not free. *Bears on* this entry as the leading candidate for replacing the retrospective clearing proxy with a prospective one, and the free `bookDepth` tree is the Binance substitute for its order-book input, with the exception of the concentration adjustment, which a bucketed depth ladder cannot support.

**Fourteenth-cycle sources (2026-10-20).**
(a) **Binance public data repository, issue tracker, three issues and the repository's own history** (https://github.com/binance/binance-public-data — issues #361 "liquidationSnapshot data missing" (opened 11 October 2024, answered by a repository collaborator on **15 October 2024**: "this data is no longer provided", closed the same day); #337 "USDT-M liquidationSnapshot data missing after 2024-03-31" (opened 14 June 2024, closed 6 October 2026 by a collaborator stating "liquidationSnapshot is no longer provided on data.binance.vision. **USD-M data has been removed**, and the **last COIN-M file is 2024-10-14**"; the 14 June 2024 comment on it also reports, from the community, "why is each row double?"); #420 "Liquidation Data Endpoint `/fapi/v1/forceOrders` and Historical Files Unavailable" (opened 16 May 2025, still open); and the repository's `updates/` directory and README Updates table, both of which contain only 2022 artefacts). Access 1, no key, HTTP 200, read live on 2026-10-20. **Primary venue documents about data availability.** *Establishes* that the liquidation-snapshot product was **discontinued by the publisher on 2024-10-15** — the same day the last file was written, at the normal one-day publication lag — so the 2024-10-14 end of the record is a **publisher-side product decision** and not a deletion, an instrument-life effect, or an unexplained window; that the **USDⓈ-M liquidation record existed through 2024-03-31 and has been removed from the archive**; that the **exact-two row duplication was publicly visible in the publisher's own repository from 14 June 2024** and has no staff explanation; and that the published update log does not cover futures paths and does not cover the 2026-04-16 backfill. *Does not establish* anything about the entry's registered prediction, about any reason for the discontinuation beyond the dated statement, or about whether a mirror of the withdrawn USDⓈ-M files exists.
(b) **Binance, "How to Download Historical Market Data on Binance?"** (venue support FAQ, https://www.binance.com/en/support/faq/how-to-download-historical-market-data-on-binance-5810ae42176b4770b880ce1f14932262 — the page renders under the venue's WAF and was read through a search index on 2026-10-20, so it is access 2 for the page itself and access 1 for the text it carries). *Establishes* that the venue's own documentation lists a "Liquidation Snapshot" product with the date range **2023-06-25 to 2024-03-31**, i.e. the venue still documents the USDⓈ-M product and its window while the files themselves are gone. *Does not establish* the COIN-M window, which the bucket itself establishes.
(c) **Binance public data archive, COIN-M `liquidationSnapshot` tree, re-enumerated and re-read on 2026-10-20** (https://data.binance.vision, access 1, marker-paged, 35 listing pages, 34,364 keys). *Establishes*, beyond the thirteenth cycle's counts: **9,652 of 26,834 symbol-days absent (36.0%)**; **110 of 118 symbols with internal gaps**; per-symbol absence rates from **0.8% (ETHUSD_PERP)** to **62.2% (ICXUSD_PERP)**; **219 dates with at least 10 live perps simultaneously absent**, the worst being **2023-09-09 and 2023-09-25 at 39 of 41 live perps (95.1%)**; **49 files re-uploaded on 2026-04-16** for the five dates 2023-09-25, 2023-11-20, 2024-04-18, 2024-06-11 and 2024-06-12; a publication-lag distribution of one day for 16,445 files, two days for 664, and 49 outliers; and the **per-symbol multiplier of 100 USD for the BTCUSD contracts and 10 USD for the eight other symbols checked**, derived from the metrics-and-klines pair. *Does not establish* the cause of any individual absence, and the catalogue does not assert one.
(d) **A cross-sectional construction description of the record, 2024-08-04 and 2024-08-05** (all 45 COIN-M perpetuals, both days, read to describe the *construction* of the registered statistic and the behaviour of the proxy, exactly as the eleventh and thirteenth cycles read single days to verify construction facts; **no return, Sharpe, drawdown, hit rate or any other performance quantity was computed for any strategy**). On 2024-08-05, **36 of the 45 COIN-M perps have a file** and 9 do not; the forced sell flow as a share of the opening open interest runs from **0.26% (TRXUSD_PERP)** to **37.94% (ENSUSD_PERP)**, with the majors in the middle (**BTCUSD_PERP 1.96%, ETHUSD_PERP 2.35%, SOLUSD_PERP 1.99%, BNBUSD_PERP 0.93%**); the SELL share of forced contracts is **99-100% for most contracts** (BTCUSD_PERP 86.4%, SOLUSD_PERP 87.1%, ETHUSD_PERP 92.4%); the open-interest reduction is **negative for every one of the 36 contracts**, from -0.98% (AAVEUSD_PERP) to -40.81% (RUNEUSD_PERP), with most between -10% and -32%; the total forced selling across the 36 contracts is **3,612,966 contracts, $65.2m** at the per-symbol multipliers; and the open-interest-reduction-to-forced-flow ratio runs from **0.56 (ENSUSD_PERP) to 34.22 (DOTUSD_PERP)**, median 9.94, with 3 of 36 contracts in which the forced flow *exceeds* the net open-interest reduction. On 2024-08-04, 35 of 45 have a file and the same statistic runs from 0.045% (BCHUSD_PERP) to 1.31% (GMTUSD_PERP), with BTCUSD_PERP at 0.482%. *Establishes* that the record supports a genuine **cross-sectional** view of forced unwinds — the handoff's open question — and that the cross-section is extremely heterogeneous in both directions, so the aggregation rule and the proxy-to-flow ratio must both be preregistered per contract. *Does not establish* any predictive relationship between the basis state and any of these quantities.
(e) **An empirical check on whether the archive's absent days are missing data or no-event days, 2026-10-20.** Event counts on eight randomly sampled present days per symbol: BTCUSD_PERP 57-154 distinct events, ETHUSD_PERP 13-199, TRXUSD_PERP 1-50, AAVEUSD_PERP 1-2, XLMUSD_PERP 1-11. Absent days are systematically **quieter** than present days (pooled median absolute daily return 0.96% against 1.79% across six symbols, and only 8.1% of the top-quintile absolute-return days are absent against 36.0% overall), and absent days are over-represented at weekends (41.5% of Saturdays against 18.8% of Mondays). *Establishes* that a per-symbol absence is *coherent* with a no-event day, that a market-wide absence is *not* coherent with one (39 of 41 perps absent on 2023-09-09 is not a market without a single forced liquidation), and that the record's missingness therefore has **two distinct regimes** — a symbol-level one that is plausibly a genuine zero, and a date-level one that is a publisher-side failure — which the archive alone cannot fully separate. *Does not establish* the cause of any individual absence.
(f) **SonarX, "Hyperliquid Liquidations Data Explained" (14 September 2026), and Hydromancer's schema reference for the `fills` dataset** (https://www.sonarx.com/blog/hyperliquid-liquidations-data; https://docs.hydromancer.xyz/reservoir/schema-reference/fills; access 1 for the documentation). *Establishes* that **a second, independent venue's archive follows the same file-existence convention** — Hydromancer's documentation states that for a day with no events there is no file ("Files are only created when non-empty") — which is direct support for the symbol-level reading of the Binance absences and for the mandatory treatment that an absent file is a zero-event day *by convention* rather than by assumption; and that Hyperliquid's liquidation record has the same **double-count hazard** (a liquidation fill appears on both parties' rows, so totals must be deduplicated by the liquidated-user leg), which the catalogue already recorded. *Does not establish* anything about Binance.
16. Scores, total rank, confidence, explanation: Causal mechanism 4/5 -> 16; evidence quality 2/5 -> 6; robustness 1/5 -> 3; free-data accessibility 5/5 -> 15; net value after frictions 2/5 -> 6; falsifiability 4/5 -> 8; originality 2/5 -> 4. Total 58/100, down from 61 and from the arithmetically correct 67 (the 63 recorded in the sixth cycle is an arithmetic error: 16+12+6+15+6+8+4 = 67, and the 73 of v1.1 less the two documented v1.2 downgrades is also 67). Confidence: low, down from low-medium. Changes in v1.2: robustness 3/5 -> 2/5, because the perp-era transfer into the post-July-2024 window now has a direct preregistered counter-result on the mean-return form of the prediction, the perp-era trigger population is structurally compressed by the funding mechanism (Ruan and Streltsov 2025), crash precursors remain event-heterogeneous (Garcia Seuma 2026), and the documented evidence that perp funding predicts volatility rather than direction relocates the payoff away from the directional claim; net value after frictions 3/5 -> 2/5, because the cash-and-carry premium is documented to be effectively unpaid over the last 365 days and the directional expression is contradicted, leaving only a low-frequency tail overlay; falsifiability held at 4/5 despite the restructuring because the v1.2 tail statistic plus momentum- and volatility-matched baselines make the claim cleaner and easier to falsify, not vaguer; mechanism, evidence quality, free-data accessibility and originality are unchanged, because no new peer-reviewed evidence was obtained in either direction and the inputs remain fully free. The downgrade is deliberately confined to the two dimensions where the new evidence bites. Rank 1: still the strongest entry in the catalogue by 5 points, but now as a *conditional* hypothesis about crash risk rather than a directional trade, and its remaining value is largely as the mandatory comparator for the other entries. Changes in v1.3: evidence quality 4/5 -> 3/5, because the supporting evidence for the prediction *as registered on the target instrument* is weaker than the entry's headline 4/5 implied. The published peer-reviewed work does support the tail form of the claim (skew and sell-liquidations) but on dated futures; the same authors' own earlier perpetual-funding result has the opposite mean sign; no external test of the crash-conditionality form on retail perps was located; and four independent perpetual-era tests find no unconditional edge in funding or basis extremes. Robustness 2/5 -> 1/5, because four distinct robustness failures are now documented on top of those cycle 6 recorded: the sign is unstable *across instruments* (dated futures versus perps, author-side); the published sample ends in July 2024; the effect of any funding-extreme signal is documented to differ in sign *across assets* (BTC opposite to ETH, BNB, XRP and DOGE on a six-asset, three-venue sample); and crash precursors are documented to be event-heterogeneous with an exogenous-shock type that shows no precursor in any variable. Net value after frictions is held at 2/5, because the absence of a carry premium and the compression of the published trigger state reduce the *frequency* of the trade but do not remove the possibility that a tail overlay has value, and because the tail overlay's payoff comes from avoiding crash losses rather than from harvest. Falsifiability is held at 4/5, and is arguably improved, because the v1.3 tail statistics, the two-arm trigger structure and the open-interest control make the claim materially harder to satisfy by accident; the withdrawal of the unattainable absolute threshold also removes a specification that could only have failed. Mechanism is held at 4/5: the mechanism family (constrained arbitrage plus forced unwind) is documented on dated futures and the 2025 cascade documents the same family running through the carry unwind, but the entry's named trigger state is not the state the 2025 evidence identifies. Free-data accessibility is held at 5/5: the core inputs - funding, basis, open interest, liquidation notional, prices - are confirmed free or access 2, and the option surface is now confirmed free at access 1 for the current book and the volatility index. Originality is held at 2/5: the mechanism and the crash-conditionality form are both published, and the entry's remaining contribution is a free-data, perp-era, percentile-specified test design. Changes in v1.4 (ninth cycle): evidence quality 3/5 -> 2/5, and only that dimension moves. The reasoning is narrow and specific: the entry's supporting evidence is a single peer-reviewed carry-crash result estimated on dated futures to July 2024, and three independent 2026 sources now bear on the transferability of that evidence rather than on the claim itself. Garcia Seuma (arXiv:2608.03616) shows the October 2025 record cascade was first-order and deeply subcritical with no pre-cascade state variable shown to anticipate it, and its companion (arXiv:2607.27070) finds the crash signature absent in exactly the two sudden-news shocks and inverting between the two largest events; Kitron and Wengrowicz (arXiv:2608.21888) find funding and basis conditioning flat across 183 Binance pairs; and Memon, Anklesaria-Dalal and Mishra (IJFMR 4(4):86388) find that direct comparisons of conditional means between up-shock and down-shock events generally fail to establish statistically significant directional asymmetry, which is the statistical form any conditional crash or reversal claim takes. None of the three is a test of this entry's registered prediction, so the falsification-handling rule is not engaged and the entry is not rejected, and the entry's *mechanism* score is deliberately held because nothing new contradicts the market-structure description itself; but the quality and independence of the evidence that a perp-era positioning state predicts crash conditionality is now one step weaker than a 3/5. Causal mechanism, robustness, free-data accessibility, net value after frictions, falsifiability and originality are all held: robustness is already at its floor of 1/5, free-data accessibility is already at its ceiling of 5/5 and the ninth cycle confirmed the mandatory open-interest baseline at access 1 - an improvement in *implementation* feasibility, not in the entry's claim - and net value is already at 2/5 on the documented weakness of the carry premium in the 2025-2026 regime. Rank 1: still the strongest entry, by 3 points, but the case now rests on the *mechanism* and on the entry's role as the mandatory comparator for every other entry rather than on a live published result, and its confidence is low. **Tenth cycle: no change.** The total stays at 58/100 with every dimension held, and the reasons are recorded rather than assumed. The cycle produced no new evidence on the registered prediction — the crash-conditionality of elevated or compressing perp basis on retail perps after July 2024 is still untested externally and is still unengaged by the falsification-handling rule. What it produced was (i) a complete cycle-10 validation specification for the entry, including the free open-interest-clearing proxy for the tail statistic and the mandatory separation of the tail statistic from the mean statistic, and (ii) four new sources that bear on the mechanism's premise (Christin et al. document persistent leveraged-long demand and historically paid carry on Binance perps; the CFTC docket letter documents liquidation amplification as a transmission channel while denying that perps are the primary cause of sharp moves) and on two competing explanations for crash-state returns (H22's documented post-crash cross-sectional reversal, H13's documented continuation after extreme up shocks). Because the mechanism score is held only when nothing new contradicts the market-structure description itself, and because the new sources *support* the premise while *complicating* the attribution of any crash-state return pattern, mechanism is held at 4/5 and evidence quality is held at 2/5: the crash-conditionality claim itself has no new support, and the new sources are about the environment in which the claim would operate, not about the claim. Rank 1 is retained, now by 6 points rather than 3, and the reason for the wider margin is CRYPTO-EDGE-003's downgrade on adverse evidence and not any improvement in this entry. **Eleventh cycle: no change. The total stays at 58/100 with every dimension held, and the reason is recorded rather than assumed.** The current contributions are: causal mechanism 4/5 -> 16, evidence quality 2/5 -> 6, robustness 1/5 -> 3, free-data accessibility 5/5 -> 15, net value after frictions 2/5 -> 6, falsifiability 4/5 -> 8, originality 2/5 -> 4 (sum 58). The cycle's findings are, in the main, about how the tail statistic is measured, not about whether elevated or compressing perp basis predicts elevated crash conditionality on retail perps after July 2024; that prediction is still untested externally and the falsification-handling rule is still unengaged. The tenth cycle set the precedent that a new source which bears on the environment in which a claim would operate, rather than on the claim, holds the dimensions, and the eleventh cycle applies the same rule consistently. Specifically: evidence quality is held at 2/5 (no new support for the registered prediction; the new sources are about the tail statistic's measurement, the identity of the forced-unwinding agents, and the post-cascade liquidity surface); robustness is held at 1/5 (already at its floor; the new evidence is adverse-neutral on this entry's claim and does not move the sign question); free-data accessibility is held at 5/5 (already at its ceiling, and the new determinations improve what is computable without changing the accessibility class); net value after frictions is held at 2/5 (Lim's two-regime liquidity result is a documented cost headwind in precisely the entry's payoff window, which argues for a lower score, but the score is a judgement about the entry's net value and the entry's payoff is a low-frequency tail overlay whose frequency is already near zero; the change is recorded in item 13 rather than scored, and a reader who prefers to score it should treat 2/5 as generous); falsifiability is held at 4/5 (the v1.6 specification is materially stronger because the proxy's units defect is fixed and the proxy's fidelity is now a mandatory, reportable validation step against two free forced-liquidation records, but a better document is not new evidence); causal mechanism is held at 4/5 (the mechanism is sharpened - forced liquidations are now documented to be overwhelmingly a front-end retail phenomenon at 96% of liquidation volume, and the venue's own settlement mechanics are documented - and nothing new contradicts the market-structure description, but the entry's named trigger state is still not the state these sources identify); originality is held at 2/5 (the mechanism and the crash-conditionality form are both published). The one score-adjacent change is recorded here as a disagreement rather than a move: the eleventh cycle's evidence would, if anything, argue for the mechanism score to rise, because the forced-unwind channel is now much better documented, but it would equally argue for the originality score to fall, because the channel is now documented by a peer-reviewed conference paper rather than only by a single author; the two roughly cancel and the entry is held. Rank 1 is retained, by 6 points over CRYPTO-EDGE-003, and the reason for the margin is unchanged: CRYPTO-EDGE-003's tenth-cycle downgrade, not any improvement here.
**Thirteenth cycle: no dimension moves; the total is 58, re-derived as 16+6+3+15+6+8+4.** The cycle's finding is a **measurement** finding, and the catalogue's standing rule from the eleventh cycle applies unchanged: evidence that bears on how a statistic is measured does not bear on a registered prediction, so the evidence-quality dimension (2/5) is untouched, the robustness dimension (1/5) is untouched, the net-value dimension (2/5) is untouched, and the mechanism dimension (4/5) is untouched. Free-data accessibility was already at the maximum 5/5 and is unchanged; the new datasets do not raise a ceiling. **A recorded disagreement on falsifiability, which the cycle declines to act on:** a reader could argue falsifiability 4/5 → 5/5 (total 60, +2) on the ground that the entry's *primary registered tail statistic* has moved from proxy-only to directly measurable on free data with a price-invariant construction, so the design is now falsifiable end-to-end rather than only through a stand-in. The cycle declines this because the direct measurement exists **only on a sibling instrument family** (inverse, not the entry's specified linear) and only over a 472-day publisher window that **ends on 2024-10-14** — before the entry's critical post-July-2024 regime is covered, before the October 2025 cascade, and before the entry's own literature-support window closes — so the design improvement is real but bounded, and 4/5 remains the honest score. The alternative placement is recorded here rather than dismissed, and it is the question the next cycle should take up with the window problem in view.

**Fourteenth cycle: no dimension moves; the total is 58, re-derived as 16+6+3+15+6+8+4.** The cycle's findings are **corrections to the catalogue's own recorded facts and to a construction specification**, and the standing rule from the eleventh cycle applies unchanged: evidence about how a statistic is measured, and corrections to the catalogue's own arithmetic or specifications, do not bear on a registered prediction, so the mechanism (4/5), evidence-quality (2/5), robustness (1/5) and net-value (2/5) dimensions are all untouched, and free-data accessibility was already at its 5/5 ceiling. Two of the corrections are of a kind the catalogue has previously treated as material and are worth naming explicitly, because a reader could reasonably weigh them differently. (i) **The proxy-to-flow ratio is not stable** (0.56x to 34.2x across contracts on one day, 4.3x to 8.2x for one contract across three days), and the thirteenth cycle's headline "10-14x" was an arithmetic mis-reading of its own two percentages. This is a *measurement* finding about the *proxy*, and the entry's robustness dimension is already at 1/5 for reasons documented in v1.2 and v1.3, so there is nothing for it to move down to; but the fact that the ratio is unstable by two orders of magnitude is the strongest single piece of evidence yet that the open-interest-clearing proxy is a **poor** stand-in for a liquidation event, and it is relevant to the entry's *falsification handling* (item 14) rather than to its score. (ii) **The USDⓈ-M liquidation record existed and was removed**, which changes the *shape* of the entry's remaining obstacle from "the statistic is not computable on the specified instrument" to "the statistic was computable on the specified instrument for 281 days and the files have been withdrawn, so the question is whether a mirror exists". A reader could argue that this **raises falsifiability from 4/5 to 5/5** (total 60, +2), on the ground that the direct measurement is now *documented* to have existed on the entry's own instrument and only its retrieval is in question, so the design is falsifiable end-to-end on the specified instrument rather than only through a sibling family. The cycle declines this, for the reason the thirteenth cycle declined the same move, and states it in a sharper form: the *withdrawal* of the record is a fact about the publisher's data policy, not a fact about the entry's design, and the entry's own critical regime (post-July-2024) was never covered by the UM record in any case — the UM window ends 2024-03-31, three months *before* the regime begins — so the documentation of the UM record does not extend the directly measurable arm of the entry's own test by a single day of the regime that matters. The alternative placement is recorded here rather than dismissed.

**Fifteenth cycle: no dimension moves; the total is 58, re-derived as 16+6+3+15+6+8+4.** The cycle's headline is a **data-access and construction finding**, and the catalogue's standing rule — that evidence about how a statistic is measured, or about data access, does not bear on a registered prediction and so does not move a score — applies unchanged: the mechanism (4/5), evidence-quality (2/5), robustness (1/5) and net-value (2/5) dimensions are untouched, and free-data accessibility was already at its 5/5 ceiling. **The fourteenth cycle's recorded disagreement on falsifiability (4/5 -> 5/5, total 60) is materially strengthened, and its ground is revised rather than repeated.** The fourteenth cycle declined that move on two grounds: that the direct measurement existed only on a **sibling instrument family**, and that the record's window ended before the entry's critical regime began. **The first ground is now removed** — the USDⓈ-M record is live, free, dated, checksum-verifiable and on the entry's own specified instrument, so the registered statistic can be computed end to end on the specified instrument over 281 days at per-event resolution, and the direct-measurement arm is a *direct* measurement for that window and a documented replication for the 199 days that follow it. **The second ground stands and is now the only one**: the record's window still ends 2024-03-31 on the specified instrument and 2024-10-14 on the inverse family, so it does not reach the entry's critical post-July-2024 regime by a single day, and the entry's registered prediction still cannot be tested where it matters most. The cycle therefore **holds falsifiability at 4/5 and the total at 58**, and records the alternative placement of 60 as closer to being accepted than it was, with the single remaining obstacle named.
**Seventeenth cycle: no dimension moves; the total is 58, re-derived as 16+6+3+15+6+8+4.** The cycle's findings are **a re-verification at panel scale, a completed measurement of the entry's registered statistic on the entry's own specified instrument, and a determination that closes the retrieval question** — and the catalogue's standing rule from the eleventh cycle applies unchanged: evidence about how a statistic is measured, and corrections to the catalogue's own recorded facts, do not bear on a registered prediction, so the mechanism (4/5), evidence-quality (2/5), robustness (1/5) and net-value (2/5) dimensions are all untouched, and free-data accessibility was already at its 5/5 ceiling. What follows is what the cycle *did* establish, because the entry's recorded facts are now wrong in three places and the corrections matter to any future cycle that reads them. (i) **The relocated linear record was re-verified live at full scale and every recorded figure reproduced exactly.** The prefix enumerates to **129,640 objects = 64,820 daily zips + 64,820 `.CHECKSUM` sidecars** in **311 symbol directories**, spanning **2023-06-25 to 2024-03-31 (281 distinct dates)**; symbol-days spanned **66,348**, present **64,820**, absent **1,528 = 2.30%**; **every object carries a LastModified on 2025-04-16 between 06:39:17Z and 06:59:29Z**, a single bulk upload inside a twenty-minute window; the documented path `data/futures/um/daily/liquidationSnapshot/` lists **zero objects** and returns **HTTP 404** on GET; and **SHA-256 was verified against the published sidecar for eleven files** — the three the fifteenth cycle opened plus eight more, including one from each end of the window, one from a symbol with an internal gap, one dated quarterly delivery contract and one USDC-quoted contract — **all matching byte-for-byte**. (ii) **The entry's registered tail statistic was computed end to end on the entry's own specified instrument over the record's full window, with a live-universe denominator built by enumerating the venue's own metrics tree** (1,293,102 keys; 646,551 dated metric files; 1,038 symbols; the universe running from 207 to 292 contracts a day), against which the relocated record's coverage is **median 95.32%, minimum 66.51% on 2023-08-26, maximum 100%**. The statistic is the forced SELL notional as a percentage of the contract's **opening** open interest value. Its **daily cross-sectional median** runs from **0.0043% to 2.9927%** (p10 0.0242%, median 0.1672%, p90 0.7987%, p99 1.7197%), and the **pooled contract-day** distribution has median 0.2119% and p99 4.1501%. **228 of the 281 days carry a cross-sectional median at or above 0.05%, 52 at or above 0.5% and 16 at or above 1%**, and the record holds **8,218,185 unique forced-liquidation events**, of which **5,091,109 (61.95%) are SELL** (liquidated longs) and 3,127,076 (38.05%) are BUY. *Establishes* that the entry's primary registered tail statistic is computable, in USDT, at per-event resolution, on the specified instrument, on a stated and now-measured population, and that the "extreme unwind" states the entry cares about are **not rare on this record** — sixteen days in 281 reach a 1% cross-sectional median. *Does not establish* any predictive relationship between the basis state and any of these quantities; **no return, Sharpe, drawdown, hit rate or forward performance quantity was computed for any strategy in this cycle, and no validation of this entry's registered prediction was performed here.** (iii) **The record's own missingness is now characterised rather than asserted, and the declared treatment is now tested.** Absence is **concentrated, not diffuse**: of 323 symbols live in the window, **87 have no absence at all**, 12 are absent on every one of their live days, and the worst 25 symbols hold 41.6% of all absent live symbol-days. On the **declared treatment** — an absent file is a *missing observation*, not a zero-event day — the test is the contract's own open interest: of the 1,529 absent symbol-days with a comparable previous-day close, **38.33% had the contract's base-asset open interest fall by more than 1% on the absent day, against 45.01% of 28,999 contract-days that do have a file**. An absent file is therefore *not* associated with an unusually violent unwind, and reading it as a zero would record "no forced liquidation" on days when the contract's own open interest was falling slightly *less* often than on recorded days. (iv) **The unit convention is now confirmed by cross-family calibration rather than by plausibility.** In the linear record `original_quantity` equals `accumulated_fill_quantity` on **100.000%** of 36,421 sampled unique rows while `last_fill_quantity` is smaller on 53.88% of them (it is the size of the *last* fill, not the total, so there is no partial-fill hazard), and the same holds on the inverse family. And because the same underlying appears in both families on the same date, the two families' **notional share** of opening open interest can be compared directly: the ratio of the linear family's notional share to the inverse family's is **1.44 at the median** on the dated pairs where both legs exist, with the inverse family's 100-USD multiplier re-derived live from the venue's own metrics on seven dates (98.3 to 102.1). A ratio near 1 confirms that the linear family's quantity is in the base asset with no contract multiplier; a ratio near 10 or 100 would have falsified it. (v) **The retrieval question is closed, and the obstruction is now known to be permanent rather than contingent.** An exhaustive census of the publisher's *entire* file store — every top-level prefix, including `data2/`, which no earlier cycle had examined and which contains only a stray `data2/data/spot/.DS_Store` and empty spot directories with **no futures tree at all** — establishes that **no per-contract forced-liquidation record of any kind exists anywhere in the publisher's bucket after 2024-10-14**. The entry's recorded obstacle was "the statistic was computable on the specified instrument for 281 days and the files have been withdrawn, so the question is whether a mirror exists"; the answer is that **no mirror exists at the publisher, and the publisher's own product decision, not a deletion, ended the record**. The entry's remaining obstacle is therefore **unchanged and now permanent**: the directly measurable arm covers 2023-06-25 to 2024-03-31 on the specified instrument and to 2024-10-14 on the inverse family, and the entry's critical post-July-2024 regime is **not reached by either**. The fourteenth cycle's single remaining ground for holding falsifiability at 4/5 — that the record's window ends before the entry's critical regime begins — **stands and is now the only one, with the second ground (retrieval) removed**. The total stays at 58, the alternative placement of 60 stays recorded, and the obstacle is now a supply discontinuity rather than a search.

17. Next most valuable research question: does extreme *perpetual* basis predict crash conditionality - the frequency and severity of forward drawdowns, and sell liquidations as a share of open interest - after momentum, realized volatility and open-interest changes are all controlled, in the post-July-2024 window that no published sample covers, and does the answer differ between the elevated-basis arm and the basis-compression arm? The question is now precisely decidable and now has a fair null: the mean-return form of the same claim has been contradicted on the same instrument family and window, the published tail claim has never been tested on that instrument, and four independent perpetual-era tests find no unconditional edge in the same signals. The outcome is decisive in one direction and informative in the other: if crash conditionality is also absent, the entry should be rejected rather than re-specified; if it is present, the entry is a genuine tail-risk overlay whose value must then be judged against the frequency of trigger states in the compressed perpetual-era basis distribution, against the open-interest-change baseline, and against which tail statistic can actually be computed on free data - and the three candidate statistics (drawdown frequency, realized skew, liquidation share) must be reported separately, because they are different claims. A second question, now of equal value: is the instrument-specific sign split (dated-futures basis predicts crashes, perpetual funding positively predicts returns) a property of the carry *instrument* or of the *sample period*, and does any published source separate the two? A third, and now the reason this entry is worth keeping at all: its role as the catalogue's mandatory comparator for every other entry is not stated anywhere in the record, and stating it explicitly would stop a future cycle from reading its rank-1 position as evidence of a stronger claim than the entry makes. **Ninth-cycle addition:** with the archive's 5-minute open-interest series now confirmed free over the whole post-July-2024 window, the entry's registered P1 statistic has a computable free proxy for the first time - the open-interest clearing that Garcia Seuma measures at -24.6% (Binance BTC) and -45% to -70% (Hyperliquid) in the October 2025 cascade - so the question is no longer blocked on data and is decidable in principle: does an elevated perp basis state predict elevated open-interest clearing and forward-drawdown frequency over the next 1-4 weeks, after momentum, realized volatility and open-interest change are controlled, and does the answer differ between the elevated-basis arm and the basis-compression arm? A fourth question is added: is the       crypto crash process a first-order transition (Garcia Seuma) rather than a critical one, and if so does a *conditional probability* framing (crash frequency given a positioning state)
      survive at all, or is the correct object the *severity* of the unwinding rather than its timing? **Tenth-cycle addition:** the cycle-10 specification is now fixed in item 14, so the
      question that outranks every other for this entry is no longer "can it be tested" but "is the *free* tail statistic the right one". Three sub-questions follow, in order. (i) The
      published tail claim on dated futures is about sell liquidations as a share of open interest; the archive has no liquidation dataset, so the free proxy is open-interest *clearing*,
      and the question is whether a clearing event is a faithful stand-in for a liquidation event at all - Garcia Seuma's per-cascade clearing magnitudes (−24.6% on Binance BTC, to
      0.30-0.55 of baseline on Hyperliquid) give a checkable benchmark, but the proxy is a *state change*, not a *flow*, and a validator must first show that the proxy separates the
      known cascades from ordinary open-interest decay before interpreting anything. (ii) H22 establishes that crash states are followed by positive cross-sectional returns and H13
      that extreme up shocks continue, so the tail statistic and the mean statistic must be reported separately and the question becomes whether a basis state can predict crash
      frequency *without* also being a momentum or volatility proxy - the entry's mandatory controls already say this, but the tenth cycle makes the separation binding rather than
      advisory. (iii) The entry's instrument-level standing rule is now under more pressure, not less: the instrument-level prior recorded in the seventh cycle (dated-futures basis predicts
      crashes, perpetual funding positively predicts returns, both by the same authors) plus Christin et al.'s documentation of historically paid carry on Binance perps means a perp
      *crash* claim is a claim about a *third* sign regime that no published source supports on any instrument. The highest-value external question is therefore unchanged in form but
      sharper in content: does any source test a positioning state against crash conditionality on *retail perps* after July 2024, and if none exists, is the entry still worth a
