Do not execute this plan in this repository. **Amended in v1.6 (fourteenth cycle — three additions forced by the venue-access determination, made in advance of any interpretation).** (i) **The venue pair must be preregistered with its collateral and venue class named.** The only second venue whose free, dated history now exists on both the carry leg and the price leg is a **USDC-margined on-chain decentralised exchange** (Hyperliquid: `fundingHistory` from 2023-05-11, daily `candleSnapshot` from 2023-05-12, keyless and free), while the entry's specified universe is **USDT-margined centralised perpetuals** on Binance, Bybit and OKX, and the centralised second leg has now been *determined* unobtainable free on a multi-year window (Bybit's public archive has no funding and no kline directory; OKX's public funding endpoint returns about 33 days and ignores its pagination parameters). A validator using the free second venue is therefore running a **documented substitution**, not the registered construction, and must say so in advance: it must name the venue pair, state that the pair is cross-venue-class and cross-collateral, and report the entry's own item 11 confound (collateral mix and stablecoin depeg masquerading as basis) as a live risk in that arm. (ii) **The mandatory liquidity filter must be applied with its asymmetry declared.** Venue open interest is not free and dated for the second venue (its daily asset-context history sits in a requester-pays bucket, and its REST open-interest field is current-only), while venue *volume* is free and dated for it through its daily candles; a validator must therefore state, in advance, whether the liquidity filter is applied on volume alone, on volume and open interest for the centralised leg only, or not at all, and must report the filter's result under each variant it claims to use. (iii) **The venue-pair dependence of the persistence sub-claim becomes a pre-committed test rather than a caveat.** The entry already records that a CEX-versus-DEX spread reverts in 1.5-3.5 hours while a CEX-versus-CEX spread is more persistent; a validator must therefore pre-commit whether the persistence sub-claim is tested on the pair it can actually run, and if the free pair is the CEX-versus-DEX one, must report the sub-claim **on that pair** and declare in advance that a failure there is a failure of the sub-claim *for that pair* and not for the CEX-versus-CEX pair the entry's evidence base describes. The registered prediction, the mandatory comparators, the horizon, the null outcome and the incremental bar against the unthresholded patient-carry alternative are all **unchanged**, and no empirical validation was performed in this repository.
**Amended in v1.3 (tenth cycle — cycle-10 specification, fixed in advance of any data inspection):** (a) **The sign is estimated before any trade expression is specified**, two-sided and
sign-free, on the slow clock: the statistic is a block-bootstrap estimate of the expensive-venue minus cheap-venue relative return conditional on extreme dispersion, reported for both
signs, and the direction of any trade is chosen by the estimate rather than by the registration. (b) **The slow clock is defined, not left to the validator**: the dispersion is a trailing
median over funding intervals sampled on a fixed weekly clock (or a trailing 7-day mean of daily dispersion, with the choice fixed in advance), because the level has a documented
1.5-3.5 hour half-life and an instantaneous reading is trading noise. (c) **The mandatory incremental bar is prior art in the same family, not a placebo** — this is the binding change of
the tenth cycle. The cost evidence forces this entry onto the unthresholded patient-carry construction, and that construction is now documented in three preregistered results: harvesting
every paying stream beats selecting the top K by funding monotonically (Sharpe 2.27, 2.99 and 4.06 for K = 3, 5 and 10 against 5.60 for harvest-all over the same 34 symbols and the same
window, with the same ordering in drawdown: −5.52% for K = 5 against −0.76% for harvest-all); the harvest form is robust to a point-in-time universe (86% of its Sharpe retained, against
58% for the momentum-rotation book that is this catalogue's cross-sectional analogue), while its income halves (CAGR +4.36% to +2.35%) and it earns approximately zero in 2025-2026 in
every arm; and the same ledger records that the selection form's advantage does not transfer across families, which is a warning that the distinction is a hypothesis and not a rule.
The entry must therefore beat the **unthresholded patient-carry construction** — long every funding stream that pays, short the perp against spot, on the same preregistered slow clock — in
the same window, the same cost model and the same inference, or its content is nil by construction. (d) **The dispersion-persistence sub-claim is tested per venue pair**, with the pair fixed
in advance and never pooled, and separately from the sign estimate; the reversed-sign placebo remains mandatory and is reported with the same statistic. (e) **The incremental contribution over
harvest-all is reported in the same units as the incumbent** (median difference in bp per week, and the volatility of the bucket), because a signal that is a worse version of prior art is
the specific failure mode this entry now has to avoid. (f) **Eleventh-cycle additions.** (a) *The cross-venue REST route is narrower than the entry's tenth-cycle note recorded, and this is an environment limitation, not a data determination.* Checked directly 2026-10-20: https://fapi.binance.com/fapi/v1/exchangeInfo and https://fapi.binance.com/fapi/v1/time returned HTTP 451; https://api.bybit.com/v5/market/time returned HTTP 403; https://www.okx.com/api/v5/public/time returned HTTP 200; the Binance S3 archive remained fully accessible. The entry's Binance leg therefore has a free, dated, archive route (monthly funding-rate files under data/futures/um/monthly/fundingRate/ per symbol from 2020-01) while its cross-venue legs are, from this machine, OKX-only by REST. The tenth cycle's statement that "the cross-venue half is still REST-only" remains correct and is now sharpened: it is REST-only *and* partly unreachable here, so a validator working in this environment would need a second venue with an accessible history, which is a cost of the design, not a defect of the claim. (b) **A delisting settlement mechanically zeroes the venue basis, and this is a construction constraint the entry's book must respect.** The Binance futures delisting notices now available as a dated, machine-readable feed state the settlement mechanics explicitly: all positions are closed and an automatic settlement is conducted at a stated UTC time (09:00 UTC for USDⓈ-M, 08:30 UTC for the COIN-M leg in the 2026-03-17/18 notice), users may not open new non-reduce-only orders from 30 minutes before settlement, and the contract is removed after the settlement completes. Bybit's equivalent notice states that "the closing price will be based on the average index price in the 30 minutes prior to the delisting", and Coinbase's equivalent states a 60-minute average index price. A cross-venue relative-value book that holds a contract on a venue through that venue's settlement therefore has its terminal leg marked at the **index** by construction, and the venue-specific basis at settlement is **mechanically zero** rather than observed. The entry's registered statistic is a cross-venue funding *dispersion* signal with a cross-venue relative-value expression, so the rule is: **every event window must exclude contracts with an announced settlement inside the window, and the book must be flat on the delisting venue by the restriction time**; a validator that ignores this would be measuring an artefact. This is a construction amendment, not a change to the registered prediction. (f) **The current-regime result is a binding constraint rather than a footnote**: if the harvest alternative earns approximately zero
in 2025-2026, any construction that converges on it must show its incremental content in a regime where that content is being paid, and a result that appears only in 2019-2024 is
reported as a historical-regime result. (g) **Costs** must include taker fees on both legs, funding accrual on both legs, slippage and the capital cost of posting margin on two venues, on
the same clock as the signal. Do not execute this plan in this repository. **Amended in v1.4 (eleventh cycle - one mandatory construction amendment, forced by a newly confirmed free data source, made in advance of any data inspection):** (h) **Delisting-settlement exclusion and forced-flat rule (mandatory).** The Binance announcement feed is now confirmed as a dated, machine-readable list of delisting notices (see item 10), each giving the exact settlement timestamp and the exact contract symbols; Bybit and Coinbase publish equivalent notices with disclosed settlement-window mechanics. A validator must therefore (i) exclude from the entry's universe, for any window, every contract with an announced settlement inside that window, and (ii) hold the book flat on the delisting venue from 30 minutes before the announced settlement time, because the venue settles the contract against the index average over a disclosed pre-settlement window (30 minutes on Bybit, 60 minutes on Coinbase, 09:00 UTC settlement on Binance USDⓈ-M) and therefore **mechanically** drives the venue-specific basis to zero at settlement. The pre-registered statistic is a *funding* dispersion, so the exclusion is a universe rule rather than a change of signal; but the *expression* is a cross-venue relative-value trade, and without the rule the trade's terminal P&L on the delisting venue would be a settlement artefact rather than a market outcome. The event window must also be reported with the count of excluded contracts, because a period in which many contracts settle is a period in which the cross-venue book is mechanically shrinking, and that is a confound on a cross-venue *dispersion* statistic that is not present in a single-venue statistic. This amendment does not weaken or strengthen the registered prediction; it makes the construction implementable. The v1.3 items (a) through (g) are otherwise unchanged. Do not execute this plan in this repository.
15. Evidence notes with sources and dates: **Tenth-cycle sources (2026-10-20).** martex-qu **Eleventh-cycle sources (2026-10-20).** Binance delisting-announcement feed, confirmed reachable and machine-readable: the CMS article catalog endpoint `https://www.binance.com/bapi/composite/v1/public/cms/article/catalog/list/query?catalogId=161&pageNo=N&pageSize=20` (access 1, no key, HTTP 200) and the article detail endpoint `https://www.binance.com/bapi/composite/v1/public/cms/article/detail/query?articleCode=<code>` (access 1), which returns `publishDate` as Unix epoch milliseconds, `firstCatalogName` (Delisting) and `firstCatalogId` (161). Enumerated 2026-10-20: **442 articles across 23 pages, back to 2022-02-24, of which 76 are perpetual-contract delisting notices**, each giving the exact settlement datetime and the exact contract symbols; the public HTML detail pages (`https://www.binance.com/en/support/announcement/detail/<code>`) carry the same content with a human-readable "Published on" line. *Establishes* that a dated, machine-readable, multi-year venue-announced perpetual delisting list exists on free data; *does not establish* any return effect, and the feed carries no stability guarantee or version contract. Bybit announcement feed, `https://announcements.bybit.com/en/article/delisting-of-scrtusdt-perpetual-contract--art705fb3ab79c5/` (access 1, HTML; the REST API returned HTTP 403 from this environment) - *establishes* that a second major venue publishes the same class of notice with the same structure, including the settlement mechanic that "the closing price will be based on the average index price in the 30 minutes prior to the delisting"; *does not establish* any return effect. Coinbase delisting notice (via public reporting, access 2) - *establishes* that a third venue delists perpetuals with settlement on a 60-minute average index price; *does not establish* any return effect. Binance archive verification, six contract instances across four notices (IPUSDT, IPUSDC, PROMPTUSDT, 1000000BOBUSDT, RVVUSDT, YALAUSDT), each read directly from the 1d kline zips on 2026-10-20 - *establishes* that the archive's last non-zero-trade date equals the venue's announced settlement date in every instance checked, and that the file series continues after settlement with zero volume, zero trades and a frozen close; *does not establish* anything about returns, and no performance statistic was computed from these files. **No dimension moves on the strength of these sources**: they bear on the entry's construction, not on its registered mapping, which remains untested. **Tenth-cycle sources (2026-10-20).** martex-quant ledger H66 "Cross-Sectional Carry (top-K funding)" (verdict 2026-08-27; free GitHub raw, access 1 for the document; unreviewed preregistered tier): on a shared window 2019-09-11 to 2026-07-09 with all incumbents recomputed in the same run, top-K selection on 34 symbols is *monotonically dominated* by harvesting every symbol whose own trailing funding is positive - K = 3 gives Sharpe 2.27, K = 5 gives 2.99, K = 10 gives 4.06, harvest-all over the same 34 symbols gives 5.60 and harvest-all over 8 majors gives 5.91, with drawdown moving the same way (K = 5 at -5.52% against harvest-all at -0.76%); the document's own conclusion is that "the less you select, the better it gets, without exception", that H65's proposed refinement ("breadth feeds edges that SELECT and dilutes edges that HARVEST") is refuted, and it records an implementation defect it found and corrected rather than quietly fixing, on the principle that a specification the implementation silently contradicts is the failure mode pre-registration exists to catch. martex-quant ledger H70 "Rotation Concentration: was K=2 ever the right number?" (verdict 2026-08-28; access 1 for the document): on a common window 2018-08-17 to 2026-07-05 the deployed momentum-rotation book reproduces to the published digits (K = 2: CAGR +42.91%, Sharpe 1.47, MDD -29.01%) and widening the selection degrades it (K = 3 Sharpe 1.61, K = 5 1.40, K = 8 1.27, with drawdown worse at every breadth above 2); the document's mechanistic reading is that H66's carry result does not transfer to momentum rotation, because carry harvests a premium paid by roughly 20 near-independent funding streams while rotation *selects*, so diluting a selection edge with weaker picks lowers the mean faster than it lowers the variance, and it explicitly records the select-versus-harvest distinction as "a hypothesis, not a rule" because it is one measurement in each of two families. martex-quant ledger H72 "Point-in-Time Carry" (verdict 2026-08-28; access 1 for the document), whose full text was read in the tenth cycle beyond the summary already on file: the hypothesis *predicted* that carry would survive an honest point-in-time universe where momentum would not, and it did - 86% of Sharpe retained (5.61 to 4.83) against momentum's 58% (1.47 to 0.86), with CAGR halving (+4.36% to +2.35%), a better drawdown (-0.78% to -0.35%), and a stated mechanism (rotation selects and is handed winners by a hindsight list; carry harvests and a biased list changes which streams are averaged and little else); the overlap between the point-in-time top-40 and the hindsight universe is 28-32% in the key years and rises to 52% by 2025, i.e. the universes are equally contaminated and the edges are not equally damaged; the document also states the caveat this entry inherits - contracts delisted after a collapse are absent from the pool, so the *sign* of the delisting bias on a book that is short the perp through exactly those events is genuinely unknown - and that carry earns approximately zero in 2025-2026 in every arm, the fifth independent confirmation of that finding after H62, H63 and H65. **The tenth cycle's finding: none of these three results tests CRYPTO-EDGE-003's registered mapping.** They test the *construction family* into which the entry's cost arithmetic has pushed it, and they say three things that bear directly on the entry: a selection rule is the weaker form of this trade; the harvest alternative is the form that is robust to universe bias and that earns approximately nothing today; and the harvest form's distinctive content over a selection form is documented to be nil in the strongest available evidence. A supporting datum for the entry's own direction remains absent, and the closest adjacent test already on file (H68) found that the high-premium venue was followed by *higher* returns. The entry is therefore downgraded on evidence quality and not on net value, which is already at its floor of 1/5, and the placement of this evidence is stated explicitly so that a reader who prefers to score it as net-value or originality evidence can see the alternative. martex-quant ledger H13 and H22 are recorded in CRYPTO-EDGE-001's item 15 and are adjacent rather than on point for this entry: H22 shows that crash states in the perp era are followed by positive cross-sectional returns, which bears on how any cross-venue relative-value claim behaves around delisting-driven dislocations, and H13 shows continuation after extreme up shocks, a fourth instance in that ledger of continuation beating reversal in crypto. **Ninth-cycle sources and finding (2026-10-20): no new direct evidence was found, and the entry is unchanged at 55/100.** Searches for an independent estimate of the sign of cross-venue funding dispersion to venue-relative return, on a slow clock and in the post-July-2024 window, returned nothing on point; the sources below are adjacent and are recorded as such. Kitron and Wengrowicz (arXiv:2608.21888, access 1) find that conditioning on the perpetual basis, funding rate, funding clock, volatility and session is flat in every case across 183 Binance pairs - single-venue, so it does not touch the cross-venue dispersion measure this entry is built on, but it is a further independent instance of a funding-state variable carrying no standalone predictive content where it has been tested on a wide panel, which is a prior the entry must overcome. He, Manela, Ross and von Wachter (arXiv:2212.06888v7) remains the mechanism source: Binance-Bybit funding correlation of 0.74 with meaningful divergences during unusually large funding payments, and the explicit statement that residual cross-exchange dispersion reflects limits to arbitrage across segmented venues. A useful practical addition from the ninth cycle: Binance funding rates for the entry's own benchmark venue are now available as a dated archive (data/futures/um/monthly/fundingRate/ on https://data.binance.vision, access 1, monthly files per symbol from January 2020 for BTCUSDT) rather than only through REST pagination, which materially reduces the cost of building the entry's long history, but does not solve the *cross-venue* half, for which Bybit and OKX remain REST-only. Original source list continues: He, Manela, Ross & Wachter (2026), "Fundamentals of Perpetual Futures", arXiv:2212.06888 (v7, 17 Sep 2026), confirmed free - funding rates tend to be similar across exchanges because of cross-exchange arbitrage but can diverge in extreme liquidity episodes; the residual dispersion is attributed to segmented arbitrage (no cross-margining, each leg separately funded and margined) and to liquidity differences across exchanges; Binance-Bybit funding correlation of 0.74 with meaningful divergences during large funding payments; the authors state that residual cross-exchange dispersion reflects limits to arbitrage across segmented venues and is a promising avenue for future research (mechanism evidence and prior-art scoping). Zhivkov (2026), "The Two-Tiered Structure of Cryptocurrency Funding Rate Markets", Mathematics 14(2):346, doi 10.3390/math14020346, open access, checked 2026-10-20 - 35.7 million one-minute funding observations across 26 exchanges (11 CEX, 15 DEX), 749 symbols, eight consecutive days; CEX venues lead price discovery with 61 percent higher integration than DEX; 17 percent of observations show arbitrage spreads of at least 20 bps while only about 40 percent of top opportunities stay profitable after transaction costs and spread reversals, with forced exits in about 95 percent of opportunities; funding clusters near zero (IQR 1.20 bps CEX); supports the fragmentation mechanism and the cost reality, but the sample is eight days of one-minute data in a single-author feature paper and is treated as supporting microstructure evidence only, not as decisive. Gornall, Rinaldi & Xiao (2025), "Perpetual Futures and Basis Risk: Evidence from Cryptocurrency", SSRN 5036933 (rev. 19 May 2025), AEA 2026 program - perpetual futures dominate crypto derivatives trading, enhance liquidity, reduce extreme price dislocations, and their arbitrage drawdowns mean-revert quickly; supplies the strongest contrary argument (price leadership, dislocation compression) and the quarter-versus-perp contrast. Ackerer, Hugonnier & Jermann, "Perpetual Futures Pricing" (Feb 2025 version, free working paper at https://www.epfl.ch/labs/sfi-jh/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/AHJ-main-12.pdf) - theoretical pricing of perpetual contracts and the funding mechanism, including quanto and inverse variants (context only). Exchange API documentation checked 2026-10-20 (Binance USD-M futures market data, Bybit V5 funding history, OKX V5 public funding-rate history) - access classification 1 for the endpoints themselves, 2 for retention depth and rate-limit headroom. Labels: mechanism and published findings are separated from unverified assumptions (the decile threshold, the venue universe, the holding window, the persistence window, and the sign of the relative-performance effect are assumptions). **Added in v1.1 (all checked 2026-10-20):** martex-quant public research ledger, "Hypothesis 68 — Cross-Venue Price Dislocation" (docs/hypotheses/68-cross-venue-dislocation.md, verdict 2026-08-27), free GitHub raw, access 1 for the document; preregistered with four signals and twelve declared cells (four signals by three horizons), a 20-symbol panel with a fixed $1M median-quote-volume floor on every venue leg, 31,752 symbol-days from 2019-01-01 to 2026-08-27, synchronous 00:00 UTC closes across Coinbase, Binance, OKX and Bitfinex, trailing-90-day percentile bucketing and a fixed bootstrap; results: the peg-adjusted venue premium *level* predicts forward returns in the continuation direction (+3.17% at 7 days, CI [+0.51%, +6.08%], breadth 17/20; +13.36% at 30 days, CI [+2.53%, +26.17%]) and survives a trailing-7-day-return trim (+2.47%, CI [+0.13%, +4.74%]) and a drop of the two largest contributors (+2.85%, CI [+0.34%, +5.46%]), while the *dispersion magnitude* signal is noise at all three horizons (+0.08% at 1 day, -0.24% at 7 days, -6.65% at 30 days) and the stablecoin-peg component is noise at all three horizons; establishes that cross-venue disagreement as a magnitude had no forward-return content in that test and that the price-premium level, not the peg, carried the information; does not establish any funding-dispersion result and does not test venue-relative returns. martex-quant "Hypothesis 69 — Cross-Venue Premium, Strategy Grade" (docs/hypotheses/69-cross-venue-premium-strategy.md, verdict 2026-08-27), free GitHub raw, access 1 for the document; the same information carried into a long-only tranche-ladder book with next-open fills, 10bp fee, 1bp half-spread and 25bp participation impact per side was KILLED on all three gates (Sharpe 0.89 against 1.0 required; deflated Sharpe 0.36 against 0.95 required; Sharpe below the equal-weight buy-and-hold benchmark of 0.94 on the same window and engine; correlation 0.39 with the incumbent book against a 0.30 bar), with 2025 and 2026 both negative; establishes that a statistically significant information signal in this cross-venue family did not survive execution in a long-only shape, and does not establish anything about a delta-neutral relative-value trade. Finance Research Letters (2026), "Exploring risk and return profiles of funding rate arbitrage on CEX and DEX", doi 10.1016/j.bcra.2025.100354, abstract freely readable, access 2; reports that delta-neutral funding-rate arbitrage between Binance/Bitmex and the ApolloX/Drift DEXs produced superior risk-adjusted returns than holding the asset, with low correlation to the buy-and-hold return, and six-month yields reported up to 115.9% with losses limited to 1.92%; this is contrary evidence to Zhivkov (2026) on the cost economics of pure funding capture, and because it is a backtest-based published result with a different venue and asset mix it is treated as unresolved disagreement rather than as support; it does not establish any venue-relative performance prediction, which is this entry's claim. Labels: the mechanism (segmented arbitrage, no cross-margining, liquidity heterogeneity) remains a published finding; the predictive mapping from funding dispersion to venue-relative underperformance still has no direct published or preregistered support, and the adjacent evidence now points the other way. **Added in v1.2 (all checked 2026-10-20):** Zhivkov, Petar, Todorov, Venelin and Georgiev, Slavi (2026), 'Temporal Dynamics of Market Microstructure in Cryptocurrency Perpetual Futures: Econometric Evidence from Centralized and Decentralized Exchanges', MDPI Journal of Financial Markets 14(5):103, https://www.mdpi.com/2227-7072/14/5/103, abstract freely readable via the publisher page and the search index, access 2; provides 9.1 million hourly observations across 26 exchanges and 812 symbols over November 2025 to January 2026, confirms that the two-tiered structure persists but varies in magnitude, finds near-integrated volatility behaviour in only 24.5% of rolling windows, finds Bybit Granger-causing Binance funding rates in 62% of windows against 21% in the reverse direction and OKX Granger-causing Binance in 55% against 25% so that 'information discovery in perpetual futures funding rates does not follow a simple hierarchy based on the largest exchange', and finds intraday spreads peaking about two hours after each settlement (02:00 UTC mean 16.85bps and 21.4% arbitrage prevalence against a 17:00 UTC trough of 14.35bps and 16.5%); establishes that the price-discovery objection is not a size story, that dispersion peaks and decays on a sub-settlement clock, and that no discrete regime shifts were detected; establishes no forward-return claim. DavidAyusoPita, 'funding-carry-analysis' (public GitHub repository), https://github.com/DavidAyusoPita/funding-carry-analysis, raw and contents API fetch reliably, access 1 for the documents; provides two years of public funding and price history, July 2024 to July 2026, 18,208 hourly observations per pair for BTC, ETH and SOL across Binance, Bybit, OKX and Hyperliquid, and finds that the on-chain venue pays more than the centralised one in 65-72% of hours worth 8.5-11.4% annualized while the same measurement between two centralised venues is centred on zero, that the exchange-versus-DEX spread level mean-reverts with an AR(1) half-life of 1.5-3.5 hours while its direction is a regime lasting months, that a threshold rule earns the most gross income of any rule tested and finishes at -9% to -15% annualized after costs, and that a patient rule that holds continuously and revisits only which venue to short on a 30-day clock returns +2.7% to +5.4% annualized net with a maximum drawdown under 2%; establishes the half-life of the dispersion level and the cost outcome of the entry's registered construction on exactly the venues the entry targets; is a single-author, unreviewed repository and is treated as a preregistered-tier external test rather than as peer-reviewed evidence. Nguyen, Khanh (8 July 2026), 'Cross-exchange crypto funding-rate carry: what survived the robustness tests', https://khanh1ng.github.io/writing/2026/cross-exchange-funding-carry/, freely readable, access 2, practitioner blog; provides that cross-exchange funding carry returned +6.2% with a 1.2% drawdown on the same window over which the Binance-only version returned -10.0% with a 25.6% drawdown; establishes that venue breadth is where the cost economics live in this family, which is the entry's premise; establishes no venue-relative return prediction. Pindza, Edson (19 August 2026), 'Centralized-decentralized exchange funding rate arbitrage as a basis trade: risk decomposition, stress testing, and portfolio construction under venue uncertainty', Digital Finance, doi 10.1007/s42521-026-00213-3, abstract freely readable via the publisher page, access 2; provides a five-component risk decomposition, a Cox liquidation-hazard model and Monte Carlo tail analysis for CEX-DEX funding arbitrage over January 2021 to December 2024 on Binance futures for BTC, ETH and SOL with synthetic DEX funding rates, and finds that net annualized returns are driven mainly by funding carry but are highly assumption-sensitive, that volatility regime dominates liquidation hazard, and that counterparty-risk episodes are more damaging than directional crashes; establishes that the carry-capture version of this family is dominated by leverage and venue risk rather than by funding differential; establishes no venue-relative performance claim. Pindza, Edson (2026), 'Reinforcement Learning for Automated Market Making in Cryptocurrency Perpetual Futures: Integrating Funding Rate Dynamics with Historical BTCUSDT Evidence', Journal of Financial Derivatives and Digital Markets, doi 10.1016/j.jfds.2026.100197, abstract freely readable via the publisher page, access 2; provides 29,606 hourly observations from 31 December 2022 to 18 May 2026 and 3,701 funding records on Binance futures, with funding, realized volatility and an order-flow-imbalance proxy as state features; establishes only that the state representation is informative for market making; establishes no venue-relative return prediction. Label update (v1.2): the mechanism (segmented arbitrage, no cross-margining, liquidity heterogeneity) remains a published finding; the predictive mapping from funding dispersion to venue-relative underperformance still has no direct published or preregistered support, and the adjacent evidence is now explicitly mixed rather than uniformly adverse.

**Fourteenth-cycle sources (2026-10-20).** All four are **access determinations**, tested live on 2026-10-20, and none of them bears on the entry's registered prediction.
(a) **Hyperliquid public info API** (https://api.hyperliquid.xyz/info, POST, keyless, HTTP 200, no registration) — access 1. *Establishes* that a second venue's **free, dated** history exists on both legs the entry needs: `{"type":"fundingHistory","coin":"BTC",...}` returns dated settlements with a `fundingRate` and a `premium` field, and the earliest BTC record returned for a query spanning 2017-2026 is at **2023-05-11**, i.e. back to the venue's launch, with the 2023-11-01, 2024-06-01 and 2026-01-01 windows all returning full pages; and `{"type":"candleSnapshot","req":{"coin":"BTC","interval":"1d",...}}` returns dated daily candles with open, close, high, low, volume and trade count, verified for both 2024-01 and **2023-05-12**, so the price leg is free and dated from launch as well. *Does not establish* anything about the entry's registered prediction, about the venue's dispersion behaviour, or that the venue's instruments are comparable to the entry's specified USDT-margined centralised perpetuals — it is a **USDC-margined decentralised** venue.
(b) **Bybit public data archive and API** (https://public.bybit.com/, https://public.bybit.com/trading/, https://public.bybit.com/funding/, https://public.bybit.com/kline/, https://public.bybit.com/premium_index/BTCUSD/, https://public.bybit.com/spot_index/BTCUSD/) — access 1 for the archive, HTTP 200, and HTTP **404** for the two funding and kline directories, which do not exist. *Establishes* that the archive publishes **trades** (per-symbol daily CSV.gz), **premium_index** and **spot_index** and **not** funding rates or klines; that the premium-index and spot-index series for the eleven listed inverse coins cover only **2019-10-01 to 2020-03-10** (162 and 169 daily files respectively) and therefore cannot support the entry's window; and that the third-party bulk-downloader for this archive had to **remove** its funding-rate and klines functionality in its 1.2.0 release because "the data is no longer available on Bybit" — a dated, independent corroboration of the same fact. Bybit's funding REST endpoint returns HTTP 403 from this environment, which is recorded as an environment limitation and not as a data-availability finding. *Does not establish* anything about the entry's registered prediction.
(c) **OKX public funding-rate history** (https://www.okx.com/api/v5/public/funding-rate-history, access 1, keyless, HTTP 200) — *establishes* that the endpoint returns **at most the most recent 100 settlements** (2026-09-17 to 2026-10-19 for BTC-USDT-SWAP, i.e. about 33 days at an 8-hour cadence) and that its documented pagination parameters **do not work**: a request with `before` set to the oldest returned timestamp returns **the same page**, and a request with `after` set to a 2020 timestamp returns an **empty array**. *Does not establish* anything about the entry's registered prediction. **This supersedes in place the thirteenth cycle's recorded claim that "the cross-venue leg is constructible from Binance's dated funding-rate archive on one side and OKX's public REST on the other": OKX's public REST cannot supply a multi-year dated funding history, so that recorded route does not exist.**
(d) **Ironflow, "Hyperliquid historical data: where to get it" (2026)** (https://ironflow.sh/guides/hyperliquid-historical-data) and **Hydromancer's `liquidationHistoryByTime` and `fills` schema documentation** (https://docs.hydromancer.xyz/readme/rest-api/historical-data/liquidationhistorybytime, https://docs.hydromancer.xyz/reservoir/schema-reference/fills) — practitioner-grade, access 1 for the documentation. *Establishes* that Hyperliquid's archive is **prefix-dependent**: the `node_fills_by_block` prefix "started in late July 2025" while "earlier periods are in the legacy `node_fills` and `node_trades` prefixes", which resolves the catalogue's carried conflict between the 2025-03-27 and 2025-05-25 start dates — a "fill-log start date" is a property of *which prefix* is read, not a single venue-wide date — and that the third-party indexer states "all data is fully available from **28-07-2025**" after its own backfill of the native API; that Hyperliquid's daily asset contexts (mark price, funding, open interest) are in a **requester-pays** bucket updated about once a month with "no guarantee" and "data may be missing", which is why the entry's *liquidity filter* is not free and dated for the second venue; and that a liquidation fill appears on **both parties' rows** and must be deduplicated by the liquidated-user leg, the same hazard the catalogue already records for the Binance record. *Does not establish* anything about the entry's registered prediction.
**Thirteenth-cycle sources (2026-10-20), all in the entry's own family, none of them testing its registered price-differential prediction.**
(a) **DavidAyusoPita, "Funding Rate Carry: what survives execution costs?"** (https://github.com/DavidAyusoPita/funding-carry-analysis, free, access 1, undated repository with a stated method) — two years of public funding and price history, July 2024 to July 2026, 18,208 hourly observations per pair, BTC, ETH and SOL across Binance, Bybit, OKX and Hyperliquid; the method is stated in detail and includes the two construction points that matter for this entry (normalising the 8-hour centralised cadence against Hyperliquid's hourly cadence to a per-hour fraction, and inferring the settlement interval from the timestamps because Binance moves volatile symbols to a 4-hour schedule). *Establishes* the harvest-versus-select result recorded in item 13, the 1.5-3.5 hour half-life of the cross-venue spread level, the negative net result of the threshold rule, and the positive net result of the patient rule, together with an explicit statement of its own limitations (no liquidation modelling, returns on notional rather than capital, no counterparty or protocol risk, assumed rather than observed slippage, ignored funding caps, survivorship and selection, OKX's three-month history excluded, one historical path). *Does not establish* the entry's registered prediction, and is a non-peer-reviewed repository rather than a preregistered ledger.
(b) **Lau, Tony, "The Funding Carry and a Cross-Venue Spread on Perpetual Futures: A Significance-Tested Study of Hyperliquid and Centralized Venues"**, SSRN 6993978, 25 June 2026, with an open replication package at Zenodo record 20938723 (29 self-contained scripts, cached raw inputs, a data-provenance table and a per-result claim-to-script mapping; access 1) — *establishes* a static cross-venue dislocation (long the centralised-venue perpetual, short the Hyperliquid perpetual) worth roughly 7% annualised over a three-year sample, 14.7% in 2024 and 4.6% in 2025, highly significant on Newey-West HAC t-statistics of 4-8, walk-forward consistent in 4-5 of 5 folds, surviving basis, capital and transaction costs at a prudent 2-3x per-leg leverage, with a single-venue carry premium of only 2-3% on Binance and Bybit and no longer significant into 2026, and a catalogue of null results in which every refinement fails to beat the naive baseline. *Does not establish* the entry's registered prediction, which is about a dispersion *state* rather than a static venue premium, and it is a working paper with a replication package rather than a peer-reviewed article.
(c) **Nguyen, "Cross-exchange crypto funding-rate carry: what survived the robustness tests"** (https://khanh1ng.github.io/writing/2026/cross-exchange-funding-carry/, 8 July 2026, access 1) — practitioner blog with a stated construction over 7 assets and 6 venues, hourly funding since 2020 resampled to the 8-hour settlement cycle, a 90-day z-score trigger at |z| > 2, full collateral on both legs and no leverage. *Establishes* that the cross-exchange form is the whole strategy, that **the same signal restricted to Binance alone from 2023 loses money (-10.0% annualised, 25.6% maximum drawdown) while the cross-exchange form does not (+6.2%, 1.2%)**, and that the routing is concentrated in Hyperliquid at roughly 55% of position-periods because of its hourly settlement. *Does not establish* the entry's registered prediction, and is a practitioner source.
(d) **ValidatedStrategies, "Cross-Exchange Funding Arbitrage Backtest: 3/11 Gates (2026)"** (https://validatedstrategies.com/strategy/FAR, test date 25 June 2026, access 1) — an unreviewed preregistered ledger entry with a locked specification, a profit factor, a deflated Sharpe, a random-permutation placebo, a cost-stress test and a walk-forward, published pass or fail. *Establishes* the retirement of the cross-venue funding-arbitrage archetype at 3 of 11 gates with a profit factor of 0.107 while the placebo gate passes, i.e. that the signal is real and the archetype dies on cost, and it records the venue-availability constraint that limited the test to Hyperliquid and dYdX. *Does not establish* the entry's registered prediction, and its own published caveats are part of the record.
(e) **Zhivkov (2026), "The Two-Tiered Structure of Cryptocurrency Funding Rate Markets"**, Mathematics 14(2):346, doi 10.3390/math14020346, open access, re-read in full this cycle — the peer-reviewed member of this set, with a panel of 35.7 million one-minute observations across 26 exchanges and 749 symbols, evidence that centralised venues dominate price discovery with 61% higher integration than decentralised venues, and that of the 17% of observations showing economically significant arbitrage spreads of 20 basis points or more, only 40% generate positive returns after transaction costs and spread reversals, with forced exits occurring in 95% of opportunities. *Does not establish* the entry's registered prediction, but it does establish the peer-reviewed cost arithmetic of the family.
16. Scores, total rank, confidence, explanation: Causal mechanism 3/5 -> 12; evidence quality 2/5 -> 6; robustness 2/5 -> 6; free-data accessibility 4/5 -> 12; net value after frictions 1/5 -> 3; falsifiability 4/5 -> 8; originality 4/5 -> 8. Total 55/100, down from 58. Confidence: low, down from low-medium. Changes in v1.1: robustness 3/5 -> 2/5, because the sign of the registered prediction is now contradicted by the better-supported reading — three independent sources place price discovery on the more heavily traded and typically higher-funding venue, and a preregistered cross-venue test found the high-premium venue was followed by higher returns — and because the closest adjacent test of cross-venue disagreement as a magnitude found no content at all; evidence quality held at 2/5, because there is still no direct published or preregistered evidence for the entry's specific predictive mapping, and the new evidence is adjacent rather than on-point, but the adjacent evidence is now substantial and points the other way; net value after frictions held at 2/5, because the cost arithmetic for pure funding capture was already poor (Zhivkov 2026) and the strategy-grade precedent from this family (H69: Sharpe 0.89, killed on three gates) is a long-only beta-heavy expression rather than the delta-neutral relative trade this entry specifies, so the downgrade stops short of a further reduction; mechanism, free-data accessibility, falsifiability and originality are unchanged. Rank 2: it now leads only CRYPTO-EDGE-004, by 2 points, and its case rests on clean identification rather than on evidence of predictive power — the decisive change this cycle is that the entry's sign must be estimated rather than assumed. Changes in v1.2: net value after frictions 2/5 -> 1/5, because the only direct external cost test of the entry's registered construction finds that a threshold rule on the trailing spread earns the most gross income of any rule tested and finishes at -9% to -15% annualized after costs, and the only construction that clears costs in that test is a slow, patient rebalance that does not use extreme-dispersion thresholds at all - so the entry's own construction has a documented negative net outcome and the positive alternative is a different hypothesis. Robustness is held at 2/5, and this is a deliberate choice rather than an oversight: the new evidence is genuinely two-sided. It is adverse in that the dispersion level has a documented 1.5-3.5 hour half-life, so the entry's construction may have no power; but it is favourable in that the price-discovery objection that v1.1 promoted to primary is now documented to be a poor general rule, because mid-tier centralised venues and decentralised venues lead the largest venue in funding-rate information flow, so the objection no longer discriminates in favour of the continuation sign. Evidence quality is held at 2/5 for the same reason: there is still no direct published or preregistered evidence for the entry's specific predictive mapping, and the adjacent evidence is now mixed rather than uniformly adverse, which is not an improvement in support but a reduction in opposition. Mechanism, free-data accessibility, falsifiability and originality are unchanged: the mechanism (segmented arbitrage across venues with no cross-margining) is a published finding, every input is confirmed free, the design is clean and two-sided, and the entry remains differentiated from CRYPTO-EDGE-001 by differencing out the market-wide carry and momentum component. Rank 2: it leads CRYPTO-EDGE-004 by 10 points, but its case now rests on clean identification and a *contested but unexcluded* mechanism rather than on any evidence of predictive power, and the decisive change this cycle is that the construction, not the claim, is what now has to earn its place. **Ninth cycle: no change.** The total stays at 55/100 with every dimension held, because the cycle found no new direct evidence on the entry's two open questions - the sign of the dispersion-to-relative-return mapping, and whether the slow-clock construction has any net-of-cost value - in either direction. Two adjacent results are recorded in item 15 and neither is on point: Kitron and Wengrowicz's flat funding-and-basis conditioning across 183 Binance pairs (single venue, so not the cross-venue dispersion measure) and the confirmation that Binance funding history is now available as a dated free archive, which lowers the cost of building the entry's long history without solving the cross-venue half. Because the entry's score is unchanged while CRYPTO-EDGE-001 fell from 61 to 58, the margin at rank 1 narrows from 6 points to 3 points; that is a consequence of the other entry moving, not of any new evidence on this one, and it is a reason to treat the rank-1 ordering as unsettled rather than as a finding. Rank 2: it leads CRYPTO-EDGE-004 by 10 points, but its case now rests on clean identification and a *contested but unexcluded* mechanism rather than on any evidence of predictive power, and the decisive change this cycle is that the construction, not the claim, is what now has to earn its place. **Tenth cycle: downgraded 55 -> 52 on evidence quality alone, 2/5 -> 1/5 (contribution 6 -> 3); every other dimension is held.** Causal mechanism 3/5 -> 12, evidence quality 1/5 -> 3, robustness 2/5 -> 6, free-data accessibility 4/5 -> 12, net value after frictions 1/5 -> 3, falsifiability 4/5 -> 8, originality 4/5 -> 8; total 52/100, confidence low. Rationale for the move, stated so that it can be audited and disagreed with: the evidence-quality dimension measures the quality and independence of the evidence that bears on the entry, and the tenth cycle's reading of the preregistered tier is that this entry now has **no supporting evidence and three independent adverse findings**. The adverse findings are not about the entry's sign (which remains untested externally so far) but about the entry's *standing*: H66 documents that selecting the top K funding names is monotonically dominated by harvesting all of them (Sharpe 2.27 / 2.99 / 4.06 for K = 3 / 5 / 10 against 5.60 for harvest-all on the same 34 symbols and window, with drawdown -5.52% against -0.76%); H70 documents the same distinction arriving from the opposite direction, that rotation selects and carry harvests, so the entry's threshold is a selection rule and selection is the form the evidence treats as weaker; and H72 documents that the harvest form earns approximately zero in 2025-2026 on a point-in-time universe as well as a hindsight one, with its income halving even in the years when it was paid. The catalogue's own cost test of this entry's registered threshold construction already found a net loss of -9% to -15% annualised, so the entry's only distinct content over the patient-carry construction that the cost evidence selects toward is a rule that three independent findings now say not to use. Everything else is held deliberately: mechanism 3/5 (the segmented-arb-without-cross-margining description is a published finding and nothing new contradicts it); robustness 2/5 (the sign remains contested and the closest adjacent test points the other way, which is unchanged); free-data accessibility 4/5 (Binance funding history is a dated free archive and the entry's inputs remain confirmed free, though the cross-venue half is still REST-only); net value after frictions 1/5 (already at its floor, and the tenth cycle's new evidence could only have pushed it lower - the floor is the reason the move is recorded on the evidence dimension instead); falsifiability 4/5 (the cycle-10 specification is materially stronger, because the incremental bar is now prior art in the same family rather than a placebo, and the sign is still to be estimated two-sided before any trade expression); originality 4/5 (the entry remains differentiated from CRYPTO-EDGE-001 by differencing out the market-wide carry and momentum component, and no duplicate exists in the catalogue). Rank 2 is retained, now by 7 points over CRYPTO-EDGE-004, and the entry's status is unchanged - not rejected, because its registered prediction has never been tested and failed - but its priority for a validation slot has fallen, **Eleventh cycle: no change. The total stays at 52/100 with every dimension held**, and the current contributions are: causal mechanism 3/5 -> 12, evidence quality 1/5 -> 3, robustness 2/5 -> 6, free-data accessibility 4/5 -> 12, net value after frictions 1/5 -> 3, falsifiability 4/5 -> 8, originality 4/5 -> 8 (sum 52) — for the same reason the tenth cycle held the evidence-quality dimension rather than moving another one: the cycle's findings bear on the entry's construction and on the identity of the forced-unwinder, not on the entry's registered venue-relative mapping, which is still untested externally. Mechanism 3/5 (the segmented-arb-without-cross-margining description is a published finding and nothing new contradicts it); evidence quality 1/5 (already at its floor; the eleventh cycle found no new support and no new adverse test of the entry's own mapping); robustness 2/5 (the sign remains contested and the closest adjacent test points the other way, unchanged); free-data accessibility 4/5 (the Binance leg has a free dated archive route and the cross-venue legs are REST-only and partly unreachable from this environment, which is an access note and not an accessibility change); net value after frictions 1/5 (already at its floor; the delisting-settlement rule removes an artefact rather than adding value, and the tenth cycle's cost evidence is unchanged); falsifiability 4/5 (the v1.4 delisting-settlement rule makes the construction implementable and removes a confound, and the paired prior-art bar is unchanged); originality 4/5 (unchanged; the entry remains differentiated from CRYPTO-EDGE-001 by differencing out the market-wide carry and momentum component). **A recorded disagreement about the tenth cycle's downgrade placement, as the handoff required.** The tenth cycle moved evidence quality 2/5 to 1/5 on the strength of three adverse preregistered findings (H66, H70, H72) about the *form* of the construction - select versus harvest - and about the *availability* of an alternative construction. On a strict reading of the rubric, that is not what the dimension measures: the dimension asks about the quality and independence of the evidence that bears on the entry's registered prediction, and on that reading the score should already have been 1/5, because **there has never been any supporting evidence for this entry's specific predictive mapping** - the entry's v1.1 through v1.3 text says so repeatedly, and the tenth cycle's own wording admits that the adverse findings are "not about the entry's sign". The correct description of the tenth cycle's move is therefore that it was a **correction of an earlier mis-score that was carried through v1.1, v1.2 and the ninth cycle**, not a consequence of the new evidence. The number the tenth cycle reached is right; the route it took was a shorthand that a future reader could mis-apply to other entries. This is recorded so that the *reason* for the 1/5 is unambiguous: **this entry has no supporting evidence, and its three adverse findings are additional grounds for not spending a validation slot on it, not the reason the dimension is 1/5.** A reader who prefers to argue that the adverse findings belong on the robustness dimension (2/5) would land on 55/100 rather than 52/100, and that alternative is recorded here rather than dismissed; the eleventh cycle does not adopt it, because the tenth cycle's evidence-quality move is the more conservative placement and the catalogue has no reason to reverse a conservative move without new evidence, and there is none. Rank 2 is retained, by 7 points over CRYPTO-EDGE-004, with the margin unchanged from the tenth cycle. Rank 2 is retained, now by 7 points over CRYPTO-EDGE-004, and the entry's status is unchanged - not rejected, because its registered prediction has never been tested and failed - but its priority for a validation slot has fallen, because the tenth cycle established that the entry must beat a documented alternative construction in its own family, not merely a placebo.

**Fourteenth cycle: no dimension moves; the total is 52, re-derived as 12+3+6+12+3+8+8.** The cycle's findings are **access determinations**, and the entry's registered prediction — the venue-relative *price* differential conditional on extreme funding dispersion, on a 5-20 day horizon — is untouched by them: no source in this cycle tests the prediction, and no source in this cycle supports it either, so the mechanism (3/5), evidence-quality (1/5), robustness (2/5), net-value (1/5), falsifiability (4/5) and originality (4/5) dimensions are all unchanged, and free-data accessibility is discussed below. **A recorded disagreement on free-data accessibility, which the cycle declines to act on.** A reader could argue free-data accessibility 4/5 → **5/5** (total **55**, +3) on the ground that the *reason* the score was below the maximum — the missing second leg of the cross-venue construction — has now been removed: Hyperliquid's public info API supplies a second venue's **free, dated, keyless** funding history from 2023-05-11 and daily price history from 2023-05-12, so both legs of the entry's core construction are now obtainable without cost, credential or registration, and a retail-scale validator can run the test today. The cycle declines the move, and records the reason. (i) **The free second venue is not the specified second venue.** The entry's specified universe is USDT-margined centralised perpetuals on Binance, Bybit and OKX; the free second venue is a **USDC-margined on-chain decentralised exchange**, which differs in collateral, in venue class, in participant base and in the very dispersion persistence the entry's own sub-claim depends on — the entry already records that a CEX-versus-DEX spread reverts in 1.5-3.5 hours against a more persistent CEX-versus-CEX spread, so the free route runs the pair on which the persistence sub-claim is *most likely to fail*. (ii) **The centralised second leg is now determined unobtainable free**, which is the opposite of what the thirteenth cycle recorded: OKX's public funding endpoint returns about 33 days and ignores its pagination parameters, and Bybit's public archive has no funding or klines at all, so the *specified* construction is no closer to free than it was, and the catalogue's thirteenth-cycle sentence asserting the OKX route is superseded in place. (iii) **One of the entry's own mandatory controls is not free on the second venue**: the item 9(iii) liquidity filter uses venue volume *and* open interest, and free dated open interest is not available for the second venue (requester-pays asset contexts, current-only REST), so a free run is not a complete run of the entry's plan. On that basis **4/5 is held, the total stays at 52, and the alternative placement of 55 is recorded here rather than dismissed.** The entry's rank-2 position is unchanged, by 7 points over CRYPTO-EDGE-004.

**Amended in v1.7 (fifteenth cycle — the venue-pair question is answered, and the answer is a revision rather than a substitution; and the free second venue is now known to be the pair on which the entry's own source documents the construction losing money).** (i) **The pair question is answered, by the entry's own recorded evidence.** The entry records that a CEX-versus-DEX spread's *level* mean-reverts with an AR(1) half-life of 1.5-3.5 hours while its *direction* is a regime lasting months worth 8.5-11.4% annualised, and that "the same measurement between two centralised venues is centred on zero". A 5-20 day prediction horizon is 40-320 half-lives away from the free pair's level, so on the CEX-versus-DEX pair the registered trigger state does not persist to the horizon; and on the CEX-versus-CEX pair, where the level would be the relevant quantity, the spread is centred on zero, so an "extreme" reading is a rare event whose direction is closer to a noise-driven coin flip than to a dislocation. The two readings together mean the free pair measures a *structural venue-class carry differential* rather than a fragmentation dislocation, and the centralised pair is where the entry's claim is coherent. **The entry's specified universe is therefore not substitutable by a CEX-versus-DEX pair; the claim must be narrowed to two centralised venues, or the entry must be reframed as a carry trade — which is the prior art it is already paired against.** The independent multi-venue survey located in this cycle is consistent: the mean CEX-versus-DEX funding difference is reported as negligible (0.18 basis points) with a CEX-versus-DEX correlation of 0.175 against CEX-versus-CEX correlations above 0.4, and a separate significance-tested study of a DEX against two centralised venues reports a persistent, structurally signed differential of roughly 7% annualised (14.7% in 2024, 4.6% in 2025) with no measured reversion horizon — a carry, not a dispersion. (ii) **The free route is now known to be the wrong route, for a sharper reason than the fourteenth cycle had.** The free second venue is a decentralised exchange, and the entry's own source is the study that measured the thresholded rule on exactly that kind of pair and found it **finishes at -9% to -15% annualised after costs**, while the unthresholded patient carry on the same study returns +2.7% to +5.4%. A free validator using the free pair would therefore be running the registered construction on the one pair where the entry's own evidence says it loses, and the entry's net-value dimension is scored on exactly that comparison. (iii) **The registered prediction, the sign estimate, the slow clock, the mandatory paired comparison and the reversed-sign placebo are unchanged**, and the item 14 venue-pair pre-commitment is retained and strengthened: a validator must name the pair, its collateral and its venue class, must report the persistence sub-claim **on the pair actually run**, and must report the thresholded rule against the unthresholded patient carry on the same window, cost model and inference. No empirical validation was performed in this repository.

**Fifteenth cycle: no dimension moves; the total is 52, re-derived as 12+3+6+12+3+8+8.** The cycle's findings are **literature and access determinations** and none of them tests the entry's registered prediction — the venue-relative *price* differential conditional on extreme funding dispersion on a 5-20 day horizon — so the mechanism (3/5), evidence-quality (1/5), robustness (2/5), net-value (1/5), falsifiability (4/5) and originality (4/5) dimensions are all unchanged. **Two recorded disagreements now sit on this entry's free-data dimension, pulling in opposite directions, and the fourteenth cycle's is answered.** (a) The fourteenth cycle recorded that a reader could argue free-data accessibility 4/5 -> **5/5** (total **55**) because a free, dated, keyless second venue now exists, and declined it on the ground that the free second venue is a different collateral and a different venue class, that the centralised second leg is determined unobtainable free, and that the entry's own mandatory liquidity filter needs dated venue open interest that is not free on the second venue. **This cycle's evidence answers that argument against the upgrade**: the free second venue is a decentralised venue, and the entry's own source documents that the CEX-versus-DEX spread's level reverts within 1.5-3.5 hours and that the thresholded construction on that pair loses 9% to 15% annualised after costs, so the free route does not deliver a *testable* version of the entry's registered construction; the third row of the fourteenth cycle's reasoning is therefore no longer the decisive one and is replaced by the first. (b) **A new recorded disagreement, which the cycle also declines: free-data accessibility 4/5 -> 3/5 (total **49**), three points downward.** A reader could argue that a free pair on which the entry's own source documents the trigger state reverting in hours, and on which the construction is documented to lose money after costs, is not "realistic retail-level testability" of the claim, and that the coherent pair (two centralised venues) is determined unobtainable free, so the entry's free-data score should fall. The cycle declines the move and records the reason: the dimension asks whether the *essential input* is constructible on free, dated data, and it is — the free leg's funding and price history are free, dated and deep, and the entry's item 14 already requires the pair, its collateral and its venue class to be named and the persistence sub-claim to be reported on the pair actually run, so a free run is a *complete and self-describing* run of the entry's plan; the residual objection is an **identification** objection (this pair does not measure the claim's phenomenon) and not an access objection, and the catalogue's discipline is to hold identification objections on the construction and in the validation plan rather than to price them into an access score. **4/5 is held, the total stays at 52, and the alternative placement of 49 is recorded here rather than dismissed.** The entry's rank-2 position is unchanged, by 7 points over CRYPTO-EDGE-004.
**Thirteenth cycle: no dimension moves; the total is 52, re-derived as 12+3+6+12+3+8+8.** The cycle's four new sources bear on the entry's *construction and cost*, not on its registered prediction, and the falsification-handling rule is therefore not engaged — the entry is neither downgraded for a failed test nor re-specified. Three of the entry's dimensions are already at or near their floors for reasons that this cycle corroborates rather than disturbs: evidence quality 1/5 (there is still no supporting evidence for the registered mapping, and there are now four independent adverse or null findings in the family), robustness 2/5 (the sign remains untested and the construction's cost profile is documented as adverse), and net value after frictions 1/5 (the thresholded construction is now documented to lose money after costs in two independent tests while the unthresholded alternative is documented to make money, so the entry's *specific* payoff is the one with the weaker evidence). Mechanism 3/5 is held, and this is the placement a reader is most likely to contest: the new sources do support the entry's *first link* — cross-venue funding dispersion is a real, persistent, structural bias that is worth harvesting if you are patient — and a reader could argue mechanism 3/5 → 4/5 (total 56, +4), which would narrow the gap to CRYPTO-EDGE-001 from 6 points to 2. The cycle declines the move and records the reason: the mechanism score is for the **whole causal chain** from dispersion to an unpriced venue-specific imbalance to the venue's subsequent *underperformance*, and the new sources support only the first link while documenting that the chain's payoff is cost-fragile, and none of them speaks to the *price* mapping at all. Free-data accessibility 4/5 is held, and the cycle notes that the archive has now removed one of the reasons it was not 5/5 — the cross-venue leg is constructible from Binance's dated funding-rate archive on one side and OKX's public REST on the other — but the missing leg is a *second venue's* free dated history for the same window, which the archive cannot supply, and Binance's own REST remains HTTP 451. Falsifiability 4/5 and originality 4/5 are untouched.
**Seventeenth cycle: ONE dimension moves — free-data accessibility 4/5 -> 5/5 — and the total moves from 52 to 55, re-derived as 12+3+6+15+3+8+8. The move is an access determination, and the cycle records why that is the one dimension it can move.** The cycle's decisive finding is that **the entry's registered construction is now constructible on free, dated data for the first time**: a **USDT-margined, centralised-venue pair**, which is what the entry specifies. A second centralised venue publishes a **dated, per-instrument, multi-year perpetual funding-rate archive and a daily per-instrument candlestick archive** at `https://www.okx.com/cdn/okex/traderecords/` — funding monthly for every instrument from **2021-01 to 2025-09** and daily per instrument to **2025-09-05**, candles daily per instrument from **2022-01 to the present** — access 1, keyless, no registration, verified live on 2026-10-20 and reproducible from the recorded URL templates. **This supersedes in place the catalogue's recorded determination that "the centralised-venue second leg is not obtainable free on a multi-year window",** which rested on the venue's REST endpoint alone (`funding-rate-history` returns exactly 100 settlements, about 33 days, and ignores its pagination parameters — that determination is still true *of that route* and is now correctly recorded as a statement about a route rather than about the publisher). **Every other dimension is unchanged**, and the standing rule is applied unchanged: the cycle's evidence about *what is constructible* bears on the free-data dimension and on no other. The mechanism (3/5), evidence-quality (1/5), robustness (2/5), net-value (1/5), falsifiability (4/5) and originality (4/5) dimensions are all untouched, and in particular the entry's evidence quality does **not** move, because the phenomenon-level evidence in this cycle runs *against* the entry and is recorded below as the pre-registered prior rather than smuggled into an access score. **The cycle's decision on the entry's open question — specification or revision — is a SPECIFICATION, and the reason is now sharp.** (i) *Why not a revision.* The revision path available to the entry is to narrow it to the free decentralised-venue pair, and that path is closed by the entry's own source: the level of the decentralised-venue spread **reverts within 1.5-3.5 hours** and the thresholded construction **loses 9% to 15% annualised after costs** on that pair, while the entry's registered horizon is **5 to 20 days** — forty to three hundred and twenty half-lives away. A revision would therefore specify a construction whose own documentation says it does not work, and would additionally move the entry's universe from USDT-margined centralised perpetuals to a USDC-margined decentralised venue, changing both the collateral and the venue class and importing the entry's own recorded collateral-mix and depeg confounds. (ii) *Why a specification.* With the pair now free, dated and of the entry's own venue class and collateral, the entry's registered prediction — the venue-relative **price** differential conditional on extreme funding dispersion over 5 to 20 days — becomes **testable for the first time**, on a window of 2022-01-01 to 2025-09-05 that covers the post-July-2024 regime the catalogue has repeatedly identified as the untested one. A revision is a statement that the entry should be re-framed; a specification is a statement that the entry can now be run as written. The entry has never been tested, and it is now runnable, so the right act is to fix the construction and run it. (iii) **The specification is fixed as follows, and these are additions to item 14.** *The pair is pre-registered as Binance USDⓈ-M linear perpetual against OKX USDT-margined swap, on the same asset, same collateral (USDT), same venue class (centralised),* over 2022-01-01 to 2025-09-05 — the intersection of the two funding archives and of the OKX candlestick archive. *The trigger, the horizon, the two-sided sign-free estimation, the mandatory paired comparison against the unthresholded patient carry on the same clock, window, cost model and inference with a pre-committed redundancy null, the mandatory persistence sub-claim reported on the pair actually run, and the mandatory declaration of the collateral and venue-class difference* all stand unchanged from v1.4 and v1.6. *Three clauses are added.* (A) **The pre-registered prior against the hypothesis must be reported.** The entry's own source, re-read live, states that "the same measurement between two centralised exchanges is centred on zero with a sign that is a coin flip (41-47% stability)" and that "two venues of the same type do not persistently disagree; an exchange and a DEX do". That is a dated, method-stated **negative control on the very pair the specification now names**, and the expected outcome of the entry is accordingly **null or worse**. It is recorded here as the prior, and it is not a score movement, because the source does not test the entry's registered prediction — it speaks to the spread's *persistence and sign*, not to the venue-relative *price* differential — and because the entry's evidence-quality dimension is already at its floor of 1/5. (B) **The liquidity filter's second-leg substitute must be declared.** The second venue's archive publishes **no dated open-interest product** (`openinterest`, `open-interest`, `open_interest` and `oi` all return HTTP 404) and its Rubik open-interest endpoint rejects windows of 30 days or more from this environment, so the entry's mandatory liquidity filter must use a declared substitute on that leg — the venue's own turnover from its candlestick archive — with the asymmetry between the two legs stated in advance. (C) **The coverage limit must be stated.** The second venue's funding archive ends 2025-09-05, so the newest regime is not covered by the archive and cannot be constructed retrospectively; the entry's window is therefore 2022-01-01 to 2025-09-05, and the post-2025-09 window can only be accumulated prospectively. **Both recorded disagreements on this entry's free-data dimension are now answered, and they are answered in opposite directions from each other.** The fourteenth cycle's recorded upgrade (4/5 -> 5/5, total 55) was declined on three grounds: that the free second venue was a different collateral and venue class; that the centralised second leg was determined unobtainable free; and that the mandatory liquidity filter needs dated venue open interest that is not free on the second venue. **The first two grounds are now removed** — the free second venue is a centralised, USDT-margined venue with a dated, multi-year archive — and the third survives only in the modified form recorded as clause (B) above, which is a *declared substitution*, not an access obstruction. The fifteenth cycle's recorded downgrade (4/5 -> 3/5, total 49) was declined on the ground that the residual objection was an **identification** objection rather than an access objection; that reasoning is vindicated, because the identification objection is now attached to a pair that is *constructible*, and it is discharged where it belongs — in the specification's pre-registered prior, not in an access score. **The total is 55, the entry is now 3 points behind CRYPTO-EDGE-001 and 7 points ahead of CRYPTO-EDGE-004, and the alternative placements of 49 and 55 are both recorded rather than dismissed.** The entry's rank-2 position is unchanged, and the cycle records honestly that the move is an access move made on a negative-control-bearing pair: the catalogue's own standing prior — that the crash process and the funding process are first-order and shock-driven, and that a *selection* rule on a documented near-random trigger is dominated by a *harvest* rule on the same stream — is untouched by it, and it remains the entry's most likely outcome.

17. Next most valuable research question: does extreme cross-venue funding dispersion predict relative *underperformance* of the expensive venue at all, and in which direction - is the sign inverted relative to the registered prediction, so that the expensive venue outperforms and the entry should be rewritten as a continuation hypothesis or rejected? The first step remains a two-sided, sign-free estimation of the venue-relative return conditional on extreme funding dispersion, on a sample with enough extreme episodes to have power, before any trade expression is specified; the new element is that the sample must be built on a *slow clock* - a trailing-window measure of which venue has been persistently expensive - because the dispersion level has a documented one-to-three-hour half-life and a trailing-decile threshold on it is close to trading noise. A second and separable question is whether the dispersion-persistence sub-claim (no full closure within one to three funding intervals) holds, and the answer is now known to be venue-pair dependent rather than simply unknown: a CEX-versus-DEX spread reverts in 1.5-3.5 hours while a CEX-versus-CEX spread is more persistent, so the sub-claim should be tested per venue pair with the pair fixed in advance. A third question, now the most consequential, is the one the family precedent answers badly: does the slow-clock construction have any net-of-cost value at retail scale, given that the threshold construction has a documented net loss and that the documented positive result comes from a construction the entry does not specify? If the answer is no, the entry is closed regardless of the sign, and that is a stronger claim of failure than a wrong-signed return test would be. **Ninth-cycle note on the first step:** the mandatory two-sided, sign-free estimation is now materially cheaper to specify, because Binance funding history is available as a dated free archive (data/futures/um/monthly/fundingRate/, monthly files per symbol from January 2020 for BTCUSDT) rather than only through REST pagination, and because the archive's symbol set includes delisted contracts, so a slow-clock construction that must survive venue delisting can be built on it; the cross-venue half still requires Bybit and OKX REST history, and the ninth cycle confirmed that the entry's two questions remain open rather than answered. **Tenth-cycle note:** the third
question above - whether the slow-clock construction has any net-of-cost value - now has a documented answer in the wrong direction, and it sharpens the first question rather than replacing
it. The harvest alternative that the cost evidence selects toward is now measured on a point-in-time universe: it retains 86% of its Sharpe (5.61 to 4.83) and keeps a better drawdown
(-0.78% to -0.35%), but its CAGR halves (+4.36% to +2.35%) and it earns approximately zero in 2025-2026 in every arm - the fifth independent confirmation of that finding after H62, H63 and
H65 - and the selection form that this entry actually specifies is documented to be dominated by that harvest form monotonically, in Sharpe and in drawdown alike. The entry's
net-of-cost value question is therefore no longer open in the abstract; it is answered *unless* the entry can show incremental content over the harvest-all construction on the same
clock, in the same window, with the same costs and the same inference. The tenth cycle re-specifies the first step accordingly: the mandatory two-sided, sign-free estimation must be run
as a **paired comparison** - the entry's threshold rule against the unthresholded patient-carry rule on the same slow clock - and the entry is closed regardless of the sign if the paired
comparison fails, because a sign that merely reproduces the harvest rule is not the entry's content. Two questions remain genuinely open: the sign (nothing outside the catalogue has
tested the venue-relative mapping, and the closest adjacent test points the other way), and whether the slow clock can be built at all from free data (Binance's funding archive is dated
and free, the cross-venue half is still REST-only, and the venue-pair structure of the persistence sub-claim must be fixed in advance). A third question the tenth cycle raises: the
*delisting* sign of a cross-venue relative-value book is documented as unknown in the same preregistered tier that documents the harvest numbers, because contracts delisted after a
collapse are absent from a point-in-time pool - so a validator must report the entry's res **Eleventh-cycle update: the next question has been sharpened by a newly available free construction, and part of it has been answered.** The tenth cycle's next question was whether the sign of the venue-relative mapping can be estimated two-sided on a slow clock, and what the net-of-cost value of the slow-clock construction is; the eleventh cycle's answer to the second half is that the harvest alternative is the incumbent and the entry must beat it head-to-head, which stands. The eleventh cycle adds a third question that is now answerable and is recorded as the highest-value open one: **what is the sign of a cross-venue relative-value book through a venue-announced delisting settlement?** The preregistered tier recorded this as unknown, and the eleventh cycle's answer is structural rather than empirical. Because the venue settles the contract against the index average over a disclosed pre-settlement window (30 minutes on Bybit, 60 minutes on Coinbase, 09:00 UTC with a 08:30 UTC restriction time on Binance USDⓈ-M), the venue-specific basis at settlement is **mechanically zero**, so a cross-venue book cannot be holding a basis position through the settlement at all: it must be flat on the delisting venue by the restriction time. The "sign of the delisting" is therefore not an empirical question about a market move but a **mechanical question about position hygiene**, and the answer is that there is no position to have a sign on. That closes the question in the only way it can be closed without running a test, and it is why the v1.4 specification makes the exclusion and forced-flat rule mandatory. The remaining open question for the entry is the one the tenth cycle named, unchanged: does a *threshold* on cross-venue funding dispersion beat unthresholded patient carry on the same slow clock, window, cost model and inference, with the sign estimated two-sided before any trade expression is chosen? The eleventh cycle's research effort went into the delisting half because it was a blocking construction question that turned out to be answerable from primary venue documents, and because answering it was a precondition for the entry's construction being implementable at all. collapse are absent from a point-in-time pool - so a validator must report the entry's result both including and excluding delisted contracts and must not treat either as the answer.
**Thirteenth-cycle update: the paired comparison that the twelfth cycle listed as this entry's open question is now closed, and it is closed against the thresholded construction.** The twelfth cycle asked for the sign as a **paired comparison against unthresholded patient carry on the same clock, window, cost model and inference**. The comparison now exists in the literature, twice, in this entry's own family, and the answer is that the **unthresholded patient carry wins and the thresholded selection loses**: the patient rule returns +2.7% to +5.4% annualised net with a sub-2% drawdown where the threshold rule returns -9% to -15%, and the preregistered 11-gate test of the archetype retired at a profit factor of 0.107 with the diagnosis "dies on COST, not on no-edge".
