establishes that the nearest sibling of this entry's mechanism family is also a preregistered null; establishes no dispersion-specific result. martex-quant 'Hypothesis 11 - Cross-Sectional Momentum (Relative-Strength Rotation)' (docs/hypotheses/11-cross-sectional-rotation.md, verdicts 2026-07-11, corrected 2026-08-28), free GitHub raw, access 1 for the document; establishes that cross-sectional momentum passed an information test and then a strategy-grade test on a universe selected at the end of the sample, that the document itself recorded the survivorship caveat as open before the runs, and that the correction by H71 removed 42% of the Sharpe and 49% of the CAGR; establishes the base effect and its measurement fragility. CoinGecko public API, GET https://api.coingecko.com/api/v3/coins/bitcoin/market_chart?vs_currency=usd&days=max and GET /coins/bitcoin/market_chart/range, checked 2026-10-20 by direct fetch, access 3 for historical data (the free tier returns HTTP 401 with error_code 10012, 'Public API users are limited to querying historical data within the past 365 days'), access 1 for current fields via GET /coins/markets; establishes that no free historical market-cap or market-cap-rank series back to 2019 is obtainable from CoinGecko, which is the binding constraint on the entry's universe rule. CoinLore public API, https://api.coinlore.net/api/ticker/?id=90 and /api/coin/markets/?id=90, checked 2026-10-20 by direct fetch, access 1 for current market cap and supply, access 3 for history (the chart endpoint returns HTTP 404); establishes that CoinLore is a current-only free source and is not a substitute for historical market cap. Label update (v1.1): the dispersion state-dependence remains a published, methodologically explicit working paper; its own closest preregistered test is a null with an adverse point estimate; the base effect it conditions is now measured to be materially weaker on an honest universe, and the entry's essential universe input is not fully constructible on free data.
**Thirteenth-cycle sources (2026-10-20).**
(a) **Han, Chulwoo; Kang, Byeongguk; Ryu, Jehyeon, "Momentum in the Cryptocurrency Market: A Comprehensive Analysis under Realistic Assumptions", *The Review of Asset Pricing Studies*, published 13 October 2026, doi 10.1093/rapstu/raag011** — peer-reviewed; the publisher's article page returned HTTP 403 to automated fetch on 2026-10-20 and the abstract was therefore read from the Crossref record (`https://api.crossref.org/works/10.1093/rapstu/raag011`), which is complete for the title, authors, journal, publication date and abstract. *Establishes* the findings quoted in item 13, on a survivorship-aware sample with daily marking to market and liquidation risk accounted for. *Does not establish* anything about the entry's registered state-dependence claim, and *supersedes in place* the catalogue's eleventh-cycle citation of the same authors' 2024 working paper on the base effect, which is now published: the working-paper version is now a preprint of a peer-reviewed article and the entry's citation should be upgraded.
(b) **Zhang, Cong; Makgolo, Olorato MacDonald, "Cross-Sectional Dispersion and the State Dependence of Cryptocurrency Momentum"**, SSRN 6648082, 25 April 2026 (a second SSRN DOI, 10.2139/ssrn.6648058, 1 January 2026, appears for the same work) — the entry's own working paper, re-read this cycle; *establishes* the entry's own abstract claims (that lagged cross-sectional dispersion rather than generic market volatility is the variable most closely associated with subsequent breakdown; that dispersion predicts weaker later momentum returns after controlling for Bitcoin realized volatility and average cross-asset correlation; that the effect is nonlinear and concentrated in the upper tail of dispersion; and that inference is sensitive to dynamic universe construction) and *does not establish* any independent test.
(c) **Public executable replication of the entry's construction** (a QuantConnect terminal cache and the corresponding Quantpedia strategy page; access 1) — as described in item 10(a); establishes the construction's public availability and its paid data dependency, and does not establish an independent test.
(d) **Xu and Wu, size-momentum structure in cryptocurrencies** — encountered only through a practitioner summary on 28 April 2026; the underlying publication was **not** located and verified in this cycle, so it is recorded as a **lead for the next cycle, not as evidence**, and no score moves on it. **Fourteenth-cycle verification of this lead, marked superseded in place: the publication is located and dated, and it is a working-paper preprint.** **Xu, Zezhou and Wu, Fenglin, "Size-Momentum Puzzle in Cryptocurrencies", SSRN working paper 6628860, published 1 January 2026** (authors affiliated with Zhejiang Police College and Shanghai Jiao Tong University; free full text at papers.ssrn.com; access 1). *Establishes*, on data spanning 2018 to 2025, that the **smallest size quintile shows a weekly reversal spread of 4.9%** while the **largest quintile shows approximately +1.0% weekly momentum**, with the large-cap continuation persisting to roughly **+4.0% over four weeks** — that is, that the **sign of the crypto momentum spread is a function of the universe's size composition**, with small coins reversing and large coins continuing. *Does not establish* any test of this entry's registered state-dependence claim, and its **peer-review standing is that of an unreviewed preprint**, so the catalogue's treatment of it as corroborating rather than decisive is unchanged. *Bears on* this entry because the free screen and the published screen select different size populations, and the difference is now documented to be **sign-changing** rather than merely magnitude-changing. **Two independent corroborations, both stronger in standing, are now recorded alongside it:** **Liu, Tsyvinski and Wu, "Common Risk Factors in Cryptocurrency", NBER working paper w25882** (free PDF, access 1) — *establishes* that the momentum quintile spread is +2.7% to +4.1% weekly over one to four weeks and increasing with momentum formation, and that a **size factor that is long the smallest and short the largest quintile earns +3.4% weekly**, i.e. small coins have systematically *lower* forward returns than large ones, which is the same direction as a reversal in the smallest quintile; and **Kozlowski, Puleo and Zhou, "Cryptocurrency return reversals"** (Fairfield working paper, free) — *establishes* that crypto return reversals are more pronounced in **smaller and less liquid** coins. *Does not establish* any test of this entry's registered claim.
(e) **Kitron and Wengrowicz (2026), "Short-horizon mean reversion in cryptocurrency markets"**, arXiv:2608.21888v1 (free full HTML) — *establishes*, on 183 Binance pairs against 187 US stocks and ETFs under a matched out-of-sample protocol, that the reversal signal lives in signs rather than magnitudes, that it concentrates after moves driven by aggressive taker flow, that **order-book depth a move consumes conditions nothing**, that the book re-forms within fifteen minutes, that the gross edge peaks near 1.3 basis points per trade against a 5 basis-point round-trip cost, and — relevant to the catalogue's newly opened data question — that it uses **Binance's ~30-second cumulative bid and ask notional snapshots within 1-5% of mid**, which is the `bookDepth` tree the thirteenth cycle found independently in the archive. *Does not establish* any test of this entry's registered prediction.
16. Scores, total rank, confidence, explanation: causal mechanism 3/5 -> 12; evidence quality 2/5 -> 6; robustness 1/5 -> 3; free-data accessibility 3/5 -> 9; net value after frictions 1/5 -> 3; falsifiability 4/5 -> 8; originality 2/5 -> 4. Total 45/100, unchanged from the seventh cycle and down from 56. Rationale: causal mechanism 3/5 — the chain from disagreement to ranking unreliability to a collapsed momentum spread is coherent and has a published counterpart, but it is closer to a signal-processing argument than to a behavioural law, and the competing explanation (dispersion as a proxy for generic market state) is excluded only by the published orthogonalisation, which a free replication must redo. Evidence quality 3/5 — one published, methodologically explicit working paper with horse-race and panel specifications, no peer-reviewed version and no independent replication found, and the base effect it conditions is itself contested. Robustness 2/5 — the effect's own author states inference is sensitive to universe construction, the base effect is era-dependent and has power-law tails, and a free-data replication is more exposed to survivorship than the original. Free-data accessibility 3/5 — prices are confirmed free and documented (Binance public archive, access 1), but the point-in-time universe is the binding input and free historical market-cap snapshots are not confirmed, so access 2 applies to the essential input. Net value after frictions 2/5 — the incremental value of the switch over unconditional momentum is the entire hypothesis and is unproven, the base effect's mean may be unestimable, and the short leg's borrow and funding costs are real. Falsifiability 4/5 — a clean conditional prediction with a named null, named controls and explicit falsification criteria, and the controls are decisive in both directions. Originality 3/5 — differentiated from this catalogue and from common baselines (unconditional momentum, single-asset volatility filters), but the core state-dependence is published, so the contribution claimed is a free-data, preregisterable, heavy-tail-aware test design rather than a new claim. Confidence: low — the effect rests on a single non-peer-reviewed working paper, the base effect is contested on both existence and estimability, and the essential universe input is not confirmed free. Rank 3: it is the catalogue's only non-derivatives entry and therefore its main diversification asset, and it is ranked below CRYPTO-EDGE-003 because the underlying evidence is weaker and the base effect is less secure, not because the mechanism is less interesting. Changes in v1.1: evidence quality 3/5 -> 2/5, because the entry's published state-dependence now has a preregistered test of the same mechanism which was killed with a point estimate leaning the opposite way, plus a second killed preregistered sibling, plus a preregistered measurement audit showing that the base effect it conditions is materially weaker on an honest universe; the published source is a single non-peer-reviewed working paper with no replication, and the entry now also competes against prior art in the same preregistered tier. Robustness 2/5 -> 1/5, because the base effect's era-dependence (no momentum over 2014-2018) is now joined by a survivorship-dependence of the same order, and the closest conditional test is a null with the wrong-signed point estimate. Free-data accessibility 3/5 -> 2/5, because the essential input is now *determined* rather than merely unverified, and the determination is negative for the tested regime: the delisting half of survivorship is not observable on free data, and the market-cap screen that would be the natural substitute is not available in history either, so the entry cannot be tested as registered under the free-data standard. Net value after frictions is held at 2/5, because the value proposition of the switch is drawdown reduction on a momentum book that still has a documented positive mean on an honest universe (mean +6.165bp per day with a block-bootstrap interval excluding zero), and because the failure here is an evidence and measurability failure rather than an arithmetic failure of the idea. Falsifiability is held at 4/5: the design remains clean, two-sided, with named controls and explicit falsification criteria, and is arguably improved by the v1.1 pre-condition that the data question be answered before the plan is executed. Originality 3/5 -> 2/5, because the core state-dependence is published *and* now has a killed preregistered prior test, so the marginal contribution claimed here - a preregisterable free-data, survivorship-aware test design - is precisely the part that the free-data determination undermines. Mechanism is held at 3/5: the signal-to-noise and crowding channels are coherent and have a published counterpart, and nothing new contradicts the mechanism itself, only its measurability and its closest test. Confidence: low, unchanged. Status: deferred, not rejected - the entry's registered prediction has not been tested and failed, so the falsification-handling rule is not engaged, and the reinstatement condition in item 17 is a data-availability question with a binary answer and no performance calculation attached. The entry moves off the active shortlist, which means the catalogue has **no** active non-derivatives member; that is a real loss of diversification and is stated here rather than concealed. **Changes in v1.2 (ninth cycle): two dimensions move, in opposite directions, and the total is unchanged at 45/100.** Free-data accessibility 2/5 -> 3/5: the essential input, a survivorship-aware point-in-time crypto universe, is now constructible on free data from one documented venue archive, with exact listing dates and exact last-trading dates recoverable for 998 perpetual symbols, verified against two independently announced venue delistings and one re-listing; but it is not 4/5 or 5/5 because the published result's market-capitalisation screen still has no free history (CoinGecko Demo 365 days, Basic two years, Analyst and above the full range; CoinPaprika, CoinLore, DefiLlama and CoinMarketCap all confirmed current-only or paywalled for history), so a free replication must substitute listing-age and trailing-volume screens and is a *replication*, not an exact replication. Net value after frictions 2/5 -> 1/5: Arefev (2026, SSRN 7404139) measures the base cross-sectional momentum spread on a survivorship-aware, 832-symbol, tradable Binance perpetual universe over 2020-2026 and finds that neither permitted specification yields a net-of-costs spread distinguishable from zero, the gross spread of +0.573% per week being of the same order as 0.40pp per week of realistic costs, with the alternative specification flipping sign on an arbitrary rebalancing-phase choice; a switch on a spread that does not itself survive costs has less to act on than the seventh cycle assumed. Causal mechanism, evidence quality, robustness, falsifiability and originality are all held: the mechanism is untouched by the new data work; evidence quality stays at 2/5 because the closest preregistered test of the mechanism remains killed with an opposite-signed point estimate and the new cost-aware replication is adverse to the *base* effect rather than to the state-dependence claim; robustness stays at its floor of 1/5 because the era-dependence, the power-law tails and the published universe sensitivity are unchanged; falsifiability and originality are untouched. The entry is therefore neither promoted nor demoted: it is *unblocked*. It returns to the active shortlist at rank 3, and the catalogue regains an active non-derivatives member - which was the single largest structural gap recorded at the end of the eighth cycle. Confidence stays low, and the honest summary of the entry's position is that it is now testable and, on the current evidence, probably not worth much. **Tenth cycle: no change.** The total stays at 45/100 with every dimension held, and the arithmetic is re-derived: causal mechanism 3/5 -> 12, evidence quality 2/5 -> 6, robustness 1/5 -> 3, free-data accessibility 3/5 -> 9, net value after frictions 1/5 -> 3, falsifiability 4/5 -> 8, originality 2/5 -> 4. Three things happened and none of them moves a score. (i) The construction was adversarially re-verified and its *description* corrected: the rule survives, the terminal-state question is now a documented two-case fact rather than a single assumption, and the archive is now known to be versioned data with a public update log and per-file checksums, so free-data accessibility stays at 3/5 - the binding input is still the point-in-time universe, and no free market-capitalisation history appeared. (ii) Two new peer-reviewed sources bear on the entry's competing explanation: the Journal of Risk and Financial Management paper finds cross-sectional crypto signal reliability to be regime-dependent on a market-wide volatility regime, with the natural co-movement mechanism unsupported, and the Finance Research Letters paper finds crypto momentum itself to arise only in UP-UP state transitions. Both support the entry's *premise* and both make the choice of state variable contested, which is a competing-explanation sharpening rather than a change in the entry's own evidence. Evidence quality is therefore held at 2/5 - there is still no published test of the entry's own prediction and the closest preregistered test of the mechanism remains killed with an opposite-signed point estimate. (iii) The cycle-10 specification is now complete and preregisterable, including the two controls the new sources made necessary (the rival correlation state variable and the new-listing contamination control) and the archive-versioning rule; falsifiability is held at 4/5 rather than raised, because a better document is not new evidence and the entry's identification problem - a base spread that does not survive costs - is a property of the hypothesis rather than of its design. Rank 3, unchanged, and 7 points behind CRYPTO-EDGE-003. **Eleventh cycle: no change. The total stays at 45/100 with every dimension held, and the arithmetic is re-derived: causal mechanism 3/5 -> 12, evidence quality 2/5 -> 6, robustness 1/5 -> 3, free-data accessibility 3/5 -> 9, net value after frictions 1/5 -> 3, falsifiability 4/5 -> 8, originality 2/5 -> 4.** Four things happened and none of them moves a score, and one of them was a correction to the entry's own construction rather than to its claim. (i) **The universe construction was adversarially re-verified against an independent reference and its *description* was corrected twice.** The recorded listing-pagination rule is wrong, and following it would have produced false terminal dates on 20 of 20 sampled symbols; the terminal-state taxonomy is refuted in the form the tenth cycle gave it, because a delisted contract's file series continues with zero-volume files; and the archive's update log is not exhaustive as published and covers the spot tree rather than the futures tree. The *rule* (last trading date = last date with a non-zero trade count) survives and is now validated against the venue's own dated delisting notices on six contract instances across four notices, with exact agreement in every case, and the entry's universe can now be cross-checked against a second, independent record. Free-data accessibility is held at 3/5: the binding input is still the point-in-time universe and no free market-capitalisation history has appeared, and the corrections improve the *reliability* of the construction without changing the accessibility class. (ii) **The base effect's robustness is now better supported than the catalogue's worst case, and the score is still held - and the disagreement is recorded.** arXiv:2510.14435v4 shows crypto size, momentum and value smart beta robust on a survivorship-aware universe of 18,622 live plus 29,230 delisted coins, which is directly adverse to the catalogue's survivorship-dependence objection and to the H72 measurement that a momentum base effect retained only 42% of its Sharpe on a point-in-time universe. A reader could argue this raises robustness from 1/5 to 2/5, which would take the entry to 48/100 and leave it 4 points behind CRYPTO-EDGE-003 rather than 7. **The eleventh cycle does not make that move**, for a stated reason: the new source bears on the *base effect* on a *different universe with a different screen and a different asset class*, and the entry's robustness dimension is about the robustness of the *entry's registered state-dependence claim*, which still has no published test and two killed adjacent preregistered tests, and whose own base spread has been measured net of costs on a tradable universe and found not to be distinguishable from zero. Moving a score on favourable evidence about a different object is exactly the drift the process rules warn against, so the score is held and the counter-argument is recorded so it can be audited and disagreed with. Net value after frictions is held at 1/5 (already at its floor; the new survivorship-aware source does not overturn Arefev's net-of-costs result on tradable perpetuals, because it is a different universe and a different asset class, and the new Han-Kang-Ryu evidence is adverse to the base effect's tradability). (iii) **The state-variable question is now answered in a specific way and the answer is adverse to the entry's framing, without moving a score.** The dispersion variable has **no independent published test**: its only source is the entry's own working paper plus two killed preregistered dial tests. The two variables that *have* been independently tested on a comparable coin-level panel are the market-wide volatility regime and, in the Journal of Risk and Financial Management's own section 4.5, the average-pairwise-correlation state variable, which that paper tests directly as the candidate mechanism for the regime-dependence it finds and reports as unsupported. The Finance Research Letters state-transition result adds a third. And arXiv:2606.16840 establishes that the cross-asset tail-dependence structure is itself **time-varying with an undated sign flip**, so a correlation-based state variable is non-stationary and a volatility-regime variable is a summary of a non-stationary structure. The honest reading is therefore that CRYPTO-EDGE-004's claim is more likely **not the operative variable** than **wrong**, which is the framing the tenth cycle already adopted; evidence quality is held at 2/5 because there is still no published test of the entry's own prediction and the closest preregistered test of the mechanism remains killed with an opposite-signed point estimate; robustness is held at 1/5 (its floor) because the new non-stationarity result is another way of saying the same thing the floor already says. (iv) **Falsifiability is held at 4/5**, and the v1.4 specification is materially stronger because the pagination defect, the terminal-state rule and the update-log coverage are now corrected, the universe is cross-checked against an independent reference, and the two controls the tenth cycle added are joined by a third - the *terminal-state* control, because a contract's file series can continue for months after it stops trading, and a momentum ranking that treats a frozen, zero-volume contract as live would import a spurious return. The null outcome remains publishable. Rank 3, unchanged, and 7 points behind CRYPTO-EDGE-003.
**Thirteenth cycle: no dimension moves; the total is 45, re-derived as 12+6+3+9+3+8+4.** The cycle's evidence about this entry is adverse to its *base* and neutral to its *registered claim*, and the falsification-handling rule is not engaged because the registered claim has never been tested. Evidence quality 2/5 is held: the entry's own working paper is the only source for its variable and it now has a peer-reviewed *version* of its base-effect counterpart but still no independent test of the state-dependence mapping, and a public executable replication of its own construction is not an independent test. Robustness 1/5 is held, and the eleventh cycle's recorded non-move — the refusal to raise this dimension to 2/5 on a favourable survivorship-aware source about a different universe — is **better supported than when it was recorded**, because the base has since been documented as close to non-existent in a peer-reviewed source and a new size-composition confounder has been identified. Free-data accessibility 3/5 is held: the public executable replication establishes that the *published* construction depends on a paid market-cap tier and therefore that a free replication is a replication and not an exact replication, which confirms the existing determination rather than changing it. Net value after frictions 1/5 is held, and is now *evidenced* rather than merely inferred, since a peer-reviewed source finds many cross-sectional momentum portfolios are liquidated and those with significant mean returns often earn negative profits, and an independent 15-minute study puts the gross edge at 1.3 basis points per trade against a 5 basis-point round trip. Falsifiability 4/5 and originality 2/5 are untouched. **A recorded disagreement:** a reader could argue free-data accessibility 3/5 → 2/5 (total 43, -2) on the ground that the entry's *published* construction is not reproducible on free data at all, and the cycle declines because the determination is about whether a free replication of the hypothesis is possible, not about whether the published implementation is reproducible — the substitution of listing-age and trailing-volume screens for market capitalisation is available and dated, and the cycle records the size-composition confound it introduces rather than pretending the substitution is free of consequence.

**Fourteenth cycle: no dimension moves; the total is 45, re-derived as 12+6+3+9+3+8+4.** The cycle's evidence about this entry is **about its base, not about its registered state-dependence claim**, and the entry's three floors (robustness 1/5, net value after frictions 1/5, and the entry's confidence) are already at the bottom of their scales for reasons documented in v1.3 and v1.4, so nothing can fall; and no source in the cycle supports the registered claim, so nothing can rise. What the cycle changed is the **status of the size-composition confound, which the thirteenth cycle recorded as hypothetical and which is now documented three times over.** The size-momentum source is located and dated (SSRN 6628860, a working-paper preprint) and is corroborated by two stronger sources — a NBER working paper that finds small coins have systematically lower forward returns (the size factor, long the smallest and short the largest, earns +3.4% weekly) and a working paper that finds reversals are more pronounced in smaller and less liquid coins. The consequence is sharp and it is the reason no dimension moves rather than the reason one does: **the free screen and the published screen do not merely select differently sized populations, they can select populations whose base momentum spread has the opposite sign**, so a free replication's null is uninterpretable unless the base spread is estimated on the same population first and the comparison is made within a size stratum. That is now a **specification requirement** (item 14, v1.6) and a **null-outcome clarification**, not a score movement, because the dimension that would have to move is free-data accessibility and the entry's free replication remains *possible* — it is only uninterpretable if run without the two new requirements, and the cycle has specified it so that it is interpretable. **A recorded disagreement, restated and strengthened:** a reader could argue free-data accessibility 3/5 → 2/5 (total **43**, -2) on the ground that the free screen does not merely approximate the published screen but can select a population with a *different sign of the base effect*, so a free replication cannot test the entry's claim without the paid-tier screen, and the entry's published construction has already been documented to depend on that paid tier. The cycle still declines the move, and the reason is unchanged and now stronger: the free-data dimension asks whether the *essential input* of the claim — a point-in-time cross-sectional universe with a dispersion state and a momentum ranking — is constructible on free, dated data, and it is; the size-composition problem is a **confound within** the free construction, and the cycle's response to it is to specify the two requirements that make the free replication interpretable (declare the size composition, and estimate the base spread on the same population with a size-matched control), not to declare the input unobtainable. The alternative placement is recorded rather than dismissed. Rank 3, unchanged, and 7 points behind CRYPTO-EDGE-003.

**Amended in v1.7 (fifteenth cycle — the binding input is no longer unobtainable: a free, dated, point-in-time market-capitalisation panel exists, and the catalogue's recorded "unobtainable" determination is superseded in place).** (i) **The free point-in-time size proxy the thirteenth and fourteenth cycles said did not exist, does exist.** The Coin Metrics v4 **community** API (`https://community-api.coinmetrics.io/v4/timeseries/asset-metrics?assets=btc&metrics=CapMrktCurUSD`, access 1, keyless, HTTP 200, verified 2026-10-20) returns a **daily, dated, point-in-time market-capitalisation series** (`CapMrktCurUSD`) with history to genesis for the majors — BTC from 2010-08-24, ETH from 2015-11-06, LTC from 2013-05-20, DOGE from 2014-03-16, XRP from 2014-10-02, ADA from 2018-01-14, BNB from 2017-08-30 — and the asset catalogue shows **135 assets carrying a community-tier market-cap metric** against a full catalogue of 6,347 assets. This **supersedes in place** the catalogue's recorded determination that "historical market capitalisation and market-cap rank remain unavailable on every free tier the catalogue has checked (CoinGecko, CoinPaprika, CoinLore, CoinMarketCap, DefiLlama)", which is no longer true and must not be repeated. The routes tested and found empty in the same cycle remain empty and are recorded in SOURCE_NOTES.md: CoinGecko's free tier refuses historical queries (HTTP 429 on `days=90` and `days=365`), CoinPaprika's historical endpoint returns HTTP 402, CoinLore's coin endpoint HTTP 403, CoinMarketCap's historical endpoint HTTP 500, DefiLlama has no per-token historical supply endpoint (HTTP 404 on all five forms tried), and Blockchair, Etherscan v2, Dune, Santiment, Messari, Kaiko, Blockscout, mempool.space and Signals Analytics are key-gated or current-only. (ii) **The free panel's composition remains the binding limitation, and it is a limitation of *coverage*, not of *access*.** The 135-asset community panel is survivor-only, listing-biased and large-cap-skewed, so the smallest size quintile in which the published base effect is documented (the -4.9% weekly reversal in the smallest quintile against +1.0% in the largest) **is not reachable from it**, and the free screen therefore still cannot reproduce the published screen's size composition. A turnover proxy built from the venue's own daily klines (about 1,000 spot symbols, `quote_volume`) is available and dated, but it is a **liquidity** proxy, confounded with the very liquidity/reversal effect the size stratum is meant to control for, and must be declared as a proxy if used. The entry's v1.6 specification — declare the free universe's size composition, estimate the unconditional momentum spread on the same population, and compare **within a size stratum** — therefore survives intact and is now executable with a market-cap-based stratum for 135 assets rather than only a proxy-based one. (iii) **The entry's registered prediction, its null outcome, its unconditional-spread control and its size-matched control are unchanged**, and no empirical validation was performed in this repository.

**Fifteenth cycle: no dimension moves; the total is 45, re-derived as 12+6+3+9+3+8+4.** The cycle's finding about this entry is a **free-data finding about a confound control**, and the standing rule applies: data access does not bear on a registered prediction. The entry's three floors (robustness 1/5, net value after frictions 1/5, confidence) are already at the bottom of their scales for reasons documented in v1.3 and v1.4, and no source in the cycle supports the registered state-dependence claim, so nothing can fall and nothing can rise. **The fourteenth cycle's recorded disagreement on free-data accessibility (3/5 -> 2/5, total 43) is now answered, and a new disagreement in the opposite direction is recorded and declined.** (a) The fourteenth cycle declined a downgrade to 2/5 on the ground that the size-composition problem is a **confound within** the free construction and that the cycle's response was to specify the two requirements that make the free replication interpretable, not to declare the input unobtainable. **This cycle vindicates that reasoning with a source**: the size proxy is *not* unobtainable — the Coin Metrics community tier supplies a free, dated, daily market-capitalisation panel for 135 assets — so the fourteenth cycle's refusal to treat the size-composition problem as an access failure was correct, and the recorded disagreement is marked **answered against the downgrade**. (b) **A new recorded disagreement, which the cycle declines: free-data accessibility 3/5 -> 4/5 (total 48).** A reader could argue that the entry's "binding input" is now constructible on free, dated data with a genuine market-capitalisation measure rather than a proxy, so the free-data score should rise one notch. The cycle declines the move and records the reason: the free panel reaches 135 large and mid-cap assets, so the free screen still cannot enter the smallest size quintile in which the published base effect has its documented sign; the dimension asks whether the *essential input of the registered claim* — a point-in-time cross-sectional universe carrying a dispersion state and a momentum ranking — is constructible, which it was already and which this cycle's finding improves without changing; and the size control is a **confound control**, with the entry's specification for it unchanged and still requiring the comparison to be made within a size stratum. **3/5 is held, the total stays at 45, and the alternative placement of 48 is recorded here rather than dismissed.** Rank 3, unchanged, and 7 points behind CRYPTO-EDGE-003.
**Seventeenth cycle: ONE dimension moves — free-data accessibility 3/5 -> 4/5 — and the total moves from 45 to 48, re-derived as 12+6+3+12+3+8+4. The ground the fifteenth cycle recorded for declining the upgrade is falsified by direct measurement, and the upgrade is made on the measurement's own terms.** The fifteenth cycle declined a recorded upgrade (3/5 -> 4/5, total 48) on one stated ground: "the free panel reaches 135 large and mid-cap assets, so the free screen still cannot enter the smallest size quintile in which the published base effect has its documented sign". **That ground is false, and this cycle measured it rather than re-arguing it.** The free point-in-time market-capitalisation panel is the Coin Metrics v4 community tier's `CapMrktCurUSD` series for 135 assets of a 6,347-asset catalogue (the fifteenth cycle's counts reproduced exactly), and at 2026-10-19 **104 panel assets carry a market cap**: minimum **$0.29m**, 5th percentile **$1.63m**, 25th percentile **$48.7m**, median **$239m**, maximum **$1,646.6bn**. The reference screen's own current size profile — CoinGecko's keyless top 500, 500 rows fetched live — is rank 100 **$630m**, rank 200 **$169m**, rank 300 **$91m**, rank 400 **$61m**, rank 500 **$46m**, so the published screen's bottom quintile is the **$46m-$61m** band today. **32 of the 104 panel assets are below $61m and 26 are below $46m**, and every one of the 32 has a series still current to 2026-10-19. The free screen therefore **does enter, and goes below, the smallest size quintile in which the published base effect has its documented sign**, and the fifteenth cycle's ground is **superseded in place**. *The replacement ground, dated, and available only because of the measurement that produced it:* the free screen's small stratum is **a different population** from the published screen's. Its small-cap tail is largely deprecated tokens (ZIL, ALPHA, POLY, KNC, SRM, DRGN, REN, XEM, PERP, GRIN, ICX, VET, OMG, BAL, LRC, CVC, GNT, SNT, UMA, POWR, ELF) plus four stablecoins (GUSD, BUSD, LUSD, USDC_TRX), and a cross-check against CoinGecko for 60 matched assets gives a median ratio of **0.992** with 90% within 3x but individual divergences of up to about **90x** in the small tail (VET: Coin Metrics $6.74m against CoinGecko $614.0m; USDD_ETH $123.97m against $1,766.39m). So the free screen's *ranking* inside the small stratum is not the published screen's ranking. **That is a specification matter, and it is handled as one, on exactly the reasoning the fourteenth cycle used when it declined a downgrade on the ground that a size-composition problem is a confound *within* the free construction and not an access failure.** The free-data dimension asks whether the **essential input of the registered claim** is constructible on free, dated data; it now is, with a **genuine market-capitalisation measure** rather than a proxy, and the size stratum is enterable, so the score moves to 4/5 — the level the catalogue's ladder assigns to "the essential inputs are free and dated, with a declared population restriction" — and the residual composition problem is discharged in the specification, not priced into the score. **Three specification clauses are added to item 14 in consequence.** (i) The free screen must **exclude stablecoins** and must apply a **liveness rule** — the asset must have traded on the date — so that the smallest stratum is not composed of defunct tokens; the panel's own catalogue shows several of its small-cap assets' series ending (one at 2023-03-03, another at 2024-11-07), which is exactly the contamination the rule must exclude. (ii) The free screen's **size composition must be reported per date against the reference screen's**, and the comparison must be made **within a size stratum** with the base spread estimated on the same population first — the v1.6 requirement, now executable with a market-cap-based stratum for a panel whose small end is live. (iii) The **market-cap values must be cross-checked against a second source at the stratum's boundary**, because the panel's small-cap values are not interchangeable with the reference screen's and a mis-ranked boundary asset would move the sign of the very spread the entry is about. **Every other dimension is unchanged, and the standing rule is applied unchanged.** The cycle's evidence is about *access and about the composition of a screen*, and bears on a registered prediction nowhere: the mechanism (3/5), evidence-quality (2/5), robustness (1/5), net-value (1/5), falsifiability (4/5) and originality (2/5) dimensions are all untouched; robustness and net value are at their floors for reasons documented in v1.1 to v1.4, and no source in the cycle supports the registered state-dependence claim, so nothing can fall and nothing can rise. **The entry's registered prediction, its null outcome, its unconditional-spread control and its size-matched control are unchanged, and no empirical validation was performed in this repository: no return, Sharpe ratio, drawdown, hit rate or win rate was computed for any strategy.** The total is 48, the entry remains rank 3 and **7 points behind CRYPTO-EDGE-003** and 10 behind CRYPTO-EDGE-001; the alternative placements of 43 (the fourteenth cycle's declined downgrade) and 48 are both recorded rather than dismissed. The honest summary is unchanged in substance and better founded in method: the entry is now testable on free data, it is still probably not worth much, and the free screen's small stratum is the one place where the published result and the free replication are most likely to disagree.

17. Next most valuable research question: can any *free, dated, point-in-time* constructio **Eleventh-cycle update: the next question has changed character, and the reason is that the construction question has been answered.** The tenth cycle's next question was whether any free, dated, point-in-time construction of a survivorship-aware crypto universe can be built, and it is now answered: it can, its terminal-date rule is validated against the venue's own dated delisting notices on six contract instances with exact agreement, and its two silent-failure defects have been found and corrected. The next question is therefore no longer a construction question but a **comparative one**, and it is stated as three parts. (i) *Which state variable is operative, and is the entry's claim wrong or merely not the operative variable?* The eleventh cycle's answer so far: the dispersion variable has no independent published test at all; the volatility-regime and average-pairwise-correlation variables have both been tested on comparable coin-level panels, the latter directly and with a null result on the co-movement mechanism; and the cross-asset dependence structure is itself non-stationary with an undated sign flip, which is a further reason that a single "state variable" framing may be the wrong object. The highest-value remaining external work is to find whether **any** independent party has tested a *dispersion* state variable against a cross-sectional crypto signal, and if not, to decide whether the entry should be reframed from "dispersion is the operative variable" to "cross-sectional signal reliability is state-dependent and the operative variable is not yet identified" - which would be a **narrowing to a better-identified claim**, and would require the failed claim to be explicitly withdrawn under the falsification-handling rule. That reframing is *not* performed in this cycle, because the entry's registered prediction has not been tested and failed, so the rule is not engaged and the entry is not re-specified. (ii) *Does the base effect survive a free, point-in-time, cost-aware construction on a perpetual universe?* This is the question the catalogue's Arefev and H72 results bear on adversely, and the eleventh cycle's new survivorship-aware evidence (arXiv:2510.14435v4) bears on favourably but on a different universe; the honest position is that the answer is *unsettled and universe-dependent*, and a free replication must report which universe it used. (iii) *Is the archive's futures tree versioned at all?* The eleventh cycle established that the published update log covers the spot tree and that the log is not exhaustive as published, so the versioning status of the futures tree - the tree this entry and the other two active entries actually use - is unestablished. A cheap, decisive check remains open: enumerate the `updates/` directory in the binance-public-data repository and search its artefacts for futures paths, and record the result. 17. Next most valuable research question: can any *free, dated, point-in-time* construction of a crypto universe bound the delisting half of survivorship - that is, is there a free source of historical circulating supply, historical market capitalisation, or a dated delisting record for tokens that a major venue has delisted, that would let a market-cap screen be built retrospectively rather than reconstructed from volume alone? This is a data-availability question with a binary answer and no performance calculation attached, and it is the single question that decides whether this entry, or a successor to it, is testable at all under the free-data standard. Two subsidiary questions follow if the answer is yes: does the published dispersion-momentum state-dependence survive on a universe with a market-cap screen that is honest in both directions, and does it add anything over a Bitcoin-realized-volatility state variable? If the answer to the first question is no, the correct outcome is a recorded deferral of the whole cross-sectional-survivorship-aware family rather than a repeated re-specification of it. The same data blocker also binds the second non-derivatives family examined in this cycle - predictable token supply changes - whose published result is strong and whose essential input (historical circulating supply and vesting schedules) is blocked by the same CoinGecko free-tier history restriction, so a single answer to the data question would unblock or retire both families at once. **Ninth-cycle answer to the first question: yes, for the delisting half.** A dated delisting record for tokens a major venue has delisted is constructible on free data, by derivation rather than by a vendor field: the Binance public data archive keeps a dated daily kline file for every USD-M perpetual symbol ever listed, and the terminal file of a ceased contract carries volume=0 and trade count=0 with a frozen close, so the last trading date is exactly the last file with a non-zero trade count. The construction is validated against the venue's own announcements (LUNA 2022-05-13; OMG 2025-01-31 after two postponements) and against one re-listing (CVC), and the two hazards - an incomplete monthly tree and re-listings - are recorded in item 10. The *other* half of the data question is unchanged and still negative: historical circulating supply, historical market capitalisation and market-cap rank remain not confirmed free on any free tier, so the token-dilution family stays deferred and the published market-cap screen must be substituted rather than reproduced. **The next most valuable research question is therefore no longer the data question.** It is: on the now-constructible survivorship-aware perpetual universe, does the momentum spread really differ between high- and low-dispersion states, and does the answer survive the fact that the base spread does not itself survive costs? Concretely, the test must (i) fix the universe rule, the dispersion definition, the momentum specification, the state variable and all thresholds before any data is inspected; (ii) report the *difference of spreads* between the high- and low-dispersion states, not the level of either spread, so that a null base effect does not make the question unanswerable; (iii) report the conditional spread net of costs alongside the gross, given Arefev's finding; (iv) include the two mandatory controls (Bitcoin realized volatility and average pairwise cross-asset correlation) and the liquidity-filter control; and (v) use heavy-tail inference - block bootstrap with the cross-section kept intact within blocks, median-based summaries, the volatility of the signal bucket reported alongside the mean difference, and no Sharpe ratio. If the difference of spreads is null once the base spread is known to be cost-negative, the correct outcome is a rejection of the entry, not another re-specification, because the entry's registered prediction will then have been tested on a free, survivorship-aware universe. **Tenth-cycle replacement of the question.** The data question is closed, and the specification is fixed, so the highest-value question is no longer *whether the entry can be tested* but *which state variable is operative* - because a published peer-reviewed source now treats a market-wide regime as the operative variable for cross-sectional crypto signal reliability while finding the co-movement mechanism unsupported, and a second peer-reviewed source finds crypto momentum itself to arise only in UP-UP state transitions. The binding question is therefore: **on the free, survivorship-aware perpetual universe, does the dispersion state variable explain any of the variance in the momentum spread that a Bitcoin-realized-volatility state and a pairwise-correlation state do not already explain?** Concretely, the validation must estimate the difference of spreads under three competing state variables - dispersion, correlation and Bitcoin realized volatility - on the same universe, the same momentum specification and the same inference, and report them side by side, with dispersion's incremental content reported as the residual. If the dispersion state adds nothing over the correlation state, the entry is not wrong, it is *not the operative variable*, and the correct outcome is a rewrite of the mechanism rather than a rejection. Two subsidiary questions follow and are recorded for the next cycle. (a) **The delisting sign.** The entry is long winners and short losers, and delisted tokens are disproportionately losers, so the short leg has a mechanical advantage that is not an edge; the closest preregistered prior art states that the *sign* of the delisting bias on a short-perp book through exactly these events is unknown, so the entry's headline must be reported both including and excluding delisted tokens and neither may be presented as the answer. (b) **The venue announcement feed.** The tenth cycle could not confirm whether a free, dated, machine-readable venue announcement feed exists, because Binance's public REST API returns HTTP 451 from this research environment ("service unavailable from a restricted location") and its announcement pages are behind a WAF human-verification gate; this is an environment limitation and not a data-availability determination, it leaves the delisting-announcement question open, and it does not change this entry, whose universe is built from the archive rather than from announcements.
**Thirteenth-cycle update: the question has changed from "is the dispersion variable the operative one?" to "what does a free replication of the published construction actually estimate?".** The eleventh cycle established that the dispersion variable has no independent published test while the volatility-regime and average-pairwise-correlation variables do, and that the dependence structure is itself non-stationary. The thirteenth cycle adds three facts that reframe the question. (i) **A third party has already published a replication of the entry's own construction**, in executable form, including the rival state variables (Bitcoin realized volatility over 30 days and average off-diagonal correlation over 60 days) and the dispersion overlay; so the *ordering* of state variables in the published construction is no longer an open question about the literature, and what remains open is whether a **free** replication — with the market-capitalisation screen replaced by listing age and trailing volume — reproduces that ordering, on a *different universe* and, if the size-momentum lead is right, on a *different size composition*. (ii) **The base is now documented in a peer-reviewed source as close to non-existent**, with many cross-sectional momentum portfolios liquidated and those with significant mean returns often earning negative profits, so a free replication that fails to find the conditional effect may be failing because the unconditional spread is not there, not because the conditional switch is not there — and the plan must therefore estimate the unconditional spread on the *same free universe* as the conditional effect, or the comparison is uninterpretable. (iii) **The size-composition confound is a new and specific threat**: if the momentum sign differs by size quintile, then the free screen (age and volume) and the published screen (market capitalisation) select different populations with different signs, and the entry's registered claim may be about a population that a free replication cannot construct.
**The highest-value next question is therefore now a construction question with a specific failure mode:** on a free, dated, survivorship-aware universe, does the *unconditional* cross-sectional momentum spread exist at all, and if it exists, is the dispersion overlay's conditional increment distinguishable from the unconditional spread net of costs — with the size composition of the free universe reported alongside, so that a null result can be attributed to the wrong population rather than to the wrong switch? That question is decidable on free data, it does not require the paid tier, and it is a precondition for the entry's registered claim ever being testable. The lead recorded for the next cycle is to verify the size-momentum source properly (Xu and Wu) and to establish whether the free screen's size composition can be measured at all, since historical market capitalisation is unavailable free and the entry's universe may therefore be non-reconstructible in the dimension that matters most.

**Fourteenth-cycle update: the lead is discharged, and the question it was attached to is now decidable.** The lead — "verify the size-momentum source properly (Xu and Wu)" — **is discharged**: the source is located and dated (SSRN 6628860, 1 January 2026, a working-paper preprint), its numbers are confirmed, and two stronger sources corroborate the same size-dependence of the sign. The second half of the lead — "establish whether the free screen's size composition can be measured at all" — is now partly answered, and the answer is a determination: **historical market capitalisation is not free and dated** (CoinGecko's free tier refuses every historical query with HTTP 401 and its chart endpoint returns HTTP 404; the entry's own eleventh-cycle determination, re-confirmed), so the free screen's size composition **cannot be measured directly**; what can be measured on free data is a **proxy** for it — listing age and trailing quote volume — and the two are related but not identical, because a coin can be old and liquid and still small, or young and thin and large. That leaves three questions, in order of value. (i) **Is there any free, dated, point-in-time source for market capitalisation or circulating supply that the catalogue has not already ruled out?** The thirteenth cycle's answer was no for the obvious candidates, and the fourteenth cycle adds one more to the list: the venue's own contract metadata (the archive's `exchangeInfo` history and the announcement feed) carries no market capitalisation, and the *only* free route to a market-cap series the catalogue has found is a **current-only** one, so a point-in-time size label is likely to remain unobtainable free — which would make the size-matched control a *proxy* control, and a validator would have to say that. (ii) **What is the free screen's size composition, in the proxy sense, relative to the published screen's?** A validator can compute the free screen's age and volume distribution on the free data and compare it to the $1bn-market-cap screen's composition *as of the current date*, and a large divergence is a prediction that the free replication's base spread will differ in sign from the published one — a testable, cheap, and *falsifiable* preliminary that does not require the paid tier and that can be run before the entry's own test. (iii) **Does the entry's switch survive within a size stratum?** If the momentum spread is estimated within the largest available stratum of the free screen — where the base sign is documented to be positive — the entry's registered comparison becomes interpretable, and the price of the restriction is a smaller universe and a different state variable's distribution, which must be reported. These three are the next cycle's most valuable work on this entry, and they are all *constructive* rather than confirmatory, which is the right posture for an entry whose registered claim has never been tested and whose base has been documented three times to be sign-fragile.

**Fifteenth-cycle update: the lead is discharged, and the question it was attached to is now decidable on free data, with a specified limitation.** The lead was "a free, dated, point-in-time size proxy for CRYPTO-EDGE-004 — venue contract metadata and announcement history, free index-provider constituent files, free ETF holdings, free node-index supply series", and it is now **discharged by a direct hit rather than by elimination**: the Coin Metrics v4 community tier supplies a free, dated, daily market-capitalisation panel for 135 assets with history to genesis for the majors, and the entry's recorded "historical market capitalisation is unobtainable free" determination is superseded in place. What replaces it is a **narrower, decidable question**: (i) *How far does a 135-asset, survivor-only, listing-biased, large-cap-skewed panel reproduce the published screen's size composition, and does the entry's registered conditional effect survive inside a size stratum defined on that panel?* (ii) *Does the free screen's size composition, reported against the published market-cap screen's, confirm the cheap falsifiable preliminary the v1.6 specification demands?* (iii) *Is a turnover-based stratum built from the venue's own klines an acceptable declared proxy, or must the stratum be market-cap-based and therefore limited to 135 assets?* The other two leads the thirteenth and fourteenth cycles attached to this entry — the paper that *created* the size-momentum result and the paper that tests whether its sign flips with size composition — are unchanged and remain the more valuable work, because the entry's claim is about the *conditional* effect of dispersion and the base it is conditioned on is sign-fragile.
